\item \underline{\emph{Anomalous scaling}}: There exist constants~$C\geq1$ and~$\eta>0$, depending only on~$d$ and the law of~$\g_0$, such that, for all~$\ell,m\in\N_0$ with~$\ell\leq m$,
\begin{equation*}
	\frac{\ahom_m}{\ahom_\ell}\leq C3^{-\eta(m-\ell)}\,.
\end{equation*}
For a physical side length~$r>0$, write~$\sigma_r\coloneqq\inf\{t\geq0:Z_t-Z_0\notin r\cu_0\}$, as in Theorem~\ref{t.A}. At the discrete scales we write~$\sigma_k\coloneqq\sigma_{3^{-k}}$, with~$k$ denoting a depth. Thus the discrete~$\sigma_0$ and~$\sigma_1$ correspond to physical sides~$1$ and~$3^{-1}$, respectively; the physical-scale subscript~$r=1$ denotes the exit time from the unit cube. For every~$x\in\Rd$ and every integer~$k\geq0$,
\begin{equation}
	\E\left[\int\sigma_k\,dK_x^\omega\right]\leq C3^{-(2+\eta)k}\,.
	\label{e.intro.limit.exit}
\end{equation}
For every~$x\in\Rd$, almost surely in~$\omega$, the law~$K_x^\omega$ is mutually singular with every mixture of Brownian laws with arbitrary covariance matrices, and~$K_x^\omega$-almost every path satisfies
\begin{equation}
	\sup\{\gamma\geq0:|Z_t-Z_0|=O(t^\gamma)\text{ as }t\downarrow0\}
	\leq\frac1{2+\eta}\,.
	\label{e.intro.limit.Holder}
\end{equation}
In dimension two, one can take~$\eta=\tau^2/\log3$.

\item \underline{\emph{Non-Gaussian marginals}}: Almost surely, for every~$x\in\Rd$ and every~$t>0$, the law of~$Z_t$ under~$K_x^\omega$ is absolutely continuous with respect to~$\mu$, and it is not a Gaussian law.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

Theorem~\ref{t.A} follows, with~$\mathbf P_x=K^\omega_x$: for its exit-time bound, apply~\eqref{e.intro.limit.exit} with~$3^{-k-1}<r\leq3^{-k}$.

\section{Uniqueness of the limit form}
\label{sec:mfd-uniqueness}

In this section we prove that the energies~$\int A_N|\nabla u|^2\,dx$ have a unique limit. We show that all subsequential limits of the Dirichlet forms on cubes coincide (Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-c1}). Section~\ref{sec:mfd-process-convergence} pairs this limit with the limit of the reversible measures~$\mu_N$ to construct the limiting diffusion. The argument has four parts.
\begin{enumerate}
\item We bound, uniformly in~$N$, the energy that solutions carry in small balls; this includes solutions of Neumann problems whose datum is approximated by a volume source (Sections~\ref{mfd:sec-environment}--\ref{mfd:sec-local}). These bounds control the change in the energy on a fixed cube when a fine-scale field is resampled. We use this control to prove compactness of the finite-volume energies (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-resampling}).
\item Along subsequences, the Dirichlet Green operators converge, and their limits define Dirichlet forms. These limit forms are local and consistent under restriction to subcubes, and multiplying the coefficient by a positive continuous function multiplies their energy measures by the same function (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}).
\item On a single event that does not depend on the solution, we bound the number of cubes on which the regularity, extension and harmonic-approximation estimates of Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-deterministic} fail along every chain of nested cubes (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}). We integrate this bound against the energy measure of a solution. This selects cubes carrying a fixed fraction of its energy, on which these estimates hold and the solution is close to affine.
\item Any two subsequential limit forms are comparable: the second lies between two deterministic multiples of the first. The optimal comparison constants agree, since otherwise replacing a solution by harmonic functions on the selected cubes would improve one of them (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare}). Both the energy saved by a replacement and the errors it must absorb are proportional to the gap between the optimal comparison constants. To obtain errors of this size, we apply the Efron--Stein bound of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-resampling} to the difference between the minimal energy of the second limit form and a multiple of the minimal energy of the first, rather than to each energy separately (Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-conc}). Hence the two limits are proportional. The factor is one because the coarse-grained matrices of~$e^{-H}A_N$ on large cubes approach the identity uniformly in~$N$, and both limits inherit this normalization (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-scalar}).
\end{enumerate}

Throughout, Assumptions~\ref{a.g1}--\ref{a.g4} hold. Before reducing~$\gmcdelta$, we fix all exponents and tolerances and a finite list of moment orders. Bounds in~$L^p(\P)$ for the orders in this list are uniform in~$N$. When a result below is stated for every finite~$p$, the moment orders needed for that application, including the larger orders used in H\"older's inequality, spatial maxima and resampling, are fixed first; the disorder threshold then depends on this finite list. This convention applies in particular to Lemmas~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error},~\ref{mfd:lem-prefix-limit} and~\ref{mfd:lem-band}, and Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-conc}. It does not assert all moments at one fixed positive disorder.

\subsection{Rescaled coefficients and energies}
\label{mfd:sec-environment}

At fixed~$N$, relabel the fields by replacing~$\g_i$ with~$\g_{i-N}(3^{-N}\,\cdot)$, and let~$\a_N$ be the cutoff coefficient of~\eqref{e.intro.cutoff.coefficients} formed from the relabelled fields. The diffusion with law~$K^\omega_{N,x}$ has Dirichlet form~$\int A_N|\nabla u|^2\,dx$ on~$L^2(\mu_N)$, where~$A_N\coloneqq\ahom_N^{-1}\,d\mu_N/dx$ (see~\eqref{e.intro.rescaled.generator}). In terms of the relabelled fields,
\begin{equation}
	A_N(x)=e^{H(x)}\frac{\a_N(3^Nx)}{\ahom_N}\,,
	\qquad A_N^0(x)=e^{-H(x)}A_N(x)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-normalization}
\end{equation}
If~$H$ is replaced by one of its partial sums, then~$A_N$ becomes a constant multiple of a stationary cutoff coefficient, so the results of Sections~\ref{s.self.similar} and~\ref{s.Holder.regularity} apply.

\begin{lemma}[Moments of the field~$H$]
\label{mfd:lem-infrared}
The field~$H$ and its partial sums~$\sum_{j=1}^{L'}(\g_j-\g_j(0))$ satisfy, on every compact cube~$Q$,
\begin{equation*}
	\bigl\|\|H\|_{C^{1,1}(Q)}\bigr\|_{L^p}\leq C_Q\gmcdelta\sqrt p\,,
	\qquad
	\E e^{\lambda\|H\|_{C^{1,1}(Q)}}\leq2e^{C_Q\lambda^2\gmcdelta^2}
	\quad(p\geq2,\ \lambda\geq0)\,,
\end{equation*}
with the same bounds for the partial sums, uniformly in the number~$L'$ of terms. Moreover, the partial sums converge to~$H$ locally in~$C^{1,1}$ almost surely.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
As in the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.finite.cutoff.coefficient.convergence}, scaling, the mean value theorem and~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity} give~$\|\|\g_j-\g_j(0)\|_{C^{1,1}(Q)}\|_{L^p}\leq C_Q\gmcdelta\sqrt p\,3^{-j}$ for~$j\geq1$. Sum over~$j$ and expand the exponential. Since the sum of these norms has finite mean, the partial sums also converge in~$C^{1,1}$ almost surely.
\end{proof}

On a bounded cube~$Q$, let
\begin{equation}
	E_N^Q(u,v)=\int_QA_N\nabla u\cdot\nabla v\,dx\,,
	\qquad \Gamma_N(u)=A_N|\nabla u|^2\,dx\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-energy}
\end{equation}
The form~$E_N^Q$ has domain~$H^1_0(Q)$, and we use the same integral for functions in~$H^1(Q)$ when prescribing boundary values. Let~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)$ be the minimum of~$E_N^Q(u)$ over~$u\in H^1(Q)$ with~$u-g\in H^1_0(Q)$. Let~$G_N^Q$ be the Dirichlet Green operator, the inverse of~$-\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla)$ on~$L^2(Q,dx)$ with zero Dirichlet boundary values, so that
\begin{equation}
	\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle
	=\sup_{v\in H^1_0(Q)}\left(2\int_Qfv\,dx-E_N^Q(v)\right)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-quadratic-response}
\end{equation}
For the constant flux~$p$, the quantity~$|Q|\,p\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)p$ is the energy~$E_N^Q(v)$ of a solution~$v$ of~$-\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla v)=0$ in~$Q$ with boundary flux~$A_N\nabla v\cdot n=p\cdot n$, and it has the variational formula~\eqref{e.variational.astar}. Since~$d\mu_N/dx=\ahom_N A_N$, the same energy, regarded as a form on~$L^2(\mu_N)$, has generator~$u\mapsto\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla u)/(\ahom_NA_N)$, the generator of the diffusion~\eqref{e.intro.rescaled.generator}; its resolvent is studied in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-speed}.

\subsection{Local estimates and approximation of the Neumann datum}
\label{mfd:sec-local}

We derive the local estimates from the coarse-grained inequalities of Section~\ref{s.coarse.graining}, the moments of Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, and the regularity estimates of Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity}. The constants in these estimates have the required moments, uniformly in~$N$. These estimates also give the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4}, the coercivity bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} and the bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs} on cutoff functions, which are used in Section~\ref{s.tightness}.

\smallskip

At radii above the smallest field scale~$3^{-N}$, we apply Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity} after replacing~$H$ by a partial sum. At smaller radii, elliptic estimates for coefficients with small oscillation complete the bounds. At these small radii we use Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes} for the coefficient and its logarithmic gradient. The moments of these bounds grow with~$N$, and positive powers of~$3^{-N}$ absorb this growth.

\smallskip

\subsubsection{Coercivity and extension}

The coarse-grained bounds of Section~\ref{s.self.similar} also cover scales below the smallest field scale. We first apply them on a rescaled cube. We call a cube~$z+3^{-k}\cu_0$ of a triadic mesh a \textbf{cell}.

\begin{lemma}[Ellipticity moments on a cell]
\label{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity}
Fix~$s\in(0,1]$,~$q\in\{1,2\}$ and a bounded cube~$Q$. For every~$p<\infty$ and all sufficiently small~$\gmcdelta$, uniformly for~$0\leq k\leq N$ and cubes~$z+3^{-k}\cu_0\subset Q$,
\begin{equation}
	\bigl\|\Lambda_{s,q}(z+3^{-k}\cu_0;A_N)+\lambda_{s,q}^{-1}(z+3^{-k}\cu_0;A_N)\bigr\|_{L^p}
	\leq C_{p,s,Q}\exp\bigl(C(p+p^2)\gmcdelta^2k\bigr)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-cell-moment}
\end{equation}
In particular, on a fixed cell~$U$ these moments are bounded uniformly in~$N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Choose a finite moment order~$\xi\geq4p$ large enough for Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound} at exponent~$s/2$, then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that its remaining hypotheses hold. The estimate~\eqref{e.homogenize.at.scale.m.E}, the comparison of the weighted sums at exponents~$s/2$ and~$s$, and~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} give
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_m\bigl\|\Lambda_{s,q}(\cu_0;A_m^0)
	+\lambda_{s,q}^{-1}(\cu_0;A_m^0)\bigr\|_{L^{2p}}<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}

\smallskip

By definition of~$A_N$, the coefficient~$A_N(z+3^{-k}y)$ is the product of three factors: the scalar~$\frac{\ahom_{N-k}}{\ahom_N}\exp\bigl(H(z)+\sum_{j<k}(\g_{-j}(z)-\tau^2)\bigr)$, the ratio of~$\exp\bigl(H+\sum_{j<k}\g_{-j}\bigr)$ at~$z+3^{-k}y$ to its value at~$z$, and a function of~$y$ with the law of~$A_{N-k}^0$. Summing the bounds on the gradients of the fields as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared} bounds the oscillation of~$H+\sum_{j<k}\g_{-j}$ over the cube~$z+3^{-k}\cu_0$ by a sub-Gaussian variable of size~$C_Q\gmcdelta$. The scalar involves the values of~$k$ independent fields. By~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering.ratio}, for every fixed~$v<\infty$ the sum of the scalar and its inverse, multiplied by the exponential of this oscillation, has~$L^v$ norm at most a constant depending on~$v$ and~$Q$ times~$e^{C(v+v^2)\gmcdelta^2k}$. Monotonicity and homogeneity of the coarse-grained ellipticity constants, followed by H\"older's inequality, prove~\eqref{eq:mfd-cell-moment}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Coercivity of the form~$E_N^Q$]
\label{mfd:lem-coercivity}
Let~$Q$ be a fixed cube of side at most one. For every~$N\geq0$ there is a random variable~$K_N$ such that, for every~$v\in H^1_0(Q)$ and every~$w\in H^1(Q)$,
\begin{equation}
	\|v\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^2\leq K_NE_N^Q(v)
	\quad\text{and}\quad
	\|w-(w)_Q\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^2\leq K_NE_N^Q(w)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-1}
\end{equation}
For every~$p<\infty$ there is~$\gmcdelta_0(d,p)>0$ such that~$\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ if~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On~$\cu_0$, take~$s=\nf18$. By the functional inequalities~\eqref{e.besov.grad.poincare},~\eqref{e.nabla.u.detach} and~\eqref{e.CG.Poincare.trace.zero}, followed by~$B^{\nf{7}{8}}_{2,\infty}\subset H^{\nf34}$, we obtain~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} with~$K_N=C\lambda_{1/8,1}^{-1}(\cu_0;A_N)$. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} bounds its~$L^p$ norms uniformly in~$N$. For a fixed smaller cube, rescale the same inequalities and use~\eqref{eq:mfd-cell-moment}. If its side is not triadic, then the remaining dilation lies in~$[1,3]$. Apply the same argument after replacing~$\gmcdelta$ by~$C_d\gmcdelta$, with constants depending on~$Q$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Boundary extension and bounds on grid cubes]
\label{mfd:lem-extension}
Fix~$\beta\in(\nf12,1)$ before choosing~$\gmcdelta$, and set~$\sigma=\frac12(\beta-\nf12)$. The following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item There is~$C(d,\beta)<\infty$ such that, for every cube~$q=z+r\cu_0$ with~$r\leq1$ and every~$g\in C^\beta(\partial q)$,
\begin{equation}
	\Lambda_{N,q}(g)\leq C\,\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)\,r^{d-2}\,\|g(z+r\,\cdot)\|_{C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)/\mathbb R}^2\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-2}
\end{equation}
\item Let~$Q$ be a bounded cube, and fix finitely many grids, each consisting of the cubes~$z+r\,\cu_0$ with~$r=3^{-k}$,~$k\geq0$, and~$z$ in~$z_0+r\mathbb Z^d$ for a point~$z_0$ fixed for each grid; we call their cubes contained in~$Q$ grid cubes. For every~$\eta>0$ and~$p<\infty$ there is~$\gmcdelta_0(d,\beta,\eta,p)>0$ such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there are random variables~$K_N$ with~$\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ such that, for every~$N$ and every grid cube~$q$ of side~$r\geq3^{-N}$,
\begin{equation}
	\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)+\lambda_{\sigma/2,2}^{-1}(q;A_N)\leq K_Nr^{-\eta}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-3}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We prove~\eqref{eq:mfd-2}. The change of variables~$x=z+ry$ multiplies the unnormalized energy by~$r^{d-2}$ and leaves the volume-normalized constant~$\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}$ unchanged, so we may take~$q=\cu_0$. We construct an extension~$h$ of~$g$ directly. First extend~$g$ to a~$\beta$-H\"older function~$\tilde g$ on~$\Rd$, and let~$\tilde g_k$ be its mollification at scale~$3^{-k}$. Let~$\rho(x)\coloneqq\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial\cu_0)$ and use a smooth Whitney partition~$\{\chi_k\}$ with bounded overlap, subordinate to the layers where~$\rho$ is comparable to~$3^{-k}$, satisfying~$|D^i\chi_k|\leq C3^{ik}$ for~$i=1,2$ and~$\sum_k\chi_k=1$. Set~$h\coloneqq\sum_k\chi_k\tilde g_k$. The mollifications~$\tilde g_k$ on overlapping layers differ by at most~$C[g]_{C^\beta}3^{-k\beta}$. Differentiating~$h$ and using~$\sum_kD^i\chi_k=0$ shows that~$h$ is smooth inside the cube, has continuous boundary values~$g$, and satisfies
\begin{equation*}
	|\nabla h(x)|\leq C[g]_{C^\beta}\rho(x)^{\beta-1}\,,
	\qquad |D^2h(x)|\leq C[g]_{C^\beta}\rho(x)^{\beta-2}\,.
\end{equation*}
For the fractional norm of~$\nabla h$, split pairs at a distance comparable to their distance from the boundary. On the closer pairs use the Hessian bound; on the remaining pairs use the gradient bound. Both contributions are bounded by
\begin{equation*}
	C[g]_{C^\beta}^2\int_{\cu_0}\rho(x)^{2\beta-2-2\sigma}\,dx
	\leq C(d,\beta)[g]_{C^\beta}^2\,,
\end{equation*}
since~$2\beta-2-2\sigma>-1$. The same gradient bound gives its~$L^2$ norm. Thus~$h\in H^{1+\sigma}(\cu_0)$ and~$\|\nabla h\|_{H^\sigma}\leq C(d,\beta)[g]_{C^\beta}$. Applying~\eqref{e.cg.RHS} with zero right side,~$s=\sigma$ and boundary datum~$h$, we find that the~$A_N$-harmonic function~$v$ with~$v=h$ on~$\partial \cu_0$ satisfies
\begin{equation*}
	\Lambda_{N,\cu_0}(g)=\|A_N^{\nf12}\nabla v\|_{L^2}^2
	\leq C\sigma^{-3}\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(\cu_0;A_N)
	\|\nabla h\|_{H^\sigma}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
Subtracting a constant from~$g$ does not change~$\Lambda_{N,\cu_0}(g)$ or its H\"older seminorm. The extension just constructed therefore gives~$\|\nabla h\|_{H^\sigma}\leq C\|g\|_{C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)/\mathbb R}$. Since~$\sigma$ depends only on~$\beta$, this proves~\eqref{eq:mfd-2}.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We prove~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}. Since~$L^p$ norms increase with~$p$, we may assume that~$p>1$ and~$p\eta>d$. Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} with exponents~$\sigma/2$ and~$2$. There are at most~$C3^{dk}$ grid cubes at depth~$k$, so taking the maximum over them introduces a factor~$C3^{\nf{dk}{p}}$ in~$L^p$. Reduce~$\gmcdelta$ so that~$C(p+p^2)\gmcdelta^2<\frac12(\eta-d/p)\log3$. For~$K_N$ the supremum, over~$k\leq N$ and grid cubes~$q$ of depth~$k$, of~$3^{-\eta k}\bigl(\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)+\lambda^{-1}_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)\bigr)$, Minkowski's inequality gives
\begin{equation*}
	\|K_N\|_{L^p}
	\leq C_p\sum_{k\geq0}3^{-(\eta-\frac{d}{p})k}
	e^{C(p+p^2)\gmcdelta^2k}\leq C_p\,.
\end{equation*}
The right side does not depend on~$N$, so~$\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$. By the definition of~$K_N$, every grid cube~$q$ of side~$r=3^{-k}\geq3^{-N}$ satisfies~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Morrey bounds for the Dirichlet problem}

To apply the regularity estimates with a bounded volume source, we first write that source as a divergence.

\begin{lemma}[The gradient of the Newtonian potential]
\label{mfd:lem-primitive}
Let~$f\in L^\infty$ be supported in a cube of side~$R$, and let~$g$ be the gradient of the Newtonian potential of~$f$, that is, the convolution of~$f$ with the gradient of the fundamental solution of the Laplacian. Then~$\nabla\cdot g=f$ and, for all~$x$ and~$y$ with~$|x-y|\leq R$,
\begin{equation}
	|g(x)-g(y)|\leq C\|f\|_\infty|x-y|\bigl(1+\log(R/|x-y|)\bigr)\leq CR^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty|x-y|^{\nf12}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-primitive}
\end{equation}
In particular,~$[g]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}}\leq CR^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty$ on that cube.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The kernel of the convolution and its gradient are bounded by~$C|z|^{1-d}$ and~$C|z|^{-d}$. If~$x$ is farther than~$2\sqrt dR$ from the supporting cube, the derivative bound gives~$|g(x)-g(y)|\leq C\|f\|_\infty|x-y|$ directly. Otherwise the support lies in~$B_{CR}(x)$. We split the convolution according to whether the integration variable~$z$ satisfies~$|z-x|\leq2|x-y|$. The part with~$|z-x|\leq2|x-y|$ is bounded by~$C\|f\|_\infty|x-y|$, both at~$x$ and at~$y$. For~$|z-x|>2|x-y|$, the mean value theorem bounds the difference of the kernel at~$x-z$ and at~$y-z$ by~$C|x-y||x-z|^{-d}$. The integral over~$2|x-y|<|z-x|<CR$ is at most~$C|x-y|\|f\|_\infty\log(CR/|x-y|)$. The second inequality in~\eqref{eq:mfd-primitive} follows from~$(|x-y|/R)^{\nf12}(1+\log(R/|x-y|))\leq C$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pointwise extremes of the coefficient]
\label{mfd:lem-extremes}
Let~$Q$ be a fixed cube, and set~$D_N=3^{-N}\|\nabla\log A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$. For every~$p$ there are~$C_p<\infty$ depending on~$p$ and~$Q$,~$C_d<\infty$ and~$c_d>0$ such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq c_d/p$, then
\begin{equation*}
	\|D_N\|_{L^p}\leq C_p(1+N)^{\nf12}\,,
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation}
	\bigl\|\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}+\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\bigr\|_{L^p}
	\leq C_pe^{(C_d\gmcdelta+C_p\gmcdelta^2)N}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-6}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

The linear term~$C_d\gmcdelta N$ in the exponent arises from taking the maximum over the cubes of side~$3^{-N}$ in~$Q$.

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We bound~$D_N$. We have~$\nabla\log A_N=\nabla H+\sum_{j=0}^N\nabla\g_{-j}$ and, in law,~$\|\nabla\g_{-j}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$ is at most~$3^j$ times the maximum, over at most~$C3^{dj}$ cells, of the regularity norms on unit cells from~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}. The maximum of~$n$ sub-Gaussian variables with parameter~$\gmcdelta$ has~$L^p$ norm at most~$C\gmcdelta(\sqrt{\log n}+\sqrt p)$. Therefore
\begin{equation*}
	\|D_N\|_{L^p}\leq3^{-N}\bigl\|\|\nabla H\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\bigr\|_{L^p}+\sum_{j\leq N}3^{j-N}C_p\gmcdelta\sqrt{1+j}\leq C_p\sqrt{1+N}\,.
\end{equation*}
This is the first estimate of the lemma.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We bound~$\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$ and~$\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$. The deterministic factor~$\ahom_N^{-1}e^{-(N+1)\tau^2}$ of~$A_N$ satisfies~$1\leq e^{(N+1)\tau^2}\ahom_N\leq e^{C\gmcdelta^2(N+1)}$ by~\eqref{e.ord.bounds.truncated}, Proposition~\ref{p.homogenized.coefficient.reciprocal.lower} and~\eqref{e.tau.bound}, and~$e^{\|H\|_\infty}$ has moments of every order by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}. By H\"older's inequality, it therefore suffices to bound the moments of~$\exp(\sup_Q|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|)$. Cover~$Q$ by at most~$C3^{dN}$ cubes~$Q_i$ of side~$3^{-N}$ with centers~$x_i$. On each of them,~$\sup_{Q_i}|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|\leq|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}(x_i)|+\sum_{j\leq N}\operatorname{osc}_{Q_i}\g_{-j}$, where the first term is sub-Gaussian with parameter~$C\gmcdelta\sqrt{N+1}$ and the second is sub-Gaussian with parameter~$C\gmcdelta$. H\"older's inequality gives~$\E\exp(q\sup_{Q_i}|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|)\leq Ce^{Cq^2\gmcdelta^2(N+1)}$. For~$q\geq p$, the inequality~$\exp(p\sup_Q|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|)\leq(\sum_i\exp(q\sup_{Q_i}|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|))^{\nf pq}$ and Jensen's inequality bound~$\E\exp(p\sup_Q|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|)$ by~$(C3^{dN}e^{Cq^2\gmcdelta^2(N+1)})^{\nf pq}$. We choose~$q=\gmcdelta^{-1}\sqrt{d\log3/C}$, which is admissible when~$\gmcdelta\leq c_d/p$; the exponent is then at most~$C_dp\gmcdelta(N+1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Morrey bounds at all radii]
\label{mfd:prop-growth}
Let~$t\in(d-1,d)$ and~$\alpha\in(0,1)$, fix a finite list of moment orders, and let~$Q$ be a fixed cube of side at most one. There is~$\gmcdelta_0(d,t,\alpha,\text{list})>0$ such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there are random variables~$K_N$ with~$\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ for every order~$p$ in the list, such that, almost surely, the following estimates hold for every~$f\in L^\infty(Q)$ and~$\varphi\in C^2(\overline Q)$. Here~$u_N\in H^1(Q)$ solves~$-\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla u_N)=f$ in~$Q$ with~$u_N=\varphi$ on~$\partial Q$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \underline{\emph{Morrey bound}}: for every~$x\in Q$ and~$r\in(0,1]$,
\begin{equation}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)\leq K_N\bigl(\|f\|_\infty+\|\varphi\|_{C^2}\bigr)^2r^t\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-4}
\end{equation}
\item \underline{\emph{H\"older bound}}:~$\|u_N\|_{C^\alpha(\overline Q)}\leq K_N(\|f\|_\infty+\|\varphi\|_{C^2})$ and, if~$\varphi=0$,
\begin{equation}
	\|G_N^Qf\|_{C^\alpha(\overline Q)}\leq K_N\|f\|_\infty\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-5}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proof proceeds in seven steps, and the quantity we track is the energy~$\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)$ of small balls. Step~1 replaces~$H$ by a partial sum of its series, so that the coefficient becomes a multiple of a cutoff coefficient. Steps~2 and~3 prove the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} at radii~$r\geq3^{-N}$ from the regularity estimates for cutoff coefficients, and Steps~4 and~5 prove it at radii~$r\leq3^{-N}$, away from~$\partial Q$ and near~$\partial Q$, from estimates for coefficients with small oscillation. The main difficulty lies at these small radii: there the moments of the coefficient bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes} grow with~$N$, and positive powers of~$3^{-N}$ absorb this growth. Step~6 removes the truncation, and Step~7 proves the H\"older bounds.

\smallskip

Rescale so that~$Q=y_0+\cu_0$. In the coordinates~$y=3^Nx$ of the relabelled fields, the domain is~$3^Ny_0+\cu_N$, a deterministic translate of~$\cu_N$. Throughout the proof,~$K_N$ denotes a random variable with~$\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ for every order~$p$ in the list, which may change from line to line. Choose the exponents as follows. Take~$\alpha_M\in(\nf12,1)$ with~$\alpha_M>\max\{\alpha,(t+2-d)/2\}$, and set~$t_0=d-2+2\alpha_M>t$. Choose~$t_1$ with~$\max\{t,d-2+2\alpha\}<t_1<t_0$, and require~$\gmcdelta$ to be so small that~$\alpha_M\leq1-C\gmcdelta|\log\gmcdelta|^{\nf12}$. We also take~$\gmcdelta$ so small that the tail condition~\eqref{eq:mfd-tail-rate} of Step~3 holds for every order in the list and for the finitely many larger orders used in H\"older's inequality, and that the moment absorptions in Steps~4,~5 and~7 hold; once the exponents and the orders are fixed, each of these is a condition on~$\gmcdelta$ alone.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We replace~$H$ by a partial sum~$\sum_{j=1}^{L'}(\g_j-\g_j(0))$ of its series, which makes the coefficient a constant multiple of a cutoff coefficient. In Steps~1--5 we keep the notation~$H$,~$A_N$,~$u_N$ and~$\Gamma_N$ for the truncated objects; the truncation is removed in Step~6. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization} and~\eqref{e.intro.cutoff.coefficients},
\begin{equation*}
	A_N(3^{-N}y)=\ahom_N^{-1}e^{L'\tau^2}\exp\Bigl(-\sum_{i=N+1}^{N+L'}\g_i(0)\Bigr)\a_{N+L'}(y)\,,
\end{equation*}
so~$A_N(3^{-N}\cdot)$ is a random scalar multiple of the cutoff coefficient~$\a_{N+L'}$. In the coordinates~$y$, the rescaled function~$u_N(3^{-N}\cdot)$ solves~$-\nabla\cdot(\a_{N+L'}\nabla u_N(3^{-N}\cdot))=\nabla\cdot g$, where~$g$ is the vector field constructed in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-primitive} for the source~$f(3^{-N}\cdot)$, divided by~$3^{2N}$ and by this random scalar and extended by zero. Moreover,~$u_N(3^{-N}\cdot)=\varphi(3^{-N}\cdot)\in C^{1,\nf12}$ on the boundary. Since~$N+L'\geq N$, the estimate~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate} applies with~$m=N$; we write~$\mathcal L=\mathcal L_{N+L'}(\alpha_M,N)$ for the minimal scale in that estimate.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We prove the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} at the radii~$3^{-N}\leq r\leq3^{-\mathcal L}$. For~$x\in Q$ and~$0\leq n\leq N-\mathcal L$, we have~$B_{3^{n-N}/2}(x)\cap Q\subset3^{-N}\bigl((3^Nx+\cu_n)\cap(3^Ny_0+\cu_N)\bigr)$. Substituting the definitions of the random scalar and of~$\b_{N+L',N}$, the constants~$L'\tau^2$ cancel, and the random scalar times the average~$(\b_{N+L',N})_{3^Ny_0+\cu_N}$ equals~$\fint_Qe^H\,dx$. We square~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate}, multiply it by the volume, which is at most~$3^{dn}$, and return to the~$x$-coordinates. Using Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-primitive} with~$R=3^N$ and bounding~$\fint_Qe^H\,dx$ and its inverse by~$e^{\|H\|_{L^\infty(Q)}}$, we obtain, for~$r=3^{n-N}/2$,
\begin{equation}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)\leq Cr^{t_0}\Bigl(\Gamma_N(u_N)(Q)
	+e^{\|H\|_{L^\infty(Q)}}\bigl(\|f\|_\infty^2+\|\varphi\|_{C^2}^2\bigr)\Bigr)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-macro}
\end{equation}

\smallskip

For windows meeting~$\partial Q$, the energy estimate used here is the boundary version obtained in Step~6 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}, from Lemma~\ref{l.centered.energy.good.scale} at its nearest good anchor, together with its cutoff extension in Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity}. The minimal scale~$\mathcal L$ constructed in Propositions~\ref{p.Holder.regularity} and~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity} is independent of the window center. Thus the same calculation gives~\eqref{eq:mfd-macro} near the boundary, with the single scalar cancellation displayed above.

\smallskip

The factor~$e^{\|H\|_{L^\infty(Q)}}$ has moments bounded uniformly in~$N$ and~$L'$ by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}. The random variable~$\mathcal L$ does not depend on~$x$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We treat the remaining large radii~$3^{-\mathcal L}<r\leq1$ and bound the total energy. For these radii,~$\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r\cap Q)\leq\Gamma_N(u_N)(Q)\leq3^{t_1\mathcal L}r^{t_1}\Gamma_N(u_N)(Q)$. Together with~\eqref{eq:mfd-macro}, this gives a bound~$K_Nr^{t_1}$ at every radius~$r\geq3^{-N}$, also when~$\mathcal L>N$. For an order~$p$ in the list and for the larger orders used in H\"older's inequality,~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.minimal.scale} gives~$\E3^{pt_1\mathcal L}<\infty$ as soon as
\begin{equation}
	\frac{(1-\alpha_M)^2}{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}>pt_1\log3\,,
	\label{eq:mfd-tail-rate}
\end{equation}
which is a condition on~$\gmcdelta$ alone once~$\alpha_M$,~$p$ and~$t_1$ are fixed. The global energy has moments: by~\eqref{eq:mfd-quadratic-response},~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} and~\eqref{eq:mfd-2},
\begin{equation*}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(Q)\leq2\Lambda_{N,Q}(\varphi)+2\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle\leq2\Lambda_{N,Q}(\varphi)+2K_N\|f\|_{L^2}^2\,,
\end{equation*}
uniformly in the truncation, since the truncated coefficient satisfies the same estimates.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We prove the Morrey bound for~$r\leq3^{-N}$ on balls away from~$\partial Q$. Let~$p_1<\infty$ satisfy~$d-2d/p_1>t_1$ and~$1-d/p_1>\alpha$, and let~$\ell_N$ be~$3^{-N}(1+D_N)^{-1}$ times a small constant depending on~$d$ and~$p_1$, chosen below. For~$x_0\in Q$ with~$B_{2\ell_N}(x_0)\subset Q$, the oscillation of~$\log A_N$ on~$B_{2\ell_N}(x_0)$ is at most~$2\ell_ND_N/3^{-N}$, hence at most twice the small constant. Dividing the equation by~$A_N(x_0)$ gives an equation with coefficient~$A_N/A_N(x_0)$, whose logarithm is bounded by twice the small constant on the ball, and source~$f/A_N(x_0)$. By~\citet[Theorem~2, equation~(48)]{Meyers1963}, the admissible gradient exponent tends to infinity as the ellipticity ratio tends to one. Choose the small constant so that the admissible exponent exceeds~$p_1$, subtract the mean of~$u_N$, and apply that estimate with initial gradient exponent~$2$ and a finite source exponent greater than~$d$. After scaling to the unit ball, this gives
\begin{equation*}
	\Bigl(\fint_{B_{\ell_N}(x_0)}|\nabla u_N|^{p_1}\Bigr)^{\nf1{p_1}}\leq C\Bigl(\fint_{B_{2\ell_N}(x_0)}|\nabla u_N|^2\Bigr)^{\nf12}+C\ell_NA_N(x_0)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
H\"older's inequality on~$B_r(x_0)$,~$r\leq\ell_N$, and the fact that~$A_N$ is within a fixed factor of~$A_N(x_0)$ on~$B_{2\ell_N}(x_0)$ yield, for every~$r\leq\ell_N$,
\begin{equation}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r)\leq C\Bigl(\frac r{\ell_N}\Bigr)^{d-2d/p_1}\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{2\ell_N})
	+C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|_\infty^2\ell_N^{2+\nf{2d}{p_1}}r^{d-2d/p_1}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-micro}
\end{equation}
We divide by~$r^t$. Since~$d-2d/p_1>t_1>t$, the right side is largest at~$r=\ell_N$, so
\begin{equation*}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r)r^{-t}\leq C(1+D_N)^t3^{tN}\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}})+C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|_\infty^23^{-(d+2-t)N}\,.
\end{equation*}
For~$\ell_N\leq r\leq3^{-N}$, we use~$\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r)\leq\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}})\leq(3^{-N}/\ell_N)^tr^t3^{tN}\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}})$. Together with Step~3 at~$r=C3^{-N}$, this gives, for every~$r\leq3^{-N}$,
\begin{equation*}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r)\leq C(1+D_N)^t3^{-(t_1-t)N}K_Nr^t
	+C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|^2_\infty3^{-(d+2-t)N}r^t\,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{eq:mfd-6}, the positive powers~$3^{-(t_1-t)N}$ and~$3^{-(d+2-t)N}$ absorb the polynomially growing moments of~$D_N$ and the moments of~$\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$, once~$\gmcdelta$ is small enough for the finitely many larger moment orders used in H\"older's inequality.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 5.} We prove the Morrey bound for~$r\leq3^{-N}$ near the faces of~$Q$, where the Dirichlet condition holds. Let~$x_0$ be within distance~$2\ell_N$ of a face, say~$\{x_d=0\}$. The function~$u_N-\varphi$ vanishes on the face and solves~$-\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla(u_N-\varphi))=f+\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla\varphi)$, where~$\|\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla\varphi)\|_\infty\leq CA_N(x_0)(\|D^2\varphi\|_\infty+\ell_N^{-1}\|\nabla\varphi\|_\infty)$ on the ball around~$x_0$ on which~$\log A_N$ has small oscillation, as in Step~4. We reflect~$u_N-\varphi$ oddly,~$A_N$ evenly and the source oddly across the face; the reflected function solves the reflected equation, with no additional source concentrated on the face. Indeed, for a compactly supported test function on the doubled ball, the difference of the test function and its reflection, restricted to~$Q$, lies in~$H^1$, has zero trace on the face, and vanishes near the other faces, so it is an admissible test function for the original equation. The map that folds the reflected domain back onto~$Q$ is~$1$-Lipschitz, so the oscillation of the logarithm of the reflected coefficient on a ball~$B$ centered on the face is at most that of~$\log A_N$ on~$B\cap\overline Q$. Hence~\eqref{eq:mfd-micro} applies to the reflected function and coefficient, since the proof of~\eqref{eq:mfd-micro} uses only the boundedness and the small oscillation of the coefficient, not its differentiability across the face. Since the energy measure of the reflected function on a symmetric set~$B$ is twice the energy measure of~$u_N-\varphi$ on~$B\cap Q$, and
\begin{equation*}
	\Gamma_N(u_N-\varphi)(B_{C3^{-N}}\cap Q)
	\leq2\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}}\cap Q)
	+C\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|\nabla\varphi\|_\infty^23^{-dN}\,,
\end{equation*}
we obtain
\begin{align*}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r\cap Q)
	&\leq C(r/\ell_N)^{d-2d/p_1}\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}}\cap Q)
	+C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|_\infty^2\ell_N^{2+\nf{2d}{p_1}}r^{d-2d/p_1}\notag\\
	&\qquad+C\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|\varphi\|_{C^2}^2(1+D_N)^d\ell_N^{\nf{2d}{p_1}}r^{d-2d/p_1}\,.
\end{align*}
Dividing the last term by~$r^t$ and maximizing at~$r=\ell_N$ gives~$C\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|\varphi\|_{C^2}^23^{-(d-t)N}(1+D_N)^t$. The positive power~$3^{-(d-t)N}$ absorbs its moments as in Step~4. Near an edge or a corner of~$Q$, we reflect across each face meeting the neighborhood of~$x_0$. On a set invariant under these reflections, the energy of the reflected solution is two to the power of the number of these faces times the energy of the solution in the part of the set inside~$Q$. Divide by this factor to obtain the estimates on the original cube.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 6.} We remove the truncation. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}, the truncated coefficients converge uniformly on~$\overline Q$ to the untruncated~$A_N$ almost surely. For fixed~$N$, they are therefore bounded away from zero once the truncation is sufficiently large. Testing the difference of the two weak equations with the difference of the solutions, which lies in~$H^1_0(Q)$, bounds its energy by the uniform distance between the coefficients times the product of the~$L^2$ norms of the gradient of the untruncated solution and of the gradient of the difference. The lower bound on the coefficients then shows that the gradients converge in~$L^2(Q)$. Consequently, for every~$x$ and~$r$, the truncated energies converge to~$\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r\cap Q)$. By Steps~2--5, these energies satisfy~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} with random constants whose~$L^p$ norms are bounded uniformly in~$N$ and in the truncation, for every order~$p$ in the list. Their limit inferior gives a constant for the untruncated estimate, and Fatou's lemma preserves these moment bounds.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 7.} We prove the H\"older bounds. At radii above~$3^{-N}$,~\eqref{e.Holder.estimate.boxes.local} is a Campanato bound at every~$x\in Q$, with exponent~$\alpha_M>\alpha$ and the same source norm; the large radii~$3^{-\mathcal L}<r\leq1$ are handled as in Step~3, and the~$L^2$ oscillation on~$Q$ is bounded by the energy through~\eqref{eq:mfd-1}. At radii below~$3^{-N}$, the~$W^{1,p_1}$ bound of Step~4 and Morrey's inequality bound the oscillation on~$B_{\ell_N}$. After division by~$r^\alpha$, the energy on the ball of radius~$C3^{-N}$, the volume source and the boundary datum contribute, respectively,
\begin{align*}
	&CK_N^{\nf12}\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}^{\nf12}
	3^{-((t_1-d+2)/2-\alpha)N}(1+D_N)^C\,,\\
	&C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|_\infty
	3^{-(2-\alpha)N}(1+D_N)^C\,,\\
	&C\|\varphi\|_{C^2}
	3^{-(1-\alpha)N}(1+D_N)^C\,.
\end{align*}
All three powers of~$3^{-N}$ are positive by the choice of the parameters. Chaining balls of radius~$\ell_N$ inside a cube of side~$3^{-N}$ introduces another fixed power of~$1+D_N$. Above the smallest field scale, telescoping the cube averages produces the geometric sum~$\sum_{s\leq R}s^{\alpha_M}\leq CR^{\alpha_M}$. With the same absorption of moments as in Step~4, this proves the H\"older bounds. Approximate a general bounded source by smooth sources. Remove the truncation as in Step~6: the equicontinuity just proved and the convergence in~$L^2$ give uniform convergence of the truncated solutions.
\end{proof}

\smallskip

\begin{remark}[Morrey and H\"older bounds on larger cubes]
\label{mfd:rem-large-cube-growth}
For the coefficient~$A_N$, both conclusions of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} hold on every fixed cube~$Q$, with moment constants depending on~$Q$. The disorder threshold depends only on~$d$,~$t$,~$\alpha$ and the prescribed moment orders. To see this, choose~$j\geq1$ so that the side~$R$ of~$Q$ satisfies~$R\leq3^j$, and set~$\lambda=3^j$. The scale shift
\begin{equation*}
	(S_j\omega)_\ell(y)=\g_{\ell+j}^\omega(3^jy)
\end{equation*}
preserves the law of the fields. Splitting the anchored infrared sum at~$j$ gives
\begin{equation}
	A_N^\omega(\lambda y)=c_N A_{N+j}^{S_j\omega}(y)\,,
	\qquad
	c_N=\frac{\ahom_{N+j}}{\ahom_N}
	\exp\Bigl(j\tau^2-\sum_{i=1}^j\g_i^\omega(0)\Bigr)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-large-cube-transfer}
\end{equation}
By~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering}, both~$c_N$ and~$c_N^{-1}$ are at most~$\exp(j\tau^2+|\sum_{i=1}^j\g_i(0)|)$, uniformly in~$N$. This envelope has every finite moment by~\ref{a.g2}.

\smallskip

If~$u_N$ solves the Dirichlet problem on~$Q$, then~$v(y)=u_N(\lambda y)$ solves it on~$\lambda^{-1}Q$, with coefficient~$A_{N+j}^{S_j\omega}$, source~$\lambda^2c_N^{-1}f(\lambda\,\cdot)$ and boundary datum~$\varphi(\lambda\,\cdot)$. The weak equation follows by changing variables in its test integrals, and the energy satisfies
\begin{equation*}
	\Gamma_N^\omega(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)
	=c_N\lambda^{d-2}
	\Gamma_{N+j}^{S_j\omega}(v)
	(B_{r/\lambda}(x/\lambda)\cap\lambda^{-1}Q)\,.
\end{equation*}
Apply Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} on the cube~$\lambda^{-1}Q$ of side at most one. Since~$t>d-2$ and~$\lambda\geq1$, the radius factor~$\lambda^{d-2-t}$ is at most one. Also~$\|\varphi(\lambda\,\cdot)\|_{C^2}\leq(1+\lambda+\lambda^2)\|\varphi\|_{C^2}$, and~$c_N\max\{1,c_N^{-1}\}^2=\max\{c_N,c_N^{-1}\}$. The energy and H\"older bounds are consequently transferred by multiplying the smaller-cube majorant at cutoff~$N+j$ by~$(1+\lambda+\lambda^2)^2\exp(j\tau^2+|\sum_{i=1}^j\g_i(0)|)$. For each requested order~$p$, use the smaller-cube proposition at order~$2p$ before choosing the disorder threshold. H\"older's inequality and preservation of the sample law then bound the product in~$L^p$, uniformly in~$N$. This proves both the Morrey and H\"older estimates on~$Q$ for~$A_N$; their moment constants may depend on the fixed position of~$Q$.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Approximating the Neumann datum by a volume source}

We approximate the functional~$v\mapsto\int_Qp\cdot\nabla v$, which encodes the Neumann datum~$p\cdot n$, by a bounded volume source near the faces.

\smallskip

Let~$Q=(0,1)^d$, let~$p\in\Rd$ with~$|p|=1$, and let
\begin{equation*}
	L_p(v)=\int_Qp\cdot\nabla v\,dx=\int_{\partial Q}(p\cdot n)\operatorname{Tr}v\,d\mathcal H^{d-1}\,,
\end{equation*}
where~$n$ is the outer normal. For~$0<\epsilon<\nf18$, let~$f_\epsilon$ be the sum over~$i=1,\dots,d$ of~$p_i$ times the difference of two smooth bumps of integral one, the first supported in the layer~$\{\epsilon<1-x_i<2\epsilon\}$ near the face~$\{x_i=1\}$ and the second in the layer~$\{\epsilon<x_i<2\epsilon\}$ near the face~$\{x_i=0\}$, both obtained from one fixed profile by dilation by~$\epsilon$, and let
\begin{equation}
	L_{p,\epsilon}(v)=\int_Qf_\epsilon v\,dx\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-bump}
\end{equation}
Each summand of~$f_\epsilon$ has integral zero,~$f_\epsilon\in C^\infty(\overline Q)$,~$\|f_\epsilon\|_\infty\leq C\epsilon^{-1}$, and~$L_{p,\epsilon}(1)=0$.

\begin{lemma}[Error of the volume-source approximation of the Neumann datum]
\label{mfd:lem-neumann-error}
Let~$v_N,v_{N,\epsilon}$ be the mean-zero Riesz representers of~$L_p,L_{p,\epsilon}$ with respect to~$E_N^Q$, so that~$E_N^Q(v_N)=p\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)p$. For every~$v\in H^1(Q)$,
\begin{equation}
	|L_p(v)-L_{p,\epsilon}(v)|
	\leq C\epsilon^{\nf14}\|v-(v)_Q\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-7}
\end{equation}
With~$K_N$ from~\eqref{eq:mfd-1}, this gives
\begin{equation}
	E_N^Q(v_N-v_{N,\epsilon})\leq CK_N\epsilon^{\nf12}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-8}
\end{equation}
For every moment order~$q$ in the list, if~$\gmcdelta$ is small enough,
\begin{equation}
	\|E_N^Q(v_N)-E_N^Q(v_{N,\epsilon})\|_{L^q}\leq C_q\epsilon^{\nf14}\,,
	\qquad \sup_{N,\epsilon}\|E_N^Q(v_{N,\epsilon})\|_{L^q}<\infty\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-9}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Subtract~$(v)_Q$ and extend to~$H^{\nf34}(\Rd)$. Fourier transformation in the normal variable and Cauchy--Schwarz give, on each face,
\begin{equation*}
	\|v(\tau,\cdot)-v(0,\cdot)\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^{d-1})}^2
	\leq\|v\|_{H^{\nf34}(\Rd)}^2
	\int_{\mathbb R}\frac{\min\{4,\tau^2\xi^2\}}
	{(1+\xi^2)^{\nf34}}\,d\xi
	\leq C\tau^{\nf12}\|v\|_{H^{\nf34}(\Rd)}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
Integrate against the bump profile and sum over the faces to get~\eqref{eq:mfd-7}. Test the difference of the two weak equations with~$v_N-v_{N,\epsilon}$ and use coercivity to obtain~\eqref{eq:mfd-8}. The reverse triangle inequality for the energy norm then gives
\begin{equation*}
	|E_N^Q(v_N)-E_N^Q(v_{N,\epsilon})|
	\leq CK_N^{\nf12}\epsilon^{\nf14}E_N^Q(v_N)^{\nf12}
	+CK_N\epsilon^{\nf12}\,.
\end{equation*}
The response~$E_N^Q(v_N)=p\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)p$ has the required moments uniformly in~$N$ by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity}. H\"older's inequality at the doubled orders proves~\eqref{eq:mfd-9}.

\smallskip

We also record the transfer used on a fixed physical cube~$Q=w+R(0,1)^d$. For~$p\ne0$, set~$\widehat p=p/|p|$ and let~$f_h^{\widehat p}$ be the preceding unit-cube source. Define
\begin{equation*}
	f_{Q,h}(w+Ry)=\frac{|p|}{R}f_h^{\widehat p}(y),\qquad
	L_{Q,p}(u)=\int_Qp\cdot\nabla u,\qquad
	L_{Q,p,h}(u)=\int_Qf_{Q,h}u\,.
\end{equation*}
For~$p=0$ take both loads and the source to be zero. With~$\widetilde u(y)=u(w+Ry)$, change of variables gives
\begin{equation*}
	L_{Q,p}(u)=|p|R^{d-1}L_{\widehat p}(\widetilde u),\qquad
	L_{Q,p,h}(u)=|p|R^{d-1}L_{\widehat p,h}(\widetilde u)\,.
\end{equation*}
In particular,~$\int_Qf_{Q,h}=0$,~$\|f_{Q,h}\|_\infty\leq C_{Q,p}h^{-1}$, and the fixed dilation of the fractional norm in~\eqref{eq:mfd-7} gives
\begin{equation*}
	|L_{Q,p}(u)-L_{Q,p,h}(u)|
	\leq C_{Q,p}h^{\nf14}\|u-(u)_Q\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}\,.
\end{equation*}
Thus the source factor in physical coordinates is~$R^{-1}$.

\smallskip

The coercivity bound needed here also transfers to fixed larger cubes. For~$R\leq1$ use Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-coercivity}. For~$R>1$, choose~$k\geq1$ with~$\rho=R/3^k\in(1/3,1]$ and use the chart~$x=z+3^ky$ from the centered residual cube of side~$\rho$, where~$z$ is the center of~$Q$. The environment
\begin{equation*}
	(\Psi\omega)_j(y)=\omega_{j+k}(z+3^ky)
\end{equation*}
has the original law. The coefficient at cutoff~$N+k$ in this environment is bounded above by~$F_Q(\omega)A_N(z+3^ky)$, and the same bound holds with~$A_N^0$ on the right, where we may take
\begin{equation*}
	F_Q=\exp\left(k\tau^2+\sum_{i=1}^k\|\omega_i\|_{C(\overline Q)}
	+\|H(\Psi\omega)\|_{C(\overline Q_\rho)}
	+\|H(\omega)\|_{C(\overline Q)}\right)\,.
\end{equation*}
Here~$Q_\rho$ is that residual cube. The normalization ratio is bounded by~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering}, namely~$\ahom_N\leq e^{2k\tau^2}\ahom_{N+k}$; the other factors are precisely the retained coarse layers and the two infrared restrictions. Each has every fixed exponential moment on its fixed compact set. Pull back the mean-zero function, and likewise a function with zero trace (the~$H^1_0$ clause of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-coercivity}); use that lemma on~$Q_\rho$ and scale the energy by~$3^{k(d-2)}$. The fixed scaling of the fractional norm gives a physical coercivity constant
\begin{equation*}
	K_{Q,N}\leq C_QF_Q\,K_{Q_\rho,N+k}(\Psi\omega)\,.
\end{equation*}
The constants in the moment bounds may depend on~$Q$; the disorder threshold is the one for the finite doubled moment orders on the residual cube. H\"older's inequality and preservation of the environment law give the required uniform-in-~$N$ moments, for both infrared conventions. For a triadic~$R$,~$\rho=1$. Only a first-moment residual bound is needed for a fixed polynomial tail: the union bound at~$\sqrt T$ gives~$\P[K_{Q,N}>T]\leq B_QT^{-1/2}$ after absorbing~$C_Q$ into~$F_Q$. This uses no independence between~$F_Q$ and the residual coercivity constant.

\smallskip

Let~$Y_N$ and~$Y_{N,h}$ be the variational responses to these two physical loads. Their Riesz representers and the preceding load estimate give
\begin{equation*}
	|Y_N-Y_{N,h}|
	\leq 2C_{Q,p}h^{\nf14}K_{Q,N}^{\nf12}Y_N^{\nf12}
	+C_{Q,p}^2h^{\nf12}K_{Q,N}\,.
\end{equation*}
The trace bound~$|L_{Q,p}(u)|\leq C_{Q,p}\|u-(u)_Q\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}$ gives~$Y_N\leq C_{Q,p}K_{Q,N}$ by the variational formula. Consequently, at any of the finite moment orders chosen before disorder,
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_N\|Y_N-Y_{N,h}\|_{L^q}\leq C_{q,Q,p}h^{\nf14},\qquad
	\sup_{N,\,0<h<1/8}\|Y_{N,h}\|_{L^q}<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
These are the fixed-cube versions of~\eqref{eq:mfd-9}. They use transported loads and coercivity; the deterministic response of the identity coefficient on~$Q$ is~$|Q|\,p\cdot p$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Reflection across the faces}

Let~$a$ be a scalar coefficient, and let~$u$ be a solution with zero conormal derivative and bounded source~$f$. Reflect~$u$ and~$f$ evenly across a set of faces. This gives a solution~$u\circ T$ of the equation with coefficient~$a\circ T$ and source~$f\circ T$, where~$T$ folds the reflected domain onto the original cube. The reflected equation has no additional source concentrated on the folding planes. On a set invariant under these reflections, the energy of~$u\circ T$ is two to the power of the number of reflected faces times the energy of~$u$ in the part of the set inside the original cube.

\begin{lemma}[Effect of folding on~$\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}$]
\label{mfd:thm-fold}
Let~$T$ fold the cube~$z+\cu_M$ across coordinate planes through~$z$, as above. For every continuous positive coefficient~$a$ on its closure, scalar~$a_0>0$,~$s\in(0,\nf12)$ and~$n\leq M$,
\begin{equation}
	\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}(z+\cu_M,n;a\circ T,a_0)^2
	\leq\left(1+\frac{3d}{3^{1-2s}-1}\right)
	\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}(z+\cu_M,n;a,a_0)^2\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-11}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write~$\kappa_n(a)$ for the maximum, over grid cubes~$U\subset z+\cu_M$ of side~$3^n$, of~$\sup_{|e|=1}J(U,e,e;a/a_0)$. On a cube meeting no folding plane,~$T$ reflects some of the coordinates, so changing variables by~$T$ shows that the energy for~$a\circ T$ is at most~$\kappa_n(a)$. Subdivide each cube that meets a folding plane triadically, and repeat the subdivision on the subcubes that still meet one. After~$j$ subdivisions, the subcubes that still meet a folding plane fill at most a fraction~$d3^{-j}$ of the cube. By subadditivity~\eqref{e.subadditivity.J},~$\kappa_n(a\circ T)\leq\kappa_n(a)+\sum_{j\geq1}3d\,3^{-j}\kappa_{n-j}(a)$. Insert this inequality into Definition~\ref{d.mathcal.E} and sum the geometric series~$\sum_{j\geq1}3^{-(1-2s)j}$ to obtain~\eqref{eq:mfd-11}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Energy decay for the folded coefficient]
\label{mfd:prop-folded-iteration}
There are dimensional constants~$C<\infty$ and~$K\geq1$ with the following property. Let~$\gmcdelta\leq C^{-1}$ and~$\gamma\in[1/2,1)$ satisfy~$1-\gamma\geq CK\gmcdelta|\log\gmcdelta|^{1/2}$, let~$m,L\in\N_0$ with~$m\leq L$, and let~$T$ fold the cube~$z+\cu_m$ across coordinate planes through~$z$. Set~$\gamma_*=1-(1-\gamma)/K$. Define~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$ by~\eqref{e.L.def.reg} for the cube~$z+\cu_m$, with all parameter choices in~\eqref{e.choices.hepk.Holder.proof} evaluated at~$\gamma_*$; in particular,~$\lambda_*=\lambda/K$ and~$\ep_*=\ep/\sqrt K$, where~$\lambda$ and~$\ep$ denote the choices at~$\gamma$. If~$u\in H^1(z+\cu_m)$ solves~$-\nabla\cdot((\a_L\circ T)\nabla u)=\nabla\cdot\tilde g$, with~$\tilde g\in C^{0,\nf12}(z+\cu_m;\Rd)$, then, for~$0\leq n\leq m-\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$,
\begin{multline}
	\|(\a_L\circ T)^{\nf12}\nabla u\|_{\underline L^2(z+\cu_n)}
	\\
	\leq C3^{(1-\gamma)(m-n)}
	\left(\|(\a_L\circ T)^{\nf12}\nabla u\|_{\underline L^2(z+\cu_m)}
	+(\b_{L,m})_{z+\cu_m}^{-\nf12}3^{\nf m2}[\tilde g]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}(z+\cu_m)}\right)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-folded-energy}
\end{multline}
For every~$h\geq0$, this minimal scale satisfies
\begin{equation*}
	\P[\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)>h]
	\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\gamma)^2(h-C)_+}
	{CK^2\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}\right)\,,
\end{equation*}
uniformly in~$L$,~$m$ and the deterministic translate~$z$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Apply the argument proving~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate} at~$z$, using the original averages~$(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}$. The harmonic approximation and excess decay use the good event through~$\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}$, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates}. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-11}, folding multiplies this quantity by at most a dimensional constant. The bounds with~$q=1$ at the larger exponents follow from the bound with~$q=2$ at~$s_0=\nf1{32}$ by weighted Cauchy--Schwarz. Choose a dimensional~$K\geq1$ large enough to absorb these factors, and evaluate the parameter choices~\eqref{e.choices.hepk.Holder.proof} at~$\gamma_*=1-(1-\gamma)/K$, so that~$\lambda_*=\lambda/K$ and~$\ep_*=\ep/\sqrt K$, as in the statement. This uses the same good events at the stricter parameter~$\ep_*$. The error sum, the bound on bad scales and the ratios of the original averages are the bounds~\eqref{e.ep.j.z.sum},~\eqref{e.Bz.bound} and~\eqref{e.ratio.of.bs} with these parameter choices. They supply the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC};~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} supplies its coarse-grained ellipticity inputs. All its cubes are centered at~$z$ and invariant under the reflections. The stricter choices give the asserted estimate with exponent~$\gamma$.

\smallskip

We record the tail of the minimal scale with this reduced tolerance. Each of the two scales in~\eqref{e.L.def.reg} has tail length bounded by a constant times
\begin{equation*}
	s_0^{-7}\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|\lambda_*^{-2}
	+s_0^{-8}\ep_*^{-2}\lambda_*^{-1}
	\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|
	\leq \frac{CK^2\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}{(1-\gamma)^2}\,.
\end{equation*}
Indeed, both~$\lambda_*^{-2}$ and~$\ep_*^{-2}\lambda_*^{-1}$ gain the factor~$K^2$. Equations~\eqref{e.M.lambda.m.bound} and~\eqref{e.N.theta.m.bound.goodscales}, or~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.error.stopping.tail} and~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.stopping.tail} for the cutoff version, therefore give
\begin{equation*}
	\P[\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)>h]
	\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\gamma)^2(h-C)_+}
	{CK^2\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}\right)\,.
\end{equation*}
The constants are uniform in the cutoff, the root scale and its deterministic translate. The hypothesis on~$1-\gamma$ places~$\gamma_*$ in the range of Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity}, because~$C$ may be taken at least as large as the constant in that proposition. These are parameter choices in the existing estimates; the definitions of the good events are unchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Bounded Neumann sources]
\label{mfd:cor-neumann-source}
Let~$\alpha\in(0,1)$ and~$t\in(d-1,d)$, and fix a finite list of moment orders. There is~$\gmcdelta_0(d,\alpha,t,\text{list})>0$ such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then on every fixed cube~$Q$ there are random variables~$K_N$ with~$\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ for every order~$p$ in the list, such that the following holds for every~$f\in L^\infty(Q)$ with~$\int_Qf=0$. If~$u_N\in H^1(Q)$ is the mean-zero solution of
\begin{equation*}
	\int_QA_N\nabla u_N\cdot\nabla v=\int_Qfv
	\qquad(v\in H^1(Q))\,,
\end{equation*}
then
\begin{equation*}
	\|u_N\|_{C^\alpha(\overline Q)}\leq K_N\|f\|_\infty\,,
\end{equation*}
and, for every~$x\in Q$ and~$0<r\leq1$,
\begin{equation*}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)\leq K_N\|f\|_\infty^2r^t\,.
\end{equation*}
The same threshold and moment bounds hold with~$A_N$ replaced by~$A_N^0$ or by the coefficient obtained by replacing~$H$ with~$H_{L'}$, uniformly in~$L'$, with the solution~$u_N$ and the energy~$\Gamma_N$ computed using the corresponding coefficient. For each fixed~$Q$, a common~$K_N$ may be chosen for these infrared conventions and for all sources. For the stationary coefficient~$A_N^0$, the same bounds hold on each translate~$w+Q$ of~$Q$, with the corresponding translated random constants, and a fixed finite collection of such translates may be handled by taking the maximum of these constants.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
We track the energy~$\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)$ of small balls. Step~1 rescales the problem to the stationary coefficient. Step~2 joins each target ball to a ball of fixed radius by a chain of at most~$d+1$ applications of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-folded-iteration}, reflecting evenly across each face that the chain comes close to. The random factors in these applications depend on the target ball; the main work, in Steps~3 and~4, is to bound them, up to small negative powers of the radii, by random variables that do not depend on the target and whose moments are bounded uniformly in~$N$; this is done by summing over meshes of stopping depths and of reference averages. Together with the coercivity estimate~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} for the total energy, this gives the Morrey bound for~$r\geq3^{-N}$. Step~5 treats the radii below~$3^{-N}$ and proves the H\"older bound; the last paragraphs remove the infrared truncation and pass to general fixed cubes.

\smallskip

We first work on~$Q=(0,1)^d$. Choose
\begin{equation*}
	\max\{t,d-2+2\alpha\}<t_1<t_0<d,\qquad
	\eta=t_0-t_1,\qquad 0<\theta<d+2-t_0\,.
\end{equation*}
Fix all moment orders to be used below before choosing~$\gmcdelta$. These include the given list, the larger orders for products with coercivity constants and the auxiliary orders used for the two meshes. When this proposition is used in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-16}, include~$3p$; when it is used at order~$P$ and at order~$2P$ in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-band}, include~$3P$ and~$6P$, respectively. This is still a finite list. The auxiliary orders are~$v$ in Step~3 and~$q$ in Step~4, and the conditions on~$\gmcdelta$ imposed in those steps depend only on these orders and on quantities fixed before~$\gmcdelta$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We specify the physical normalization. Temporarily replace~$H$ by~$H_{L'}=\sum_{i=1}^{L'}(\g_i-\g_i(0))$, including~$L'=0$. Reindexing the stationary fields gives
\begin{equation*}
	A_N^{(L')}(y)=c_{N,L'}\a_{N+L'}(3^Ny),\qquad
	c_{N,L'}=\ahom_N^{-1}
	\exp\left(L'\tau^2-\sum_{i=1}^{L'}\g_i(0)\right)\,.
\end{equation*}
Here the stationary field on the right is taken in the reindexed environment. Choose a measurable representative of~$f$ bounded everywhere by its essential supremum, changing it on a null set if necessary; this preserves its integral and weak load. Under~$X=3^Ny$ and~$v(X)=3^Nu_N(3^{-N}X)$, its equation has source~$(c_{N,L'}3^N)^{-1}f(3^{-N}X)$. Even reflection replaces this source by its composition with the coordinate fold. On a reflection-invariant set, the stationary energy of~$v$ is~$2^{|I|}3^{dN}c_{N,L'}^{-1}$ times the physical energy in its intersection with~$Q$, where~$I$ is the set of reflected coordinates.

\smallskip

Thus the physical reference in the source term is the stationary average multiplied by~$c_{N,L'}$. At depth~$k$ it has the equivalent formula
\begin{equation*}
	b_{N,k}(z)=\fint_{B_{3^{-k}/2}(z)}
	\frac{\ahom_{N-k}}{\ahom_N}
	\exp\left(H_{L'}(x)+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\g_{-j}(x)-k\tau^2\right)\,dx\,,
	\qquad 0\leq k\leq N\,.
\end{equation*}
Use the fixed shift of depth prescribed by the outer cube in the iteration: for a root of radius~$R_i=3^{-k_i}/2$ this reference is~$b_{N,(k_i-J)_+}(z_i)$, where~$J$ is a fixed dimensional buffer. Its averaging radius is comparable to~$R_i$ with a fixed factor. We write~$b_i$ for this physical reference. Its inverse is~$b_i^{-1}$; the factor~$c_{N,L'}$ has already been included.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We construct the reflected chains. Fix~$J$ first, and then fix~$L_*>10$ with~$L_*\geq2\sqrt d\,3^J$. Fix a half-triadic~$R_*>0$ with~$R_*<(100L_*)^{-1}$ and~$R_*\leq3^{-J}/2$. All three are fixed independently of~$N$ and~$L'$. At a center~$y$, put~$\delta_i=\min\{y_i,1-y_i\}$ and let~$p_i$ be its nearer face,~$0$ or~$1$. If~$I$ is the set of activated coordinates, the current center~$z_I$ has coordinate~$p_i$ for~$i\in I$ and coordinate~$y_i$ otherwise. Start with the least half-triadic radius~$s$ containing the target cube. Stop if~$s\geq R_*/24$.

\smallskip

If an inactive face satisfies~$\delta_i\leq100L_*s$, activate~$i$, project the center to that face, and replace~$s$ by the least half-triadic radius at least~$s+\delta_i$. The old target lies in the new one, and the radius increases by at most~$3(1+100L_*)$. Otherwise choose the largest half-triadic~$R$ such that~$R\leq R_*$ and~$4L_*R\leq\delta_i$ for every~$i\notin I$. Then~$8s<R$. Reflect across every activated face and apply Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-folded-iteration} between radii comparable to~$s$ and~$R$. If~$R=R_*$, stop. Otherwise activate a nearest inactive face and take the least half-triadic radius at least~$L_*R+\delta_i$ about the newly projected center. The clearance condition keeps every other face outside the outer cube of this application.

\smallskip

Each transition activates a new coordinate. There are at most~$d$ transitions and~$d+1$ energy-decay applications, including at edges and corners. The intervening containments bound the enlargement factors by fixed constants. Telescoping the ratios in the energy-decay applications bounds their product by~$C(r/R_*)^{t_0}$. No assumption that the target meets only one face is made.

\smallskip

Set~$\gamma=(t_0-d+2)/2$. In each application let~$B_i$ be the minimal stopping depth for the original, translated stationary field at the reduced tolerance of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-folded-iteration}, with its fixed outer-scale buffers. If the requested depth is below that stopping depth, use energy monotonicity instead: increasing a fixed~$c\geq t_0\log3$ makes~$e^{cB_i}(s/R_i)^{t_0}\geq1$ in that case. Thus the same one-step inequality holds at every requested depth. The local Newtonian primitive of the evenly reflected bounded source has half-H\"older seminorm bounded by~$CR_i^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty$, by~\eqref{eq:mfd-primitive}. If instead a primitive supported on the fixed outer cube is used, its local bound has the harmless extra factor~$1+\log(1/R_i)$. Keeping this larger bound, composition of the chain gives
\begin{equation*}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)
	\leq C e^{c\sum_iB_i}r^{t_0}
	\left[R_*^{-t_0}E_N^Q(u_N)
	+\|f\|_\infty^2\sum_i
	b_i^{-1}R_i^{d+2-t_0}(1+\log(1/R_i))^2\right]\,.
\end{equation*}
Here and below constants absorb the at most~$2^d$ reflection multiplicity and the bounded number of enlargements. It remains to bound the random factors~$e^{c\sum_iB_i}$ and~$b_i^{-1}$, up to small negative powers of the radii, by random variables that do not depend on the chain and whose moments are bounded uniformly in~$N$; Steps~3 and~4 do this.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We sum the stopping-depth mesh. At target depth~$n$, use spacing~$3^{-(n+J)}$. Enlarge the target by this spacing to contain every target centered in the corresponding mesh cell, and choose an interior mesh point even for targets touching the boundary. Catalogue all permutations of the coordinates and all~$d+1$ root-depth slots between~$0$ and~$n+J$. Activated sets are initial segments of the permutation, so every chain in Step~2 is represented. This catalogue has cardinality at most~$C_J(n+J+1)^{d+1}3^{dn}$. The allowance in a slot of depth~$k\leq N$ is the minimal stopping depth on the outer stationary cube at scale~$N-k+J$, plus~$J$ and the fixed iteration buffer; put it equal to zero when~$k>N$.

\smallskip

By the reduced-tolerance tail just proved, there are fixed~$A,C$ such that each allowance~$B$ satisfies
\begin{equation*}
	\P[B>h]\leq A e^{-\beta(h-A)_+},\qquad
	\beta=\frac{(1-\gamma)^2}{CK^2\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}\,.
\end{equation*}
This is uniform in every deterministic translated center, cutoff and root depth. For~$a>0$ and~$\beta\geq2a$, summing the tail gives
\begin{equation*}
	\E e^{aB}\leq e^{a(A+1)}
	\left(1+A\sum_{h\geq0}(e^a-1)e^{-(\beta-a)h}\right)
	\leq C(A,a)\,.
\end{equation*}
Choose~$v$ larger than all the moment orders needed for the products below and such that~$v\eta>d$, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that~$\beta\geq2(d+1)vc$. H\"older's inequality over the~$d+1$ slots gives a uniform~$v$th moment of~$Z_{N,n,\pi}=C_0e^{c\sum_iB_i}$. It requires no independence between the slots. Define
\begin{equation*}
	U_N=\sup_{n\geq0,\,\pi}\,3^{-\eta n}Z_{N,n,\pi}\,.
\end{equation*}
Then
\begin{equation*}
	\E U_N^v\leq C\sum_{n\geq0}
	(n+J+1)^{d+1}3^{(d-v\eta)n}<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
The bound is uniform in~$N$. In particular, the mesh factor for a target of size~$r$ is at most~$CU_Nr^{-\eta}$. We have summed minimal stopping depths, whose tail controls the entire unavailable prefix of scales.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We sum the reference mesh at its own root depth. Write~$s_{N,k}(x)$ for the integrand defining~$b_{N,k}$. At spacing comparable to~$R_k=3^{-k}/2$, use the statistic
\begin{equation*}
	T_{N,k}(y)=e^{\operatorname{osc}_{B_{3R_k}(y)}
	(H_{L'}+\sum_{j<k}\g_{-j})}
	\bigl(s_{N,k}(y)+s_{N,k}(y)^{-1}\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
Every average centered within~$R_k$ of~$y$ and its inverse are bounded by~$T_{N,k}(y)$. The pointwise positive and negative moment bounds and the bounded-scale oscillation estimate used in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} give, at every fixed auxiliary order~$q$,
\begin{equation*}
	\|T_{N,k}(y)\|_{L^q}
	\leq C_q e^{C(q+q^2)\gmcdelta^2 k},\qquad k\leq N\,.
\end{equation*}
These are bounds for the normalized physical reference; in particular, they include the ratio~$\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N$. Choose~$q$ at least as large as~$v$ and with~$q\theta>d$, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ until~$C(q+q^2)\gmcdelta^2<\tfrac12(\theta-d/q)\log3$. There are at most~$C3^{dk}$ points at depth~$k$. Consequently, for
\begin{equation*}
	V_N=\sup_{k\leq N,\,y\text{ in the depth-}k\text{ mesh}}
	R_k^\theta T_{N,k}(y)\,,
\end{equation*}
Minkowski's inequality bounds~$\|V_N\|_{L^q}$ by
\begin{equation*}
	C_q\sum_{k\geq0}3^{-(\theta-d/q)k}
	e^{C(q+q^2)\gmcdelta^2 k}<\infty\,,
\end{equation*}
uniformly in~$N$ and~$L'$. Thus~$b_i^{-1}\leq CV_NR_i^{-\theta}$, including the fixed depth shift in Step~1. The strict margin gives~$\sup_{0<R\leq R_*}R^{d+2-t_0-\theta}(1+\log(1/R))^2<\infty$. With at most~$d+1$ source terms, Steps~2--4 therefore imply, for~$r\geq3^{-N}$,
\begin{equation*}
	\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)
	\leq CU_N r^{t_1}
	\bigl(E_N^Q(u_N)+V_N\|f\|_\infty^2\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
The mean-zero coercivity estimate~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} bounds~$E_N^Q(u_N)$ by~$CK_N\|f\|_\infty^2$. Taking~$U_N,V_N$ and~$K_N$ at twice the requested orders bounds the resulting product in every requested~$L^p$. Here~$K_N$ denotes the coercivity constant; the final constant in the statement is enlarged to include the resulting products.

\smallskip

The constants can be chosen uniformly in the infrared approximation. On the fixed compact enlargement used above, put~$S=\sum_{i\geq1}\|\g_i-\g_i(0)\|_{C^{1,1}}$. The proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared} gives~$\|S\|_{L^a}\leq C\gmcdelta\sqrt a$ and finite exponential moments at every fixed order. It bounds~$H$,~$0$ and all~$H_{L'}$ simultaneously. In particular, their normalized references and inverse ellipticity constants compare with the zero-infrared ones by~$e^S$. Use the doubled orders in these products; the microscopic logarithmic gradients are bounded by the same envelope as well. The stopping-depth mesh is built from the reindexed stationary field. Every root actually used in a chain has~$k\geq J$, hence its outer scale~$N-k+J$ is at most~$N\leq N+L'$. Equation~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.replacements} therefore uses the same stopping variable for all~$L'$. Unused catalogue slots can use that same stationary stopping variable without a one-step application. Thus no union over infinitely many infrared truncations is needed. The root~$R_*$ and all geometric constants were fixed before their number of terms. For the stationary coefficient~$A_N^0$, the translates~$w+Q$ use the law-preserving translation of this field and their own cutoff and reference. Any spatial maximum needed inside a fixed cube is included in the displayed mesh sums. A fixed finite collection of such translates can be included in their catalogues without changing the exponent conditions.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 5.} We finish at the microscopic scales and pass to~$H$. Reflect evenly across every face meeting the microscopic neighborhood. The coordinate fold is~$1$-Lipschitz, so it preserves the required small oscillation of~$\log A_N$; the reflected weak equation has the evenly reflected bounded source. Take~$p_1$ so large that~$d-2d/p_1>t_1$ and~$1-d/p_1>\alpha$, and apply~\eqref{eq:mfd-micro} at~$\ell_N=c3^{-N}(1+D_N)^{-1}$. Step~4 at scale~$3^{-N}$ supplies its outer energy. Dividing by~$r^t$ gives the two factors~$3^{-(t_1-t)N}$ and~$3^{-(d+2-t)N}$ from Step~4 of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth}; their strictly positive exponents absorb the coefficient-extreme moments at the finitely many larger orders used in the products. Divide by the reflection multiplicity to return to~$Q$.

\smallskip

For the H\"older estimate, the local coarse-grained Poincar\'e inequality applied to the same energy bound gives the Campanato exponent~$t_1+2-d$ minus an arbitrarily small mesh loss. Choose that loss so that the exponent is larger than~$2\alpha$. Its inverse-ellipticity mesh has the same convergent moment summation as~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}. Below~$3^{-N}$ use the preceding~$W^{1,p_1}$ estimate and Morrey's inequality. The first two factors in Step~7 of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} have positive exponents because~$t_1>d-2+2\alpha$; there is no boundary-datum term. Telescope the cube averages and use the mean-zero condition to control the supremum norm. Enlarging the random constant gives both asserted estimates. Every constant constructed above is a statistic of the coefficient, independent of~$f$ and of the chosen solution.

\smallskip

On the common infrared-convergence event the truncated coefficients converge uniformly on~$\overline Q$. Compare the weak equations on the mean-zero space, testing with the difference of the solutions. Coercivity yields gradient convergence; the H\"older bound yields uniform convergence. Fatou's lemma preserves the moment bounds. One can use the same moment-bounded coefficient statistics for every source before this passage, so the final constant is source independent. Countable intersections make the event common to all cutoffs and the required cell families; the deterministic weak-equation estimates then apply to every bounded mean-zero source.

\smallskip

Finally consider a fixed cube~$w+R\cu_0$. For~$A_N$, choose its fixed triadic scale, transport the stationary environment by translation and that scale shift, and run the preceding argument on the remaining cube of side in a fixed bounded interval. For~$A_N^0$ and the truncations~$H_{L'}$, a shift of a cube with~$R>1$ to bounded size deletes the coarsest layers of the coefficient, which only the anchored coefficient retains. We therefore run the preceding argument directly on~$w+R\cu_0$ in these cases. Its chains have roots of radius at most~$R_*$, so their outer scales do not exceed the cutoff; the catalogue of Step~3 is larger by a factor depending only on~$R$; and the global energy is bounded by the coercivity of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error}. In the anchored case, if~$A_N(w+Ry)=c_N\widetilde A(y)$ and~$\widetilde u(y)=u_N(w+Ry)$, its source is~$R^2c_N^{-1}f(w+Ry)$ and its physical energy is~$R^{d-2}c_N$ times the transported energy. Thus the transported Morrey constant is multiplied by~$R^{d+2-t}c_N^{-1}$, and the H\"older constant by~$R^2\max\{1,R^{-\alpha}\}c_N^{-1}$. The reference inverse~$c_N^{-1}$ is bounded by an exponential of the fixed anchor field and finitely many retained layers; it has every required moment uniformly in~$N$ and in the infrared truncation. Use twice the requested orders in this final product. If the shifted cutoff is negative, only finitely many original cutoffs remain, and the microscopic argument covers them. The exponent conditions and the disorder threshold are the same; the final moment bounds may depend on the fixed cube.
\end{proof}

Applying Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} to the mean-zero source~$f_\epsilon$ of~\eqref{eq:mfd-bump}, with~$\|f_\epsilon\|_\infty\leq C\epsilon^{-1}$, gives, with one random constant for all~$\epsilon$,
\begin{equation}
	\Gamma_N(v_{N,\epsilon})(B_r(x)\cap Q)
	\leq K_N\epsilon^{-2}r^t\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-13}
\end{equation}

\subsection{Compactness of the finite-volume energies}
\label{mfd:sec-resampling}

We approximate each finite-volume energy by its conditional expectation given finitely many fields, and we bound the error by resampling one field at a time. These conditional expectations form a compact family in~$L^p(\P)$.

\smallskip

On a fixed cube~$Q$, write~$E_N=E_N^Q$ for the form in~\eqref{eq:mfd-energy}. By a \emph{finite-volume energy}~$\mathcal R_N$ we mean either the minimal energy~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(\varphi)$ with a nonconstant smooth boundary datum~$\varphi$, or the quadratic form~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle$ of the Dirichlet Green operator for a nonzero smooth source~$f$. Write~$\mathcal R(g)$ for the same energy computed with the coefficient whose logarithm is~$g$, so that~$\mathcal R_N=\mathcal R(\log A_N)$.

\subsubsection{Local perturbations of the coefficient}

\begin{lemma}[Multiplicative perturbations]
\label{mfd:cor-14}
Let~$A$ be bounded above and below by positive constants, let~$g$ be bounded and supported in~$B\subset Q$. Write~$E^g(v)=\int_Qe^gA|\nabla v|^2$ and~$E=E^0$. Let~$u$ and~$u^g$ be the minimizers for the coefficients~$A$ and~$e^gA$ with the same Dirichlet boundary values, or the solutions for these coefficients with the same linear right-hand side and zero Dirichlet or zero conormal boundary condition; in the conormal case, the right-hand side vanishes on constants and the solutions have mean zero. Denote their energies by~$\mathcal R,\mathcal R^g$. Then
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		&E^g(u^g-u)\leq\|g\|_\infty^2e^{3\|g\|_\infty}\Gamma_E(u)(B),\qquad
		\Gamma_{E^g}(u^g)(B)\leq4e^{\|g\|_\infty}\Gamma_E(u)(B)\,,\\
		&|\mathcal R^g-\mathcal R|\leq2\|g\|_\infty e^{4\|g\|_\infty}\Gamma_E(u)(B)\,.
	\end{aligned}
	\label{eq:mfd-14}
\end{equation}
For two log coefficients~$g,h$ and any of these energies,
\begin{equation}
	e^{-\|g-h\|_\infty}\mathcal R(h)\leq\mathcal R(g)
	\leq e^{\|g-h\|_\infty}\mathcal R(h)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-mult}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Set~$v=u^g-u$. Subtracting the Euler equations gives~$E^g(v,\phi)=-\int_B(e^g-1)\,d\Gamma_E(u,\phi)$. Test with~$v$ and apply Cauchy--Schwarz to obtain
\begin{equation}
	E^g(v)^{\nf12}\leq
	(e^{\|g\|_\infty/2}-e^{-\|g\|_\infty/2})\Gamma_E(u)(B)^{\nf12}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-perturbation-sharp}
\end{equation}
This proves the first bound in~\eqref{eq:mfd-14}; the triangle inequality in~$L^2(B,e^gA\,dx)$ gives the second estimate. The Euler equations also give
\begin{equation*}
	\mathcal R^g-\mathcal R
	=\pm\left[\int_B(e^g-1)\,d\Gamma_E(u)-E^g(v)\right]\,,
\end{equation*}
with plus sign when~$\mathcal R$ is a minimal energy with given boundary values and minus sign when it is the energy of a solution with given right-hand side. Using~$|e^g-1|\leq\|g\|_\infty e^{\|g\|_\infty}$ proves the third bound. Monotonicity in the coefficient and scaling by constants give~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult}. These arguments use only the Euler equations and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality for energy measures, so they also apply to the regular strongly local forms constructed below.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Resampling one random field}

We bound the change in a finite-volume energy when a field~$\g_{-j}$ with~$j\geq0$ is replaced by an independent copy. The field has scale~$r=3^{-j}$ and range of dependence~$\sqrt d\,r$, so its restrictions to boxes at mutual distance larger than~$\sqrt d\,r$ are independent. We partition~$Q$ into boxes of side~$\ell$ with~$r\ll\ell\ll1$. Delete the field on strips of width of order~$r$ around the faces of the boxes, and apply the Efron--Stein inequality to the independent restrictions of the field to the remaining interiors of the boxes. The energy in the strips controls the error caused by deleting the field there. The largest energy in one box controls the differences~$X-X^{(i)}$ in the Efron--Stein inequality~\eqref{eq:mfd-ES} below. By the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4}, both are bounded by a positive power of the scale~$r$. Exponential moments of the sup norm of the field enter through Lemma~\ref{mfd:cor-14}; they grow more slowly than any negative power of the scale~$r$, so this decay absorbs them.

\begin{lemma}[Mass near the box faces and in a single box]
\label{mfd:lem-strips}
Let~$t>d-1$ and~$K<\infty$, and let~$\nu$ be a finite positive measure on a fixed cube~$Q$ such that~$\nu(B_\rho(x)\cap Q)\leq K\rho^t$ for every~$x\in Q$ and~$0<\rho\leq1$. For~$4\sqrt d\,r<\ell\leq1$, partition~$Q$ into boxes of side~$\ell$, and call \textbf{strips} the points within distance~$4\sqrt d\,r$ of a box face. There is~$C(d,t,Q)<\infty$ such that
\begin{equation*}
	\nu(\text{strips})\leq CK\ell^{-1}r^{t-d+1}\,,
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
	\max_{B\ \mathrm{box}}\nu(B)\leq CK\ell^t\,.
\end{equation*}
With~$\ell=r^{(t-d+1)/(t+1)}$ and sufficiently small~$r$, both bounds are~$CKr^b$, where~$b=t(t-d+1)/(t+1)>0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
There are at most~$C_Q\ell^{-1}$ face hyperplanes. Each strip is covered by~$C_Qr^{1-d}$ balls of radius~$4\sqrt d\,r$, and each box by a dimensional number of balls of radius~$\ell$. Apply the bound~$\nu(B_\rho(x)\cap Q)\leq K\rho^t$ and use~$\ell^{-1}r^{t-d+1}=\ell^t=r^b$ for the stated choice of~$\ell$.
\end{proof}

The sup norm on~$Q$ of a fine-scale field grows only logarithmically in the inverse of its scale, and its exponential moments grow more slowly than any power of the inverse scale. By~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}, the variable~$\|\g_0\|_{L^\infty(\cu_0)}$ is sub-Gaussian with parameter~$C\gmcdelta$. Let~$j\geq0$ and~$r=3^{-j}$, and let~$S=\|\g_{-j}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$. The field~$\g_{-j}$ has the law of~$\g_0(3^j\,\cdot)$. Cover~$3^jQ$ by at most~$Cr^{-d}$ unit cubes to obtain~$\P[S>s]\leq2Cr^{-d}\exp(-s^2/C\gmcdelta^2)$. The union bound and~$(s+a)^2\geq s^2+a^2$ show that the excess of~$S$ over~$\gmcdelta\sqrt{C(1+|\log r|)}$ has the tail~$\exp(-s^2/C\gmcdelta^2)$. Thus the excess is sub-Gaussian with parameter~$C\gmcdelta$, uniformly in~$r$. Consequently, for all~$k\geq0$,~$\lambda\geq0$,~$p<\infty$ and~$\gamma>0$, and every~$0<\gmcdelta\leq1$,
\begin{equation}
	\|S^ke^{\lambda S}\|_{L^p}
	\leq C(p,k,\lambda)\,\gmcdelta^{k}(1+|\log r|)^{\nf{k}{2}}
	\exp\bigl(C\lambda\gmcdelta\sqrt{1+|\log r|}+C_p\lambda^2\gmcdelta^2\bigr)
	\leq C(p,k,\lambda,\gamma)\,\gmcdelta^{k}r^{-\gamma}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-layernorm}
\end{equation}
The splitting of~$S$ into a deterministic logarithmic part and a sub-Gaussian excess is needed: a union bound applied directly to the exponential moment would produce the factor~$Cr^{-d}$.

\smallskip

The fluctuations are estimated with the following moment form of the Efron--Stein inequality. Let~$\xi_1,\dots,\xi_m$ be independent random elements, let~$p\geq2$, and let~$X=X(\xi_1,\dots,\xi_m)\in L^p$ be measurable. Write~$X^{(i)}$ for the variable obtained by replacing~$\xi_i$ with an independent copy, and~$\E_i$ for the integral over this copy alone. Then
\begin{equation}
	\|X-\E X\|_{L^p}\leq C_p\Bigl\|\Bigl(\sum_i\E_i\bigl[(X-X^{(i)})^2\bigr]\Bigr)^{\nf12}\Bigr\|_{L^p}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-ES}
\end{equation}
This is a consequence of~\citet*[Theorem~2]{BBLM2005}, who proved two one-sided bounds in terms of variance proxies; both proxies are dominated by the sum on the right side of~\eqref{eq:mfd-ES}. The inequality also holds conditionally on every~$\sigma$-field independent of the random elements and their copies.

\begin{lemma}[Resampling one random field]
\label{mfd:lem-15}
Let~$0<\gmcdelta\leq1$,~$t\in(d-1,d)$ and~$p\geq2$, and let~$\mathcal R_N$ be a finite-volume energy. Assume that, for every~$N$, the function~$u_N$ defining~$\mathcal R_N$ (the minimizer with the given boundary values, or the solution with zero Dirichlet boundary values) satisfies~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} on~$Q$ with the constant~$K_N$, and that
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^{3p}}+\sup_N\|\mathcal R_N\|_{L^{3p}}<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
For~$0\leq j\leq N$, let~$\mathcal R_N^{(j)}$ be the finite-volume energy obtained after replacing the field~$\g_{-j}$ by an independent copy. Let~$b$ be the exponent of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-strips}. Then there exists~$C_p<\infty$, depending on~$p$,~$d$,~$t$,~$Q$, on the boundary datum or source defining~$\mathcal R_N$ and on the two suprema in the previous display, such that, for all integers~$0\leq j\leq N$,
\begin{equation}
	\|\mathcal R_N-\mathcal R_N^{(j)}\|_{L^p}\leq C_p\,\gmcdelta\,3^{-bj/8}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-15}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Put~$r=3^{-j}$ and let~$S=\|\g_{-j}\|_\infty$ on~$Q$, as in~\eqref{eq:mfd-layernorm}. A prime denotes the corresponding quantity after this field is resampled. Throughout, expectations over this field are conditional on the other fields. For the finitely many scales at which the strip construction of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-strips} does not apply, the bound~$|\mathcal R_N-\mathcal R_N'|\leq(S+S')e^{S+S'}\mathcal R_N$ and~\eqref{eq:mfd-layernorm} suffice.

\smallskip

At the remaining scales, partition~$Q$ into boxes of side~$\ell=r^{(t-d+1)/(t+1)}$. Choose smooth cutoff functions~$\chi_i$, one for each box, supported on interior subboxes that are separated by more than~$\sqrt d\,r$, such that~$\chi=\sum_i\chi_i$ equals one outside the strips of width~$4\sqrt d\,r$. Let~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N$ be the energy after deleting~$(1-\chi)\g_{-j}$. Let~$\widehat\nu$ be the energy measure of the corresponding solution. The restrictions of the field to these subboxes are independent. Lemmas~\ref{mfd:cor-14} and~\ref{mfd:lem-strips} give~$\widehat\nu(Q)\leq e^S\mathcal R_N$,
\begin{equation*}
	|\mathcal R_N-\widehat{\mathcal R}_N|\leq CSe^{CS}K_Nr^b\,,
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
	\max_B\widehat\nu(B)\leq Ce^{CS}K_Nr^b\,,
\end{equation*}
where the maximum is over the interior subboxes~$B$. For the last bound, apply~\eqref{eq:mfd-perturbation-sharp} on the strips and the inequality that~$\widehat\nu(B)$ is at most~$2e^S\Gamma_N(u_N)(B)$ plus twice the energy, for the form with the deleted field, of the difference between the minimizers before and after the deletion. The second term bounds the change in the minimizer when replacing~$\g_{-j}$ by~$\chi\g_{-j}$.

\smallskip

Resampling the field on an interior subbox~$B$ changes~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N$ by at most~$C(S+S')e^{C(S+S')}\widehat\nu(B)$. Since~$\sum_B\widehat\nu(B)^2\leq\max_B\widehat\nu(B)\widehat\nu(Q)$, the conditional Efron--Stein inequality~\eqref{eq:mfd-ES} and conditional Jensen give
\begin{equation*}
	\|\widehat{\mathcal R}_N-\E_j\widehat{\mathcal R}_N\|_p
	\leq C_pr^{\nf{b}{2}}
	\bigl\|(S+S')e^{C(S+S')}K_N^{\nf{1}{2}}\mathcal R_N^{\nf{1}{2}}\bigr\|_p
	\leq C_p\gmcdelta r^{\nf{3b}{8}}\,.
\end{equation*}
The last step uses H\"older's inequality with three factors and~\eqref{eq:mfd-layernorm}, at a total loss of~$r^{-\nf{b}{8}}$. The errors~$\mathcal R_N-\widehat{\mathcal R}_N$ and~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N'-\mathcal R_N'$ from deleting the field on the strips are at most~$C_p\gmcdelta r^{\nf{7b}{8}}$ in~$L^p$.
