The energy~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N'$, computed with the cutoff function applied to the resampled field, has the same conditional mean as~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N$, so
\begin{equation}
	\mathcal R_N-\mathcal R_N'=(\mathcal R_N-\widehat{\mathcal R}_N)+(\widehat{\mathcal R}_N-\E_j\widehat{\mathcal R}_N)
	-(\widehat{\mathcal R}_N'-\E_j\widehat{\mathcal R}_N')+(\widehat{\mathcal R}_N'-\mathcal R_N')\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-fourterm}
\end{equation}
Since~$r^{\nf{3b}{8}}\leq3^{-bj/8}$, this proves~\eqref{eq:mfd-15}.
\end{proof}

The following martingale bound adds up the effects of the fields on which we do not condition.

\begin{lemma}[Summing resampling bounds]
\label{mfd:lem-resampling-sum}
Let~$(\xi_j)_{j\in I}$ be a countable independent family and let~$X\in L^p$,~$1\leq p<\infty$, be measurable with respect to this family. Write~$X^{(j)}$ for the result of replacing~$\xi_j$ by an independent copy. For every~$J\subseteq I$,
\begin{equation*}
	\bigl\|X-\E[X\mid\sigma(\xi_j:j\in J)]\bigr\|_p
	\leq\sum_{j\notin J}\|X-X^{(j)}\|_p\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Condition first on the retained variables, and then reveal the omitted ones one at a time, in some enumeration. At the step that reveals~$\xi_j$, the martingale difference is the conditional expectation of~$X-X^{(j)}$ given the variables revealed so far. Conditional Jensen bounds its~$L^p$ norm by~$\|X-X^{(j)}\|_p$. Sum and use~$L^p$ martingale convergence.
\end{proof}

We apply this bound to approximate an energy by conditioning on finitely many fields.

\begin{proposition}[Approximation by conditioning on finitely many fields]
\label{mfd:prop-16}
Let~$\ell\geq0$ be an integer, and let~$b$ be the exponent of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-strips}. Let~$\mathcal R_N$ be a finite-volume energy satisfying the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15} for every~$j\geq0$. Then there exists~$C_p<\infty$ such that, for every~$N\geq \ell$,
\begin{equation}
	\bigl\|\mathcal R_N-\E[\mathcal R_N\mid\g_{-j},\ |j|\leq\ell]\bigr\|_{L^p}
	\leq C_p\,\gmcdelta\,3^{-b\ell/8}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-16}
\end{equation}
On~$Q=(0,1)^d$, if instead~$\mathcal R_N=|Q|\,e\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)e$ is the Neumann energy for a Euclidean unit vector~$e$, fix~$p\geq2$ and take~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0(d,t,p)$. Assume~\eqref{eq:mfd-13}, with~$K_N$ bounded in~$L^{3p}$, and the smoothed-response bound in~\eqref{eq:mfd-9} at order~$3p$, and the approximation-error bound in~\eqref{eq:mfd-9} at order~$p$. The threshold is chosen so that the moment range of Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound} contains~$4p$ and so that Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} applies at order~$4p$; that lemma supplies the uniform~$L^{4p}$ bound for the unsmoothed response. Under these conditions,~\eqref{eq:mfd-16} holds with right side~$C_p\gmcdelta^{\nf12}3^{-b\ell/256}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For~$j\geq0$,~\eqref{eq:mfd-15} bounds the resampling error by~$C_p\gmcdelta3^{-bj/8}$, and the error is zero for~$j>N$. For~$j<0$, the field~$H$ changes by the difference of the expressions~$\g_{-j}-\g_{-j}(0)$ for the field and for its independent copy. This difference has norm of size~$C\gmcdelta3^j$ and sub-Gaussian tails by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}. Equation~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult} bounds the resampling error by~$C_p\gmcdelta3^j$. Sum both geometric series using Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-resampling-sum}.

\smallskip

For the Neumann energy on~$Q=(0,1)^d$, first use the volume source~$f_\epsilon$. The proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15} applies because Lemma~\ref{mfd:cor-14} also covers zero-conormal problems with a given right-hand side. Here~$C_p$ depends on the uniform~$L^{3p}$ bound for the source-independent~$K_N$ of~\eqref{eq:mfd-13}, the uniform~$L^{3p}$ bounds for the smoothed energies, and the uniform~$L^p$ bound for the approximation error in~\eqref{eq:mfd-9} after division by~$\epsilon^{\nf14}$. The coefficient-comparison and centered-energy estimates use their finite moment orders as well. All these orders are fixed before decreasing~$\gmcdelta$; the moment orders are those specified in the statement.

\smallskip

Choose a numerical bound~$B$ for the~$L^{3p}$ norms of~$K_N$ and the smoothed responses, the~$L^{4p}$ norms of the unsmoothed response, and the~$L^p$ approximation norm after division by~$\epsilon^{\nf14}$. The last two inputs are supplied by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} and~\eqref{eq:mfd-9}. Let~$C_{\rm resp}=C(d)$ be the constant in Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, and decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that their admissible range contains~$4p$, that is,~$4p\leq C_{\rm resp}^{-1}\gmcdelta^{-2}|\log\gmcdelta|^{-1}$. The constants and disorder threshold are chosen after these bounds and the fixed bump profile, and before the cutoff and field window. Here~$p\geq2$ as in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15};~$C_p$ depends on~$d,t,p,B,C_{\rm resp}$ and the fixed data and bump profile.

\smallskip

By~\eqref{eq:mfd-13}, the Morrey constant for the solution with source~$f_\epsilon$ is~$K_N\epsilon^{-2}$. This factor enters deletion of the field on strips linearly and the Efron--Stein estimate through its square root. Together with~\eqref{eq:mfd-9} and H\"older's inequality at the stated orders, this gives
\begin{equation*}
	\|\mathcal R_N-\E[\mathcal R_N\mid\g_{-j},\ |j|\leq\ell]\|_p
	\leq C_p\bigl(\epsilon^{\nf14}
	+\gmcdelta\epsilon^{-2}3^{-b\ell/8}
	+\gmcdelta3^{-\ell}\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
Choose~$\epsilon=3^{-b\ell/32}/16$ to obtain~$C_p3^{-b\ell/128}$.

\smallskip

For the other bound use the deterministic reference~$c_Q=|Q|\,e\cdot e=1$. With~$H$ removed, the physical dilation~$X=3^Nx$ maps~$Q$ to the translated cube of side~$3^N$ at its matching stationary cutoff~$N$. Reindex the stationary environment by this dilation and translation. Write~$J_N^{\rm ref}$ for its normalized defect~$J$. Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, at the finite orders up to~$4p$, gives~$\|J_N^{\rm ref}\|_{L^{4p}}\leq C_p\gmcdelta^2$. The matrix-deviation estimate~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.matrix.deviation.by.J} gives
\begin{equation*}
	|\mathcal R_N^0-1|^2\leq C J_N^{\rm ref}(1+J_N^{\rm ref}),\qquad
	\|\mathcal R_N^0-1\|_p\leq C_p\gmcdelta\,.
\end{equation*}
Restoring~$H$ changes the response by at most~$(e^{\|H\|_{L^\infty(Q)}}-1)\mathcal R_N^0$; H\"older's inequality and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared} bound this change by~$C_p\gmcdelta$. Conditional expectation is an~$L^p$ contraction, so
\begin{equation*}
	\|\mathcal R_N-\E[\mathcal R_N\mid\g_{-j},\ |j|\leq\ell]\|_p
	\leq2\|\mathcal R_N-c_Q\|_p\leq C_p\gmcdelta\,.
\end{equation*}
Taking the geometric mean with the preceding decay bound proves the asserted~$C_p\gmcdelta^{\nf12}3^{-b\ell/256}$ estimate. The norm of~$e$ here is Euclidean:~$|e|^2=\sum_i e_i^2$.

\smallskip

For the cell terms in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-band}, apply this estimate to the normalized response on the unit cube in the translated environment at the cell's own cutoff. Translation between~$\cu_0$ and~$(0,1)^d$ preserves the affine load and energy. More generally, if~$A_N(w+Ry)=s_N\widetilde A_m(y)$, the physical constant-flux response is~$R^d s_N^{-1}$ times the unit-cube response for~$\widetilde A_m$. The conditioning estimate applies after removing this scalar and at cutoff~$m$. Its finite moment inputs are chosen at~$3P$ and~$6P$ when the response is used at~$P$ and~$2P$. For general fixed physical cubes we use the transported volume loads in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error}, followed by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-response-bank}.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Compactness of conditional expectations}

\begin{proposition}[Strong compactness of the finite-volume energies]
\label{mfd:prop-response-compact}
Let~$Q$ be a cube, and let~$\mathcal R_N$ be the minimal energy~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(\varphi)$ with boundary values~$\varphi$, the quadratic form~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle$ of the Dirichlet Green operator with source~$f$, or the Neumann energy~$|Q|\,e\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)e$ for a constant flux~$e\in\Rd$. Assume~$1\leq p<q<\infty$ and~$\sup_N\|\mathcal R_N\|_{L^q}<\infty$. Assume also that, for some~$a>0$ and~$C_Q<\infty$, every pair of integers~$0\leq\ell\leq N$ satisfies
\begin{equation*}
	\bigl\|\mathcal R_N-\E[\mathcal R_N\mid\g_{-j},\ |j|\leq\ell]\bigr\|_{L^p}
	\leq C_Q\gmcdelta3^{-a\ell}\,.
\end{equation*}
Here~$C_Q$ may depend on the fixed cube, data and disorder, but not on~$N$ or~$\ell$. For a countable collection the exponent~$a$ is common and~$C_Q$ and the moment bound may depend on its member. For the minimal energy and the Dirichlet Green quadratic form the approximation is supplied by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-16}; for the constant-flux Neumann response on a fixed cube it follows from the load approximation proved below. Then the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The sequence~$(\mathcal R_N)_N$ is relatively compact in~$L^p(\P)$.
\item The same holds for~$\langle f,G_N^Qg\rangle$ with two sources~$f$ and~$g$.
\item For any countable collection of cubes, sources, boundary data and fixed integer shifts~$N\mapsto N+j$ of the cutoff~$N$, a common subsequence can be chosen along which all the corresponding energies converge.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix~$\ell$ and~$N\geq\ell$. The log coefficient on~$\overline Q$ is the sum of the retained potential~$\sum_{-\ell\leq j<0}(\g_{-j}-\g_{-j}(0))+\sum_{0\leq j\leq\ell}\g_{-j}$ and a remaining potential independent of the retained fields. As a function of~$v\in C(\overline Q)$, take the expectation of the energy for the log coefficient~$v$ plus that remaining potential. Independence identifies its value at the retained potential with the conditional expectation of~$\mathcal R_N$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult}, values at~$v,w$ compare by~$e^{\|v-w\|_\infty}$. Choose a fixed radius which bounds the retained potential with positive probability. Integration on this event bounds the value at~$0$ by~$\E\mathcal R_N$ times the exponential of the radius divided by that probability. The functions are therefore uniformly bounded and equicontinuous on every bounded set. A diagonal extraction on a countable dense subset of~$C(\overline Q)$ gives pointwise convergence everywhere. Conditional Jensen gives a uniform~$L^q$ bound, so convergence at the retained random potential is in~$L^p$.

\smallskip

For any~$\varepsilon>0$, choose~$\ell$ so that~$C_Q\gmcdelta3^{-a\ell}<\varepsilon/2$. A finite~$\varepsilon/2$-net of the conditional expectations is an~$\varepsilon$-net of the energies with~$N\geq\ell$. The omitted finite prefix is compact. This proves~(i).

\smallskip

For the constant-flux Neumann response on a general fixed~$Q$, use~$Y_{N,h}$ from the transferred loads in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error}. The source-independent estimate of Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} has Morrey constant at most~$C_{Q,p}K_Nh^{-2}$ for this source, while the smoothed responses have uniformly bounded prescribed moments by the load comparison. Lemma~\ref{mfd:cor-14}, the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15} and the resampling sum therefore give
\begin{equation*}
	\|Y_{N,h}-\E[Y_{N,h}\mid\g_{-j},\ |j|\leq\ell]\|_{L^p}
	\leq C(p,Q)\gmcdelta\bigl(h^{-2}3^{-b\ell/8}+3^{-\ell}\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
The required finite moment orders are fixed before disorder, independently of~$h$. The load approximation and conditional-expectation contraction yield
\begin{equation*}
	\|Y_N-\E[Y_N\mid\g_{-j},\ |j|\leq\ell]\|_{L^p}
	\leq C(p,Q)\bigl(h^{\nf14}
	+\gmcdelta h^{-2}3^{-b\ell/8}+\gmcdelta3^{-\ell}\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
Take~$h=3^{-b\ell/32}/16$. Since~$0<b<1$ and~$0<\gmcdelta\leq1$, this is at most~$C(p,Q)3^{-b\ell/128}$. Thus the finite-band hypothesis holds with~$a=b/128$ and~$C_Q=C(p,Q)/\gmcdelta$, or with any smaller positive~$a$. No bound on~$C_Q$ uniform as~$\gmcdelta\downarrow0$ is asserted here. The retained-potential argument applies to this fixed linear load vanishing on constants. This proves the general fixed-cube Neumann case; for~$p<2$ apply the resampling estimates at order two and downgrade the norm.

\smallskip

For a joint family, choose a common exponent~$a=b/256$. Apply the assumptions to the two diagonal responses with data~$f+g$ and~$f-g$; the chosen countable linear source family contains both. Polarization gives~(ii), and a diagonal extraction over the countable collection gives~(iii). Translation and any fixed cutoff shift are included in that collection. The centered unit-cube estimate of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-16} is used for the normalized cell responses; the general fixed-cube argument here only needs the transferred load approximation.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Subsequential limit forms}
\label{mfd:sec-local-form}

We construct limit forms on~$L^2(Q,dx)$ along a subsequence and show that they are local, compatible under restriction to subcubes, and multiplied by a continuous weight when the coefficient is.

\smallskip

Let~$\mathcal C$ be the set of cubes with rational centers and triadic side lengths. It contains the cubes~$\cu_j$,~$j\geq0$, and all their triadic subcubes. Fix~$1/2<\beta<\alpha<1$,~$d-1<t<d$ and~$0<\eta<2\alpha-1$, with~$\alpha$ so close to~$1$ that~\eqref{eq:mfd-33} of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-affine} holds for some~$\gamma\in(0,1)$ and~$\zeta>0$, and with~$2(1-\alpha)+\eta<\nf1{24}$. Choose a countable~$\mathbb Q$-linear family~$\mathcal D\subset C_c^\infty(\Rd)$ such that, for each cube, the functions of~$\mathcal D$ supported in the cube are dense in~$L^2$ and in~$C_c^\infty$ on that cube. This also gives density in~$C^\alpha_c$ for the~$C^\beta$ norm. For each finite union of closed rational cubes contained in a finite union of open rational cubes, include in~$\mathcal D$ a smooth cutoff function, that is, a function with values in~$[0,1]$ equal to one near the first set and zero outside the second. Also include the smooth functions used to build the cutoff functions~$\chi_{N,r}$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}(iii).

\smallskip

The \textbf{harmonic interpolant} of a function on a mesh is the function equal, on each cell, to the~$A_N$-harmonic extension of the restriction of that function to the cell boundary.

\smallskip

We extract a subsequence jointly for the finite-volume energies for these cubes and data, the solutions, the environment and the random constants in~\eqref{eq:mfd-1}--\eqref{eq:mfd-5}, together with the coercivity constants~$K_{Q,N}$ in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error} for the cubes~$Q\in\mathcal C$. Include as single random variables each constant of~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}, which is valid on all grid cubes at once, the constants of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} and Remark~\ref{mfd:rem-large-cube-growth}, which do not depend on the source, the corresponding constants for~$A_N^0$ on the cells of~$\mathcal C$ (see the discussion after Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary}), and~$3^{-\eta N}\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$, whose moments are bounded by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes}. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-response-compact} gives~$L^p$ convergence of the energies along a subsequence. The moment and H\"older bounds give tightness of all the remaining coordinates.

\smallskip

Apply Prokhorov's theorem and Skorokhod's representation theorem~\citep[Theorems~16.3 and~4.30]{Kallenberg} in the countable product of the Polish coordinate spaces. We obtain a further subsequence and a representation on a probability space where all coordinates converge almost surely. The energy limits retain their joint law with the environment and their measurability with respect to the random fields.

\smallskip

We work on the event of probability one on which all coordinates converge. The constants just included are bounded along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. The bounds~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} hold for all meshes, and the bounds~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} hold for all sources and boundary data. Fix~$Q\in\mathcal C$ and abbreviate~$E_N=E_N^Q$,~$G_N=G_N^Q$.

\subsubsection{The Dirichlet Green operator and the limit form}

\begin{proposition}[Convergence of the Dirichlet Green operators and the limit form]
\label{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}
Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \underline{\emph{Norm convergence}}: the operators~$G_N$ converge in operator norm on~$L^2(Q,dx)$ to a compact, positive, self-adjoint, injective operator~$G$.
\item \underline{\emph{Limit form}}: the form
\begin{equation}
	E(u)=\sup_{f\in L^2(Q)}\bigl\{2\langle f,u\rangle-\langle f,Gf\rangle\bigr\}\,,
	\qquad D(E)=\{u:E(u)<\infty\}=\operatorname{Ran}G^{\nf12}\,,
	\label{eq:mfd-18}
\end{equation}
is a densely defined closed Dirichlet form on~$L^2(Q,dx)$.
\item \underline{\emph{Mosco convergence}}: if~$u_N\rightharpoonup u$ weakly in~$L^2(Q)$, then~$E(u)\leq\liminf E_N(u_N)$ (the liminf inequality). Every~$u\in D(E)$ has a \textbf{recovery sequence}, that is, a sequence~$u_N\in H^1_0(Q)$ with~$u_N\to u$ in~$L^2(Q)$ and~$E_N(u_N)\to E(u)$.
\item \underline{\emph{Density}}: the functions~$Gf$ with~$f\in C_c^\infty(Q)$ are dense in~$D(E)$ for the form norm.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We show that~$G_N$ converges in operator norm. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} and the energy identity,
\begin{equation*}
	\|G_Nf\|_{H^{\nf34}}^2\leq K_NE_N(G_Nf)=K_N\langle f,G_Nf\rangle\leq K_N\|f\|_2\|G_Nf\|_2\,,
\end{equation*}
so~$\|G_N\|_{L^2\to H^{\nf34}}\leq\sup_NK_N<\infty$ along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. By Rellich's theorem the images of the unit ball under all~$G_N$ lie in a common precompact subset of~$L^2$. Given~$\varepsilon>0$, choose a finite~$\varepsilon$-net for these images consisting of elements of~$\mathcal D$, and let~$P$ be the orthogonal projection onto its span. Then~$\|(I-P)G_N\|\leq\varepsilon$. By self-adjointness,~$\|G_N(I-P)\|=\|(I-P)G_N\|\leq\varepsilon$. Hence~$\|G_N-G_M\|\leq\|P(G_N-G_M)P\|+4\varepsilon$. The operator~$PG_NP$ is determined by the finite matrix of the bilinear form~$\langle\cdot,G_N\cdot\rangle$ on the elements of the net. Polarization expresses each entry as a real linear combination of quadratic forms~$\langle h,G_Nh\rangle$ with~$h\in\mathcal D$, which converge by the choice of the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. Hence~$(G_N)$ is Cauchy in operator norm.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We show that~$G$ is injective. For~$w\in H^1_0(Q)$ and~$s\in\mathbb R$, the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-quadratic-response} gives~$\langle f,G_Nf\rangle\geq2s\langle f,w\rangle-s^2E_N(w)$, and optimizing in~$s$ gives~$|\langle f,w\rangle|^2\leq\langle f,G_Nf\rangle E_N(w)$. Let~$Gf=0$ and~$\phi\in\mathcal D\cap C_c^\infty(Q)$, and let~$w_{N,r}$ be the harmonic interpolant of~$\phi$ on the rational mesh of side~$r<\operatorname{dist}(\operatorname{supp}\phi,\partial Q)$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} with~$\|\phi(z+r\,\cdot)\|_{C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)/\mathbb R}\leq Cr\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty$,
\begin{equation*}
	E_N(w_{N,r})=\sum_q\Lambda_{N,q}(\phi|_{\partial q})
	\leq Cr^{-d}\max_q\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)\,r^{d-2}\,r^2\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty^2\,,
\end{equation*}
which is bounded along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation at fixed~$r$, since only finitely many cells of~$\mathcal C$ occur. The maximum principle on each cell gives~$\|w_{N,r}-\phi\|_\infty\leq Cr\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty$. Hence
\begin{equation*}
	|\langle f,\phi\rangle|\leq|\langle f,w_{N,r}\rangle|+Cr\|f\|_1\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty\,,
\end{equation*}
and the first term tends to zero as~$N\to\infty$, since~$\langle f,G_Nf\rangle\to\langle f,Gf\rangle=0$. Letting~$r\downarrow0$ gives~$f\perp C_c^\infty(Q)$, so~$f=0$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} The spectral theorem gives~$E(u)=\|G^{-\nf12}u\|_2^2$ with domain~$\operatorname{Ran}G^{\nf12}$, a dense closed form domain. For every~$\lambda>0$, Step~1 gives
\begin{equation*}
	(G_N^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}=G_N(I+\lambda G_N)^{-1}
	\longrightarrow G(I+\lambda G)^{-1}=(G^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}
\end{equation*}
in operator norm. The resolvent criterion~\citep[Theorem~2.4]{KuwaeShioya2003} therefore gives Mosco convergence on~$L^2(Q,dx)$. Applying the liminf inequality to normal contractions of recovery sequences proves the Markov property of~$E$. Finally,~$E(u,Gf)=\langle u,f\rangle$; if~$u$ is orthogonal in the form norm to~$G\mathcal D$, then~$\langle(I+G)u,f\rangle=0$ for every~$f\in\mathcal D$, so~$u=0$. This proves~(ii)--(iv).
\end{proof}

Since~$\mathcal C$ contains countably many cubes, the proposition gives, along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, a limit form~$E^Q$ on every cube~$Q$ of~$\mathcal C$; we write~$E=E^Q$ when the cube is fixed.

\smallskip

The comparison arguments of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare} need the quadratic forms~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle$ to be bounded above and below with positive probability, uniformly in~$N$ and in the extracted limit. Both bounds follow from moment bounds.

\begin{lemma}[Positive and negative moments of the quadratic forms]
\label{mfd:lem-response-reciprocal}
Fix a cube~$Q$ of side at most one and a nonzero function~$f\in C_c^\infty(Q)$. For every~$p<\infty$, if~$\gmcdelta$ is sufficiently small, then
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_N\E\bigl[\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle^p+\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle^{-p}\bigr]<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
The same bounds hold for every extracted limit, uniformly over the subsequence and over all values of~$\gmcdelta$ in a fixed, sufficiently small interval.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The coercivity bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} gives~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle\leq K_N\|f\|_2^2$. For the negative moments, fix a mesh of side~$r$ and let~$w_{N,r}$ be the harmonic interpolant of~$f$ on it, as in Step~2 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}. The maximum principle gives~$\|w_{N,r}-f\|_\infty\leq Cr\|\nabla f\|_\infty$. Fix~$r$ so small that~$\langle f,w_{N,r}\rangle\geq\frac{1}{2}\|f\|_2^2$ for every~$N$. The energy of~$w_{N,r}$ has a uniformly bounded~$p$th moment by~\eqref{eq:mfd-2}--\eqref{eq:mfd-3} on this fixed finite mesh. Optimize the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-quadratic-response} over the multiples of~$w_{N,r}$ to obtain
\begin{equation*}
	\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle^{-1}\leq\frac{E_N^Q(w_{N,r})}{\langle f,w_{N,r}\rangle^2}
	\leq4\|f\|_2^{-4}E_N^Q(w_{N,r})\,.
\end{equation*}
Fatou's lemma passes both moment bounds to each extracted limit.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Traces and localization}

We will apply the following trace estimate to the energy measures of the harmonic interpolants of cutoff functions (Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}).

\begin{lemma}[Measure trace estimate]
\label{mfd:lem-19}
Let~$t>d-1$ and~$K<\infty$, and let~$\nu$ be a finite positive Borel measure on~$\Rd$ supported in~$\overline Q$ such that~$\nu(B_r(x))\leq Kr^t$ for every~$x\in\overline Q$ and~$0<r\leq1$. Then the map~$v\mapsto v$, from smooth functions on~$\overline Q$ to~$L^2(\nu)$, extends by completion to~$H^{\nf12}(Q)$, and there exists~$C(d,t,Q)<\infty$ such that, for every~$v\in H^{\nf12}(Q)$,
\begin{equation}
	\int|v|^2\,d\nu\leq C(d,t,Q)\bigl(K+\nu(Q)\bigr)\|v\|_{H^{\nf12}(Q)}^2\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-19}
\end{equation}
Moreover, if~$v_N\to v$ in~$L^2(Q)$ and~$\sup_N\|v_N\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}<\infty$, then~$v_N\to v$ in~$H^{\nf12}(Q)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The Bessel kernel of order one is bounded by~$C|z|^{1-d}$ for~$|z|\leq1$ and is bounded outside the unit ball. The assumption~$\nu(B_r(x))\leq Kr^t$ and~$t>d-1$ therefore give
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_{x\in\operatorname{supp}\nu}\int\bigl(|x-y|^{1-d}\vee1\bigr)\,d\nu(y)
	\leq C\left(\nu(\overline Q)+K\sum_{n\geq0}2^{-n(t-d+1)}\right)
	\leq C\bigl(K+\nu(\overline Q)\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
Regard the Bessel potential of order~$\nf12$ as a map from~$L^2(dx)$ to~$L^2(\nu)$. The composition of this map with its adjoint has the Bessel kernel of order one as its kernel. This kernel is bounded by the integrand above. By Schur's test, the squared norm of the map is at most~$C(K+\nu(\overline Q))$. The Bessel-potential description of~$H^{\nf12}(\Rd)$ and a bounded extension operator from the cube give~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} for smooth~$v$.

\smallskip

The boundary of~$Q$ is~$\nu$-null: cover it with~$O(r^{1-d})$ balls. Hence~$\nu(\overline Q)=\nu(Q)$. Density of smooth functions in~$H^{\nf12}(Q)$ gives the asserted completion, and convergence in~$L^2(dx)$ identifies it with the ordinary integral when~$\nu$ has a bounded density with respect to Lebesgue measure. Finally, the same extension operator and Sobolev interpolation give
\begin{equation*}
	\|w\|_{H^{\nf12}(Q)}
	\leq C\|w\|_{L^2(Q)}^{\nf13}\|w\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^{\nf23}\,.
\end{equation*}
Weak compactness first puts~$v$ in~$H^{\nf34}(Q)$; applying this inequality to~$w=v_N-v$ proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}

Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, recovery sequences and solutions with zero Dirichlet boundary values are bounded in~$H^{\nf34}$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-1}, and the energy measures of the cutoff functions of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs} below satisfy~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} with a common constant. Thus~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} controls~$\int(v_N-v)^2\,d\Gamma_N(\chi_N)$ uniformly in~$N$. We use this estimate when localizing recovery sequences in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-locality}.

\subsubsection{Regularity, locality, and restriction to subcubes}

To obtain energy measures for~$E$, we show that~$E$ is regular and strongly local. Both properties are proved by multiplying recovery sequences by cutoff functions. Smooth cutoff functions are not suitable, since~$A_N$ has no upper bound uniform in~$N$; we use instead the harmonic interpolants of cutoff functions, whose energies, constants in the Morrey bounds and H\"older norms are controlled cell by cell by~\eqref{eq:mfd-2}--\eqref{eq:mfd-5}.

\begin{lemma}[Harmonic cutoff functions]
\label{mfd:lem-cutoffs}
Let~$K\subset O$ be finite unions of closed and open rational cubes, respectively, with~$K$ compact and~$\overline O\subset Q$, and let~$\chi_N$ be the harmonic interpolant, on a mesh of~$\mathcal C$, of a smooth cutoff function from~$\mathcal D$, with values in~$[0,1]$, equal to one near~$K$ and compactly supported in~$O$. The mesh is so fine that every cell meeting the set where this function lies strictly between~$0$ and~$1$ is contained in~$O\setminus K$. Then the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The function~$\chi_N$ belongs to~$H^1_0(Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$, satisfies~$0\leq\chi_N\leq1$, equals one on a neighbourhood of~$K$ and zero outside~$O$, and~$\nabla\chi_N=0$ on every cell on which this function is constant.
\item Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, the energy~$E_N(\chi_N)$, the constant in the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} for~$\Gamma_N(\chi_N)$ at all radii, and the~$C^\alpha$ norm of~$\chi_N$ are bounded. In particular, along a subsequence,~$\chi_N$ converges uniformly to a continuous function~$\chi$ that agrees with the original cutoff function on every cell where that function is constant.
\item Let~$r>0$, and let~$\chi_{N,r}$ be the harmonic interpolant on the mesh of side~$r$ of a smooth function~$\Rd\to[0,1]$ that vanishes within distance~$r$ of~$\partial Q$, equals one at distance at least~$3r$ from~$\partial Q$ and has gradient at most~$C/r$ on~$\Rd$. Let~$K_N$ be the constant of~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}. Then~$0\leq\chi_{N,r}\leq1$ and
\begin{equation*}
	E_N(\chi_{N,r})\leq C\bigl(K_N+3^{-\eta N}\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\bigr)r^{-1-\eta}\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On a cell on which the cutoff function is constant the harmonic extension is that constant, by uniqueness, and the maximum principle gives~$0\leq\chi_N\leq1$. Continuity across faces follows from the matching traces, and continuity up to the boundary from the H\"older bound for the cell solutions. At a fixed mesh there are finitely many cells, each with bounded~$\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)$ and bounded constants in the Morrey and H\"older bounds along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. Sum over the cells to obtain~(ii). The Arzel\`a--Ascoli theorem gives uniform convergence.

\smallskip

For~(iii), the maximum principle gives~$0\leq\chi_{N,r}\leq1$ as in~(i). There are~$O(r^{-(d-1)})$ cells on which the function defining~$\chi_{N,r}$ is not constant. On each such cell the rescaled function has~$C^\beta$ norm at most~$C$. If~$r\geq3^{-N}$, then~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} and~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} bound the energy on each such cell by~$C\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)r^{d-2}\leq CK_Nr^{d-2-\eta}$. If~$r<3^{-N}$, the smooth function defining~$\chi_{N,r}$ is itself a competitor in the minimization on each cell, which gives
\begin{equation*}
	E_N(\chi_{N,r})\leq C\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}r^{-1}
	\leq C\bigl(3^{-\eta N}\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\bigr)r^{-1-\eta}\,.
\end{equation*}
Both the factor~$3^{-\eta N}\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$ and the constant of~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} were included in the joint extraction, and their sum bounds the energy for all widths~$r$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Regularity]
\label{mfd:prop-regularity}
The Dirichlet form~$E$ of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse} is regular.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For~$f\in\mathcal D$, the H\"older bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-5} gives~$u_N=G_Nf\to Gf$ uniformly on~$\overline Q$, with zero boundary values. Let~$\chi_{N,r}$ be the cutoff function of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}(iii). Covering the boundary layer~$\{x\in Q:\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial Q)<3r\}$ by~$O(r^{1-d})$ balls and using~\eqref{eq:mfd-4}--\eqref{eq:mfd-5} gives
\begin{align}
	E_N(u_N(1-\chi_{N,r}))
	&\leq 2\Gamma_N(u_N)(\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,\partial Q)<3r\})
	+2\sup_{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,\partial Q)<3r}|u_N|^2E_N(\chi_{N,r})
	\notag \\ &
	\leq C(\omega,f)\bigl(r^{t-d+1}+r^{2\alpha-1-\eta}\bigr)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-20}
\end{align}
At fixed~$r$, pass to a subsequence along which~$\chi_{N,r}\to\chi_r$ uniformly. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-20} and the liminf inequality,~$Gf\chi_r\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ converges to~$Gf$ in the form norm as~$r\downarrow0$. Since the functions~$Gf$ are dense in~$D(E)$ by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}(iv),~$D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ is dense in~$D(E)$ for the form norm.

\smallskip

For density in the uniform norm, take~$\phi\in\mathcal D\cap C_c^\infty(Q)$ and its harmonic interpolants on a mesh of side~$r$. Their bounded energies and H\"older norms give a uniform subsequential limit~$w_r\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$. The cell maximum principle gives~$\|w_r-\phi\|_\infty\leq Cr\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty$. Since these~$\phi$ are dense in~$C_0(Q)$, the space~$D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ is uniformly dense as well.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Strong locality on~$D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$]
\label{mfd:prop-locality}
Let~$u,v\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$, and suppose that~$u$ equals a constant~$c$ on a neighbourhood of~$\operatorname{supp}v$. Then~$E(u,v)=0$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose two cutoff functions from~$\mathcal D$ and let~$\psi_N,\chi_N$ be their harmonic interpolants from Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}, with~$\psi_N=1$ near~$\operatorname{supp}v$,~$\chi_N=1$ near~$\operatorname{supp}\psi_N$, and~$\operatorname{supp}\chi_N\subset\{u=c\}^\circ$. For recovery sequences~$u_N,v_N$ of~$u$ and~$v$, consider the sequences~$c\chi_N+(1-\chi_N)u_N$ and~$\psi_Nv_N$. These are still recovery sequences. Indeed, they converge in~$L^2$ to~$u$ and~$v$, and the only extra gradient terms are controlled by
\begin{equation*}
	\int(u_N-c)^2\,d\Gamma_N(\chi_N)
	+\int v_N^2\,d\Gamma_N(\psi_N)\longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation*}
On the support of~$\nabla\chi_N$ we have~$u=c$, and on that of~$\nabla\psi_N$ we have~$v=0$. Apply the trace estimate~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} together with the~$H^{\nf12}$ convergence of the recovery sequences. Young's inequality gives~$\limsup E_N(c\chi_N+(1-\chi_N)u_N)\leq(1+\varepsilon)E(u)$ for every~$\varepsilon>0$, and likewise for~$\psi_Nv_N$. The liminf inequality of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}(iii) gives equality. Since~$c\chi_N+(1-\chi_N)u_N=c$ near~$\operatorname{supp}\psi_N$, the form~$E_N$ of the two sequences vanishes. Applying the liminf inequality to their sum and difference yields~$E(u\pm v)\leq E(u)+E(v)$, hence~$E(u,v)=0$.
\end{proof}

By regularity and Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-locality}, uniqueness in the Beurling--Deny representation~\citep[Section~3.2]{FOT} makes the jump and killing measures vanish. Thus~$E$ is strongly local, and its energy measures~$\Gamma_E(u,v)$ satisfy the chain rule, Leibniz rule, Cauchy--Schwarz inequality and locality on open sets~\citep[Theorem~3.2.2 and Lemma~3.2.5]{FOT}.

\smallskip

We normalize the energy measure by~$\int\phi\,d\Gamma_E(u)=E(u,u\phi)-\frac{1}{2}E(u^2,\phi)$ for bounded~$u\in D(E)$ and~$\phi\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$, so that the corresponding measure at finite~$N$ is~$\Gamma_N(u)=A_N|\nabla u|^2\,dx$, and~$\Gamma_E(u)(Q)=E(u)$ and~$\Gamma_E(u,v)(Q)=E(u,v)$. The measure~$\Gamma_E(u)$ is supported in~$\operatorname{supp}u$.

\smallskip

\begin{proposition}[Consistency under restriction to subcubes]
\label{mfd:prop-killed-consistency}
Let~$q\subset Q$ be an open cube of~$\mathcal C$, and let~$E^q$ and~$E^Q$ be the limit forms on~$q$ and~$Q$ along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. Then extension by zero to~$Q$ sends~$D(E^q)$ onto the~$E^Q$-closure of~$\{w\in D(E^Q)\cap C_c(Q):\operatorname{supp}w\subset q\}$, and~$E^q(u)$ equals the~$E^Q$-energy of the extension of~$u$ by zero, for every~$u\in D(E^q)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Zero extension of a recovery sequence on~$q$ shows that the~$E^Q$-energy of the extension of~$u$ by zero is at most~$E^q(u)$. Conversely, fix~$w\in D(E^Q)\cap C_c(q)$. Localize a recovery sequence on~$Q$ by a harmonic cutoff function equal to one near~$\operatorname{supp}w$ and supported in~$q$. The trace argument in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-locality} shows that this is still a recovery sequence. The liminf inequality on~$q$ (Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}(iii)) gives~$E^q(w|_q)\leq E^Q(w)$. Hence equality holds on~$D(E^Q)\cap C_c(q)$. Regularity of~$E^q$ (Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-regularity}) and the first inequality extend equality to all of~$D(E^q)$. The resulting isometry has closed image, and this image is exactly the stated closure.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Continuous weights and minimal energies with given boundary values}

For~$\varrho\in C(\overline Q)$ with~$\varrho>0$, let~$E_N^{\varrho}(u)=\int_Q\varrho\,d\Gamma_N(u)$ for~$u\in H^1_0(Q)$, and let~$G_N^{\varrho}$ be its Dirichlet Green operator.

\begin{lemma}[Form order implies measure order]
\label{mfd:lem-sincos}
Let~$E,F$ be regular strongly local Dirichlet forms on~$L^2(Q,dx)$ with~$D(E)=D(F)$, let~$q\subset Q$ be open, and let~$C<\infty$ be such that~$F(w)\leq CE(w)$ for every~$w\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ with~$\operatorname{supp}w\subset q$. Then~$\Gamma_F(u)\leq C\Gamma_E(u)$ on~$q$ for every~$u\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let~$v\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ with~$\operatorname{supp}v\subset q$, and let~$k\geq1$. The functions~$k^{-1}v\sin(ku)$ and~$k^{-1}v\cos(ku)=k^{-1}v(\cos(ku)-1)+k^{-1}v$ belong to~$D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ and they are supported in~$\operatorname{supp}v$. For either form, the chain and Leibniz rules show that the cross terms in the energy measures of these two functions cancel in the sum, so that
\begin{equation*}
	E(k^{-1}v\sin ku)+E(k^{-1}v\cos ku)=\int v^2\,d\Gamma_E(u)+k^{-2}E(v)\,,
\end{equation*}
and likewise for~$F$. Applying~$F\leq CE$ to both functions and letting~$k\to\infty$ gives~$\int v^2\,d\Gamma_F(u)\leq C\int v^2\,d\Gamma_E(u)$. For~$0\leq\phi\in C_c(q)$, choose by regularity a function in~$D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ with values in~$[0,1]$ that equals one on~$\operatorname{supp}\phi$ and is supported in~$q$. Also choose functions in~$D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ converging uniformly to~$\phi^{\nf12}$. Then the products of the latter with the former are admissible and their squares converge uniformly to~$\phi$. Hence~$\int\phi\,d\Gamma_F(u)\leq C\int\phi\,d\Gamma_E(u)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Continuous weights]
\label{mfd:prop-21}
Let~$\varrho\in C(\overline Q)$ be positive. Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, the forms~$E_N^\varrho$ Mosco-converge to a form~$E^\varrho$ such that~$D(E^\varrho)=D(E)$ and
\begin{equation}
	E^{\varrho}(u,v)=\int_Q\varrho\,d\Gamma_E(u,v),\qquad u,v\in D(E)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-21}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The bounds~$(\inf\varrho)E_N\leq E_N^\varrho\leq(\sup\varrho)E_N$ show that the forms~$E_N^\varrho$ satisfy the~$H^{\nf34}$ coercivity bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} up to a constant factor, and that the order reverses for their Green operators:~$(\sup\varrho)^{-1}G_N\leq G_N^\varrho\leq(\inf\varrho)^{-1}G_N$. By the finite-rank compactness argument of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}, every subsequence therefore has a further subsequence along which~$G_N^\varrho$ converges in norm on all cubes of~$\mathcal C$. The reversed order makes each limit injective. The same proposition constructs their Mosco limit~$F$, with~$D(F)=D(E)$ and equivalent form norms. Therefore~$F$ is regular, and~$D(F)\cap C_c(Q)=D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$. The proofs of locality and consistency under restriction to smaller cubes also apply: recovery sequences retain their~$H^{\nf{3}{4}}$ bound, and the energy measures of the cutoff functions are at most~$(\sup\varrho)\Gamma_N(\chi_N)$. Thus~$F$ is strongly local and its restrictions to smaller cubes satisfy~$(\inf_q\varrho)E^q\leq F^q\leq(\sup_q\varrho)E^q$ for~$q\in\mathcal C$. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-sincos}, the same comparison holds for energy measures on~$q$. For~$u\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$, take a finite partition of unity near~$\operatorname{supp}u$ subordinate to cubes on which~$\operatorname{osc}\varrho\leq\varepsilon$. Integrate against this partition of unity to obtain~$|F(u)-\int\varrho\,d\Gamma_E(u)|\leq\varepsilon E(u)$. Let~$\varepsilon\downarrow0$, extend by density in the form norm and polarize. Every subsequential limit form is therefore the form in~\eqref{eq:mfd-21}, which proves convergence of the whole sequence in the Skorokhod representation.
\end{proof}

We also obtain convergence in probability of the weighted Dirichlet Green operators along the subsequence extracted above. Indeed, given a subsequence, we represent the weight, the original energy limit and the preceding coordinates jointly on one probability space. The comparison~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult} absorbs the error in the uniform approximation of the weight. The proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-21} identifies every limit with~\eqref{eq:mfd-21}. The subsequence criterion for convergence in probability then gives the assertion, also for random positive continuous weights and for countable families of them.

\begin{lemma}[Bounded weights and deletion of a field]
\label{mfd:lem-borel-weights}
Let~$g$ be a bounded Borel function on~$Q$. Then~$E^g(u,v)=\int e^g\,d\Gamma_E(u,v)$ defines a regular strongly local Dirichlet form with domain~$D(E)$, and~$\Gamma_{E^g}(u,v)=e^g\,\Gamma_E(u,v)$. Moreover, for every field~$\g_{-j}$ with~$j\geq0$, the form~$e^{-\g_{-j}}E$, given by~$(u,v)\mapsto\int e^{-\g_{-j}}\,d\Gamma_E(u,v)$, is measurable with respect to the remaining fields.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The bounds~$e^{-\|g\|_\infty}E\leq E^g\leq e^{\|g\|_\infty}E$ show that~$E^g$ is closed on~$D(E)$, with a form norm equivalent to that of~$E$, so that~$E^g$ is regular, like~$E$. The chain rule for energy measures gives the Markov property, and the locality of energy measures gives strong locality~\citep[Theorem~3.2.2 and Lemma~3.2.5]{FOT}. The defining identity of the energy measure and the Leibniz rule give the formula for~$\Gamma_{E^g}$.

\smallskip

For the deletion, observe that~$e^{-\g_{-j}}$ is continuous and bounded above and below on each fixed cube. The coefficient~$e^{-\g_{-j}}A_N$ is independent of the deleted field for all sufficiently large~$N$. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-21}, applied to random weights as explained after its proof, identifies the limit of the Dirichlet Green operators of~$e^{-\g_{-j}}A_N$ with the Dirichlet Green operator of~$e^{-\g_{-j}}E$. Hence each quadratic form~$\langle f,G^Qf\rangle$ of this Dirichlet Green operator, with~$f\in\mathcal D$ and~$Q\in\mathcal C$, is a limit in probability of functions of the remaining fields. It is therefore measurable with respect to the remaining fields. These quadratic forms determine the form~$e^{-\g_{-j}}E$.
\end{proof}

Therefore, conditionally on the remaining fields, the forms corresponding to coefficients in which the deleted field is multiplied by the cutoff functions of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15} can be defined by weighting the fixed form~$e^{-\g_{-j}}E$ with the independent restrictions of the deleted field. The first part of the lemma allows these weights to be discontinuous.

\smallskip

\begin{corollary}[Energy-measure convergence]
\label{mfd:cor-energy-measures}
Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, the following statements hold, with weak convergence of finite measures on~$\overline Q$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If~$u_N\in H^1_0(Q)$ is a recovery sequence for~$u\in D(E)$, then~$\Gamma_N(u_N)\rightharpoonup\Gamma_E(u)$.
\item If~$u_N\to u$ in~$L^2$ and~$\sup_NE_N(u_N)<\infty$, then every weak subsequential limit of~$\Gamma_N(u_N)$ is at least~$\Gamma_E(u)$.
\item If~$(u_N)$ is as in~(b) and~$(v_N)$ is a recovery sequence for~$v\in D(E)$, then~$\Gamma_N(u_N,v_N)\rightharpoonup\Gamma_E(u,v)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Let~$0\leq\phi\in C(\overline Q)$. For a recovery sequence, the liminf inequalities of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-21} for the weighted forms with the positive weights~$1\pm\varepsilon\phi$, where~$\varepsilon>0$ and~$\varepsilon\|\phi\|_\infty<1$, together with~$E_N(u_N)\to E(u)$, give both inequalities needed for~(a). For~(b), apply the liminf inequality for the weight~$\phi+\varepsilon$, and let~$\varepsilon\downarrow0$ along a subsequence on which the measures converge weakly.

\smallskip

For~(c), Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the total variations of the mutual energy measures. Pass to a joint subsequential limit of~$\Gamma_N(u_N)$ and~$\Gamma_N(u_N,v_N)$. Apply~(b) to~$u_N+sv_N$ and~(a) to~$v_N$. For every rational~$s$, this shows that the measure
\begin{equation*}
	\lim_N\Gamma_N(u_N)-\Gamma_E(u)
	+2s\bigl(\lim_N\Gamma_N(u_N,v_N)-\Gamma_E(u,v)\bigr)
\end{equation*}
is nonnegative. Testing against~$0\leq\phi\in C(\overline Q)$ and letting~$s\to\pm\infty$ identifies the limit of~$\Gamma_N(u_N,v_N)$ with~$\Gamma_E(u,v)$. Every subsequential limit is therefore the same.
\end{proof}

For the regular strongly local form~$E$, the one-dimensional energy-image-density theorem and the absolutely continuous chain rule~\citep[Proposition~1.16 and its proof]{Fitzsimmons2004} give~$\Gamma_E(v)(\{v=c\})=0$ and~$\Gamma_E(T(v))=T'(v)^2\Gamma_E(v)$ for Lipschitz~$T$ with~$T(0)=0$, using quasi-continuous representatives. The reference normalizes the energy measure as~$2\Gamma_E$, which does not affect either assertion.

\begin{lemma}[Zero-trace differences lie in~$D(E^q)$]
\label{mfd:lem-truncation}
Let~$q\subset Q$ be a cube of~$\mathcal C$, and let~$v\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ vanish on~$\partial q$. Then~$v_q=v\indc_{\overline q}$ belongs to the space~$D(E^q)\subset D(E^Q)$ of functions extended by zero, and~$E^Q(v_q)=\Gamma_E(v)(q)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since~$v=0$ on~$\partial q$, for every~$\epsilon>0$ the set~$\{|v|\geq\epsilon\}\cap\overline q$ is compact in~$q$. Truncate~$v_q$ at height~$\epsilon$, that is, replace it by~$\operatorname{sgn}(v_q)(|v_q|-\epsilon)_+$, and multiply by a cutoff function in~$D(E^Q)\cap C_c(Q)$ equal to one near this set and supported in~$q$. The result belongs to~$D(E^q)$ by consistency under restriction to smaller cubes (Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-consistency}). The chain rule and locality show that its energy measure is the restriction of~$\Gamma_E(v)$ to~$q\cap\{|v|>\epsilon\}$. The energy of the difference of the truncations at two heights is the mass of~$\Gamma_E(v)$ on the part of~$q$ where~$|v|$ lies between these heights. Since the level sets of~$v$ carry no energy, the truncations are Cauchy in the form norm and converge uniformly to~$v_q$. Since~$E^q$ is closed,~$v_q\in D(E^q)$. Letting~$\epsilon\downarrow0$ in the first identity gives~$E(v_q)=\Gamma_E(v)(q)$, with~$\Gamma_E(v_q)(\partial q)=0$.
\end{proof}

We now identify the limits of the minimal energies~$\Lambda_{N,q}$ with given boundary values on cubes of~$\mathcal C$. The candidate is the energy in~$q$ of the limit~$U$ of the harmonic interpolants of a fixed smooth datum on the~$3^d$ cells around~$q$.

\begin{proposition}[Limits of the minimal energies with given boundary values]
\label{mfd:prop-boundary}
Let~$q=z+r\cu_0\in\mathcal C$, let~$Q=z+3r\cu_0$ be the cube formed by the~$3^d$ congruent cells around~$q$, let~$b$ be a boundary datum on~$\partial q$ from~$\mathcal D$, and fix a smooth extension of~$b$ to a function with compact support in~$Q$. Let~$U_N$ be the function equal, on each of the~$3^d$ cells, to the~$A_N$-harmonic extension of the restriction of this extension to the cell boundary. Then, along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation,~$U_N$ converges uniformly on~$\overline Q$ to a function~$U$ with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \underline{\emph{Boundary values}}: the function~$U$ belongs to~$D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$, and~$U=b$ on~$\partial q$.
\item \underline{\emph{No energy on the faces}}: the energy measure of~$U$ satisfies~$\Gamma_E(U)(\partial q)=0$.
\item \underline{\emph{Harmonicity}}: for every~$\phi\in D(E^q)$, extended by zero to~$Q$, we have~$E^Q(U,\phi)=0$.
\item \underline{\emph{Minimality}}: the minimal energies converge, and
\begin{equation*}
	\lim_N\Lambda_{N,q}(b)=\Gamma_E(U)(q)
	=\min\bigl\{\Gamma_E(V)(q):V\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q),\ V=b\text{ on }\partial q\bigr\}\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The energy, H\"older norm and constant in the Morrey bound for~$U_N$ are bounded by summing the corresponding cell bounds. Hence every subsequence has a further subsequence with~$U_N\to U$ uniformly, where~$U\in D(E^Q)$ and~$U$ equals the fixed extension of~$b$ on every cell boundary. We show that~$(U_N)$ is a recovery sequence for~$U$.

\smallskip

Let~$W_N$ be a recovery sequence for~$U$, let~$\Sigma$ be the union of the cell boundaries, and take on each cell the cutoff function of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}(iii) with~$r=\rho$. Their sum~$\chi_{N,\rho}$ vanishes near~$\Sigma$ and equals one away from its~$3\rho$-neighborhood. Use~$\chi_{N,\rho}W_N+(1-\chi_{N,\rho})U_N$, which has the same cell-boundary values as~$U_N$, as a competitor. Cellwise minimality and convexity give, for~$\varepsilon>0$,
\begin{align*}
	E_N(U_N)
	&\leq(1+\varepsilon)\bigl[E_N(W_N)
	+\Gamma_N(U_N)(\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,\Sigma)<3\rho\})\bigr]
	\notag \\ & \qquad
	+(1+\varepsilon^{-1})
	\int(W_N-U_N)^2\,d\Gamma_N(\chi_{N,\rho})\,.
\end{align*}
At fixed~$\rho$, the last term tends to zero by~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} and convergence in~$H^{\nf12}$. The term~$\Gamma_N(U_N)(\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,\Sigma)<3\rho\})$ is at most~$C\rho^{t-d+1}$ by the Morrey bound at every radius and a covering of the finitely many faces. Let first~$N\to\infty$, then~$\rho\downarrow0$ and~$\varepsilon\downarrow0$. The liminf inequality gives~$E_N(U_N)\to E(U)$.

\smallskip

Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-energy-measures}(a) now gives~$\Gamma_N(U_N)\rightharpoonup\Gamma_E(U)$. The common Morrey bound passes to the limit. Hence~$\Gamma_E(U)(\Sigma)=0$ and~$\Lambda_{N,q}(b)=\Gamma_N(U_N)(q)\to\Gamma_E(U)(q)$. For~$\phi\in D(E^q)$, a recovery sequence on~$q$, extended by zero, is also a recovery sequence on~$Q$ by consistency under restriction to smaller cubes (Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-consistency}). The identity~$E_N(U_N,\phi_N)=0$ at finite~$N$ passes to~$E(U,\phi)=0$ by Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-energy-measures}(c).

\smallskip

If~$V=b$ on~$\partial q$, then by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-truncation} the function~$w=(V-U)\indc_{\overline q}$ lies in~$D(E^q)$. Its energy measure does not charge~$\partial q$. Harmonicity therefore yields~$\Gamma_E(V)(q)=\Gamma_E(U)(q)+E(w)\geq\Gamma_E(U)(q)$. The same argument on each cell shows that the difference of two possible limits has zero energy on every cell. Since the energy measures do not charge the faces, coercivity forces this difference to vanish on~$Q$. Thus the uniform convergence and all the conclusions hold along the full sequence in the Skorokhod representation. The argument applies to every fixed finite partition into cubes with a common smooth datum vanishing on the outer boundary.
\end{proof}

We will also use this boundary identification after removing the infrared factor. On the same realization on~$Q$, put~$\varrho=e^{-H}$ and let~$E^{\varrho,Q}$ be the form obtained from the same padded Dirichlet Green limit by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-21}. Its energy measure is~$\Gamma_{E^\varrho}=\varrho\Gamma_E$, its domain is~$D(E^Q)$, and its form norm is equivalent to that of~$E^Q$. Thus regularity and consistency under restriction to subcubes are preserved.

\smallskip

We give the cell bounds for~$A_N^0$ also on cells larger than one. The unit-scale bounds follow from the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} with the infrared partial sum equal to zero. On a fixed larger cell, join the harmonic extensions of the smooth datum on a finite mesh of cubes of side at most one. Their energy bounds control the energy of this competitor and hence the total energy of the cell minimizer. For a bounded volume source, the same bound follows from the quadratic response identity and the fixed-cube coercivity in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error}.

\smallskip

For a cell~$q=z+\cu_j$,~$j\geq0$, let~$f\in L^\infty(q)$ and~$\varphi\in C^2(\overline q)$, and let~$u_N\in H^1(q)$ solve~$-\nabla\cdot(A_N^0\nabla u_N)=f$ in~$q$ with~$u_N=\varphi$ on~$\partial q$. Use the coordinates~$y=3^Nx$ and the relabelled fields of Step~1 of the growth proof. The whole cell becomes~$3^Nz+\cu_{N+j}$, and we have~$A_N^0(3^{-N}y)=\ahom_N^{-1}\a_N(y)$ and~$\b_{N,N+j}=\ahom_N$. Apply Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity} with cutoff~$L=N$, outer scale~$m=N+j$ and the exponent~$\alpha_M$ chosen in the growth proof. Its energy and oscillation estimates apply on the whole cell, including windows meeting its faces. The coefficient~$\ahom_N$ cancels with the scalar~$\ahom_N^{-1}$ when returning to the original energy. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-primitive} supplies the vector source when a volume source is present. Steps~2--3 of the growth proof therefore give, for every~$x\in q$, with~$\max\{t,d-2+2\alpha\}<t_1<d-2+2\alpha_M$,
\begin{equation}
	\Gamma_N^0(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap q)
	\leq K_{N,q}\bigl(\|f\|_\infty+\|\varphi\|_{C^2}\bigr)^2r^{t_1}\,,
	\qquad 3^{-N}\leq r\leq1\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-zero-large-cell-growth}
\end{equation}
Here~$\Gamma_N^0(u)=A_N^0|\nabla u|^2\,dx$. The total energy bound treats radii beyond the regularity range, as in Step~3. The tail~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.minimal.scale} is uniform in~$N$,~$j$ and the deterministic translation; choose the moment orders for the total energy and~$3^{t_1\mathcal L_N(\alpha_M,N+j)}$ before decreasing~$\gmcdelta$, so that~\eqref{eq:mfd-tail-rate} holds at these orders. This bounds the listed moments of~$K_{N,q}$ uniformly in~$N$.

\smallskip

At radii below~$3^{-N}$, retain Steps~4--5 of the growth proof, with~$H=0$. Combine the small-oscillation estimate of those steps with~\eqref{eq:mfd-zero-large-cell-growth}; the positive powers~$3^{-(t_1-t)N}$,~$3^{-(d+2-t)N}$ and~$3^{-(d-t)N}$ absorb the coefficient and log-gradient moments at the finitely many enlarged orders. The odd reflections give the same bound at faces, edges and corners. Step~7, using the whole-cell oscillation estimate above, gives the~$C^\alpha$ bound. Together, these steps give the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} for~$\Gamma_N^0$ at every radius~$r\leq1$ and the H\"older bounds of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth}(ii) for~$A_N^0$ on~$q$, with one random constant~$K_{N,q}$ that does not depend on~$f$ and~$\varphi$ and whose moments of the listed orders are bounded uniformly in~$N$. This is the whole-cell argument used in Step~3 of Lemma~\ref{tight:lem-static}, with~$\alpha_M$ in place of~$7/8$. Stationarity makes the moment constants uniform over deterministic translations of a fixed cell.

\smallskip

Include these finitely many cell constants in the same joint compactness extraction. The cutoff energy measures are bounded by~$(\sup_Q\varrho)\Gamma_N(\chi_{N,\rho})$. The same comparison with a recovery sequence for~$E^{\varrho,Q}$ makes the weighted harmonic interpolants a recovery sequence: the face-layer error is~$C\rho^{t-d+1}$, and the cutoff term vanishes by~\eqref{eq:mfd-19}. The weighted energy-measure convergence then gives zero energy on the faces, harmonicity and minimality. In particular,
\begin{equation}
	\Lambda_{N,q}^0(b)\longrightarrow
	\min\bigl\{\Gamma_{E^\varrho}(V)(q):
	V\in D(E^{\varrho,Q})\cap C(\overline Q),\ V=b\text{ on }\partial q\bigr\}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-weighted-boundary-minimum}
\end{equation}
This identifies the boundary minimum of the weighted form on the same padded cube as the operator limit. For a random~$H$, include it in the joint representation and use~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult} to absorb its uniform approximation. The subsequence criterion then gives convergence in probability on the original field space, after uniqueness. This applies in particular to the uses of~$e^{-H}$ below.

\begin{proposition}[H\"older boundary data, gluing, harmonic replacement]
\label{mfd:prop-gluing}
Let~$\beta$ and~$\alpha$ be as fixed in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, so that~$1/2<\beta<\alpha<1$, and set~$\sigma=\frac12(\beta-\nf12)$. Then the following statements hold along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item Let~$q=z+r\cu_0$ and~$Q=z+3r\cu_0$ be as in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary}. For every~$b\in C^\alpha(\partial q)$, the limit~$\Lambda_{E,q}(b)=\lim_N\Lambda_{N,q}(b)$ exists and satisfies
\begin{equation*}
	\Lambda_{E,q}(b)\leq C\bigl(\sup_N\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)\bigr)r^{d-2}\|b(z+r\,\cdot)\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}^2\,,
\end{equation*}
and all conclusions of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary} hold for~$b$, with a harmonic extension~$U\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$.
\item Let~$Q$ be a cube of~$\mathcal C$, and let~$q_1,\dots,q_m\in\mathcal C$ be disjoint cells such that the closure of each~$q_i$ and the concentric cube of three times its side length lie in~$Q$. Let~$V\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ satisfy~$V|_{\partial q_i}\in C^\alpha$ for every~$i$. Let~$V'$ equal~$V$ outside~$\bigcup_iq_i$ and equal, on each~$q_i$, the harmonic extension~$U_i$ of~$V|_{\partial q_i}$. Then~$V'\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ and
\begin{equation*}
	E^Q(V')=E^Q(V)-\sum_i\bigl[\Gamma_E(V)(q_i)-\Lambda_{E,q_i}(V|_{\partial q_i})\bigr]\leq E^Q(V)\,.
\end{equation*}
\item If moreover~$\Gamma_E(V)$ charges no face~$\partial q_i$ (for instance, if~$V$ is itself a limit of functions that, at finite~$N$, are harmonic in each cell for the cutoff coefficient and share a common smooth datum on the union of the cell boundaries), and the cells~$q_i$ partition a cube~$Q'\subset Q$, then~$\Gamma_E(V')(Q')=\sum_i\Lambda_{E,q_i}(V|_{\partial q_i})$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Extend~$b$ to a function in~$C_c^\alpha(Q)$ and approximate it in~$C^\beta$ by functions in~$\mathcal D$. On the fixed finite partition, apply the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary data and the maximum principle to two approximations. The bounds show that the energy~$E_N$ of the difference of the corresponding interpolants is at most~$C$ times the squared~$C^\beta$ norm of the difference of the approximations. The sup norm of the difference of the interpolants is at most the sup norm of the difference of the approximations. The interpolants converge by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary}, and the same bounds hold for their limits by the liminf inequality. Hence the corresponding harmonic extensions converge in energy and uniformly to a limit~$U$ with boundary values~$b$. Harmonicity passes to the limit by energy convergence. The energy on the faces also remains zero, since energy measures depend continuously in total variation on the function in the form norm. Minimality follows again from harmonicity and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-truncation}. Since~$\sqrt{\Lambda_{N,q}}$ is a seminorm of the datum, its reverse triangle inequality and~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} transfer the convergence of~$\Lambda_{N,q}$ and the asserted bound from the approximations to~$b$. The argument holds for each realization and covers random boundary data.

\smallskip

(ii) Consistency under restriction to subcubes extends each~$U_i$ to~$D(E^Q)$, and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-truncation} puts~$(U_i-V)\indc_{\overline{q_i}}$ in~$D(E^{q_i})$ with zero energy on~$\partial q_i$. Adding these differences to~$V$ shows that~$V'$ belongs to the form domain. Locality identifies its energy measure with~$\Gamma_E(U_i)$ inside~$q_i$ and with~$\Gamma_E(V)$ elsewhere, including the faces by Cauchy--Schwarz. Integrating gives the identity. Cell minimality gives the inequality.

\smallskip

(iii) The same identification and the assumption that~$\Gamma_E(V)$ charges no face give the sum over the partition. For a common smooth datum, the energy measure of the limit does not charge the faces by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary}; for a common~$C^\alpha$ datum, this follows from the approximation in energy in~(i).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite partitions with prescribed boundary values]
\label{mfd:lem-skeleton}
Let~$\alpha$ be as fixed in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, let the cubes~$q_i$ form a finite partition of a cube~$Q$, and let~$b\in C^\alpha(\overline Q)$ vanish on~$\partial Q$. Let~$v$ be the function equal, on each cell~$q_i$, to the limiting harmonic extension of~$b|_{\partial q_i}$. Then~$v\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$,
\begin{equation*}
	E^Q(v)\leq\sum_i\Lambda_{E,q_i}(b|_{\partial q_i})\,,
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
	\|v-b\|_\infty\leq\sup_i\operatorname{osc}_{q_i}b\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For smooth data compactly supported in~$Q$, apply the last paragraph of the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary} to this finite partition. The harmonic interpolants converge uniformly and in energy, and their cell energies add. For general~$b$, use~$\beta$ as fixed in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, with~$\beta<\alpha$. The zero extension of~$b$ can be approximated in~$C^\beta$ by~$C_c^\infty(Q)$ functions. The bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary data and the maximum principle, as in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-gluing}(i), give convergence in energy and uniformly. This proves the energy bound, also for cells touching~$\partial Q$. The cell maximum principle gives~$\|v-b\|_\infty\leq\sup_i\operatorname{osc}_{q_i}b$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Estimates along chains of nested cubes}
\label{mfd:sec-branches}

The energy measure~$\Gamma_E(u)$ depends on the same fields as the regularity, extension and harmonic-approximation estimates of Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-deterministic}. To control the energy in the cells where these estimates fail, Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} bounds, on a single event that does not depend on~$u$, the number of bad cells, defined below, along every chain of nested cubes. This bound can then be integrated against the energy measure.

\smallskip

Let~$E$ be a subsequential limit along the subsequence of cutoffs of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, realized on the Skorokhod probability space.

\subsubsection{Continuous versions of the conditions}

Fix~$s\in(0,\nf1{32}]$ before choosing~$\gmcdelta$; the harmonic lemma is applied with parameter~$8s\in(0,\nf14]$. Let~$\mathsf F_{m,z}$,~$\mathsf P_{m,z}$ and~$\mathsf R_{m,z}$ be the left sides of~\eqref{e.good.gj},~\eqref{e.good.discounted.am.vs.aj} and~\eqref{e.good.sum.J}, translated to~$z$. Here~$\mathsf P_{m,z}$ is a scalar statistic. For~$\gmcdelta=0$, they equal~$0$,~$2$ and~$0$, respectively. We use the quantity~$\mathbf G_{m,z}(s)$ of~\eqref{e.def.bfG.mhq}, suppressing its fixed argument~$s$, and the quantity~$\mathcal J_n(P)$ of~\eqref{e.normalized.J.maximum.definition}.

\smallskip

To measure failure of the bounds at center~$z$ and scale~$m$, apply continuous piecewise linear functions to~$\mathsf F_{m,z}$,~$\mathsf P_{m,z}$ and~$\mathsf R_{m,z}$, and let~$Z_{m,z}$ be their sum. Each function increases linearly from zero to one between its lower and upper thresholds, is zero below the lower threshold and one above the upper threshold, and lies between the indicators of exceeding these thresholds. The threshold pairs are~$(\epsilon/2,\epsilon)$,~$(6,12)$ and~$(\epsilon^2/4,\epsilon^2)$, respectively. Thus~$Z_{m,z}$ takes values in~$[0,3]$; it is a continuous version of the number of the three conditions~\eqref{e.good.gj},~\eqref{e.good.discounted.am.vs.aj} and~\eqref{e.good.sum.J} that fail at center~$z$ and scale~$m$. The probability bounds of Lemmas~\ref{l.bad.scales.for.nabla.gj},~\ref{l.exp.goodscales} and~\ref{l.discounted.J.local} are applied at the lower thresholds. We call these quantities and~$\mathbf G$ the primitive scores. Every response entering~$\mathsf R$ or~$\mathbf G$ at stationary scale~$n$ is computed for~$\ahom_n^{-1}\a_n$, hence for~$A_n^0$ after translation and rescaling to the unit cube. This own cutoff is part of their definition. The normalized quantities on a physical cell, defined next, are different quantities. Where~$Z_{m,z}<1$, each quantity lies below its upper threshold. Apply the deterministic good-scale estimates at these upper thresholds; in particular, they use the threshold~$12$ for~$\mathsf P_{m,z}$ in place of~$6$. Only the constants change, provided~$\tau^2\leq s\log3/16$: the product bound~$12$ gives~$e^{\|\g_{m,n}\|_\infty}\leq12\cdot3^{s(m-n)/8}$, while~\eqref{e.rho.m.n.bound} gives~$e^{|\rho_{m,n}|}\leq3^{s(m-n)/16}$. The squared coefficient ratio in~\eqref{e.combined.coefficient.ratio.reg}, multiplied by the bound~\eqref{e.good.sum.J} and its weight, therefore still decays like~$3^{-s(m-n)}$.

\subsubsection{The good event of a cell}

We specify the quantities on a cell after dividing out its coefficient scale. Use~$\beta$ fixed in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form} and set~$\sigma=\nf12(\beta-\nf12)$ as in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}. The exponent pairs used below are~$(\sigma/2,2)$ for boundary extension and~$(1,1)$ for the mean-zero Poincar\'e bound; the homogenization error uses~$(s,\infty,2)$. These exponents are fixed before the moment orders and the disorder threshold.

\smallskip

For a cube~$U=w+3^{-\ell}\cu_0$ with integer~$\ell\leq N$, including the negative depths of fixed enlargements, put
\begin{equation*}
	\kappa_j=e^{(j+1)\tau^2}\ahom_j,\qquad
	s_N(U)=\frac{\kappa_{N-\ell}}{\kappa_N}
	\exp\left(H(w)+
	\begin{cases}
		\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\g_{-j}(w),&\ell\geq0\,,\\
		\displaystyle-\sum_{j=\ell}^{-1}\g_{-j}(w),&\ell<0\,.
	\end{cases}
	\right)\,.
\end{equation*}
For~$\ell\geq0$ this is exactly the scalar of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations}. The negative-depth formula removes the finitely many retained coarse layers. In the unit chart of~$U$, let~$\widetilde A_{N,U}(y)=s_N(U)^{-1}A_N(w+3^{-\ell}y)$ and define
\begin{equation*}
	L_N(U)=\frac{\lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(U;A_N)}{s_N(U)},\qquad
	U_N(U)=\frac{\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(U;A_N)}{s_N(U)},\qquad
	V_N(U)=\frac{s_N(U)}{\lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(U;A_N)}\,.
\end{equation*}
Also retain the matrices~$s_N(U)^{-1}\a(U;A_N)$ and~$s_N(U)\a_*^{-1}(U;A_N)$, entry by entry. For scalar comparisons include every cube~$U$ of the catalogue fixed below, in particular~$q$, its concentric enlargement~$3q$, and the chosen harmonic-comparison cube, among the cubes~$W$. For each such~$W$ retain~$\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}(\cu_0,0;\widetilde A_{N,W},I)$ and~$s_N(q)/s_N(W)$. The ellipticity coordinates have value~$1$, the matrices have value~$I$, the error has value~$0$ and the ratio has value~$1$ when~$\gmcdelta=0$. The finite tests use these normalized coordinates and their deviations from these values. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations}, for~$\ell\geq0$ the coefficient~$\widetilde A_{N,U}$ is the anchored coefficient~$A_{N-\ell}$ in the translated and rescaled environment; it includes the centered increments of the fields coarser than~$U$. The signed formula gives the same identification for negative~$\ell$. In particular, its matrices are not the own-cutoff matrices of~$A_{N-\ell}^0$. The estimates for the primitive scores and for these normalized arrays are proved separately below.

\smallskip

The pair~$(1,1)$ requires no additional random test. Weighted Cauchy--Schwarz in~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.ellipticity} gives, for every cube and coefficient,
\begin{equation*}
	\Lambda_{1,1}\leq C_\sigma\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2},\qquad
	\lambda_{1,1}^{-1}\leq C_\sigma\lambda_{\sigma/2,2}^{-1}\,,
	\qquad
	C_\sigma=\frac{(1-3^{-1})^2}
	{(1-3^{-2+\sigma})(1-3^{-\sigma})}\,.
\end{equation*}
Indeed, apply Cauchy--Schwarz to~$\sum_{j\geq0}3^{-j}M_j^{\nf12} =\sum_{j\geq0}3^{-(1-\sigma/2)j}(3^{-\sigma j}M_j)^{\nf12}$, where~$M_j$ is the maximum matrix norm at relative depth~$j$ in either definition. This supplies both required exponent pairs on every enlargement from the same normalized ellipticity arrays.

\smallskip

Fix a rational cube~$Q$ and its triadic subdivision. For~$q=z+3^{-k}\cu_0$, use a fixed finite catalogue of concentric enlargements and the cubes of finitely many translated triadic grids needed for the harmonic comparison. A catalogue cube~$U$ has depth~$\ell_U=k-i$ for a fixed integer offset~$i$ and center~$z+3^{-\ell_U}v$ for a fixed grid shift~$v$. Include~$q$ and its concentric enlargement of side~$3\cdot3^{-k}$. Choose the finite grids so that every centered subcube of~$q$ is contained in a catalogue descendant of side at most nine times its own. At descendant depth~$D$, use all descendant centers and the finitely many catalogue centers needed for the scalar comparisons. Their number is at most~$C_d3^{dD}$.

\smallskip

Fix integers~$k_1\geq0$ and~$k_0\geq\max\{k_1,1\}$. At each observation center~$w$, the prefix is the sum over the physical depths~$j=\ell_U-k_1,\ldots,\ell_U+D$, that is, over the depths of~$U$ and its descendants down to relative depth~$D$, together with the~$k_1$ coarser depths~$\ell_U-k_1,\ldots,\ell_U-1$. The prefix conditions are imposed only for~$D\geq k_0$, so the prefix has at most~$D+k_1+1\leq3D$ terms. Its summands are the primitive scores~$Z_{N-j,\,3^Nw}$ and~$\mathbf G_{N-j,\,3^Nw}$ in the stationary coordinates of~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization}. Use this prefix when~$\ell_U+D\leq N$, so that every index~$N-j$ is nonnegative; otherwise put it equal to zero and use the below-cutoff estimates.

\smallskip

Choose fixed numbers
\begin{equation*}
	0<\lambda_{\rm cut}<\lambda_{\rm lim}/4<\lambda_{\rm lim}<\lambda_{\rm det}<1\,,
	\qquad 0<\vartheta<1,\qquad
	\epsilon_{\rm hom}>0,\qquad c_{\rm det}>0\,.
\end{equation*}
Fix the primitive tolerance~$\epsilon\in(0,1)$ sufficiently small for the deterministic estimates at the chosen regularity exponents, and then choose~$\lambda_{\rm det}$ sufficiently small for those estimates, and~$\lambda_{\rm lim}$ and~$\lambda_{\rm cut}$ satisfying the displayed inequalities. In particular, after reducing~$\gmcdelta$, the quantity~$C(\lambda_{\rm det}+\gmcdelta^2+\epsilon^8)$ is smaller than the gap between the iteration exponent and~$\alpha$. Choose~$c_{\rm det}$ small enough that the harmonic-comparison hypothesis holds even at error~$2\epsilon_{\rm hom}c_{\rm det}$; this leaves room for passage from the limiting error to a finite cutoff. The finite-cutoff prefix threshold is~$\lambda_{\rm cut}D$; the limiting prefix threshold is~$\lambda_{\rm lim}D$, strictly below~$\lambda_{\rm det}D$ consumed by that iteration. The \textbf{good event}~$G_E(q)$ is defined using the limiting arrays of these exact finite-cutoff quantities along the subsequence defining~$E$. It requires, for every catalogue cube~$U$, every~$D\geq k_0$ and every observation center, both limiting prefixes to be smaller than~$\lambda_{\rm lim}D$, and the following finite tests:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),leftmargin=*]
\item On every catalogue enlargement,~$\vartheta\leq L_E(U)$ and~$U_E(U)\leq\vartheta^{-1}$. Consequently, the normalized~$(1,1)$ lower and upper bounds are~$\vartheta/C_\sigma$ and~$C_\sigma\vartheta^{-1}$. For~$\mathcal A_E(U)=\lim_Ns_N(U)^{-1}\a(U;A_N)$ these bounds also give
\begin{equation*}
	\frac{\vartheta^2}{d}\operatorname{tr}\mathcal A_E(U)\,|p|^2
	\leq p\cdot\mathcal A_E(U)p\qquad(p\in\Rd)\,.
\end{equation*}
\item On the chosen comparison cube~$W$, the limiting error obeys
\begin{equation*}
	\lim_N\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}(\cu_0,0;\widetilde A_{N,W},I)
	\leq\epsilon_{\rm hom}c_{\rm det}\,.
\end{equation*}
\item For every comparison cube~$W$ the limiting ratio~$s_N(q)/s_N(W)$ belongs to~$(\nf12,2)$, strictly inside the closed interval consumed by the deterministic comparison.
\end{enumerate}
At a finite cutoff, write~$G_N^{\rm det}(q)$ for the conditions consumed by the deterministic estimates: prefix threshold~$\lambda_{\rm det}D$, ellipticity bounds~$\vartheta/2$ and~$2\vartheta^{-1}$, error bound~$2\epsilon_{\rm hom}c_{\rm det}$ and closed ratio interval~$[\nf12,2]$. When the cutoff~$N$ is understood, we also write~$G(q)=G_N^{\rm det}(q)$. Each fixed finite set of these conditions follows eventually from~$G_E(q)$ under almost-sure convergence of the arrays. The smaller prefix threshold~$\lambda_{\rm cut}D$ is used to prove the probability bound passed to the limit at the threshold~$\lambda_{\rm lim}D/4$; eventual satisfaction of the threshold~$\lambda_{\rm cut}D$ is not needed. The finite collection is chosen before~$N$,~$k$, the source and the environment. Its ellipticity and matrix conditions are used for extension and comparison on a good cell. The regularity iteration itself uses only the primitive prefixes, together with its reference-ratio estimates; it does not require transfer of the normalized tests~(a)--(c).

\smallskip

For continuous tests in~(a)--(c), use piecewise linear ramps with the following inner and outer thresholds. For the lower ellipticity coordinate they are~$(1+\vartheta)/2$ and~$\vartheta$, with the ramp increasing as the coordinate decreases; for the upper coordinate they are~$(1+\vartheta^{-1})/2$ and~$\vartheta^{-1}$; for the error they are~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}c_{\rm det}/2$ and~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}c_{\rm det}$. For the lower ratio test they are~$3/4$ and~$1/2$, and for the upper ratio test~$3/2$ and~$2$. The outer thresholds are the bounds defining~$G_E(q)$; the inner thresholds place the zero-disorder coordinates in the regions where their respective ramps vanish. The deterministic finite-cutoff bounds are weaker, as specified above. Each ramp is zero at that value and one when the corresponding outer condition fails. Finite sums of these ramps, or of absolute values of the centered normalized coordinates, are fixed nonnegative Lipschitz functions of the finite array. Their constants depend only on the displayed thresholds and catalogue.

\begin{lemma}[Deterministic consequences of the good event]
\label{mfd:in-deterministic}
Fix~$N$ and a cell~$q$ in the deterministic event~$G_N^{\rm det}(q)$. Every weak solution satisfies the following statements.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item The local H\"older estimate with source holds at exponent~$\alpha$, with a fixed deterministic constant. More precisely, if the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} is run with a fixed exponent in~$(\alpha,1)$, then the constant in its conclusion after~$D$ scales is at most~$3^{Ck_0+C(\lambda_{\rm det}+\gmcdelta^2+\epsilon^8)D}$, where~$C(\lambda_{\rm det}+\gmcdelta^2+\epsilon^8)$ is smaller than the difference between this exponent and~$\alpha$.
\item The bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values holds on~$q$ with the factor~$\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}, which is comparable to the finite-cutoff scalar~$s_N(q)$ specified above; this scalar is the factor by which the coefficient on~$q$, rescaled to unit size, is a multiple of a copy of~$A^0_{N-k}$, up to the oscillation factor of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity}.
\item The interior harmonic comparison of Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates} holds with tolerance~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ and the source term normalized by~$s_N(W)$ on its comparison cube~$W$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On~$G_N^{\rm det}(q)$, we apply the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} with the bad set consisting of the~$k_1$ additional indices and the indices of the scales at which one of the conditions fails. This set has at most~$2k_0+\lambda_{\rm det}D$ elements. By~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.E} the exponent in the constant of the conclusion is at most~$C(\gmcdelta^2+\epsilon^8+\lambda_{\rm det})D+Ck_0$. This gives~(i). Condition~(a) gives both the~$(\sigma/2,2)$ bound for extension and, by its weighted Cauchy--Schwarz comparison, the~$(1,1)$ bound for mean-zero Poincar\'e. In particular,~$\operatorname{tr}\mathcal A_N\leq dU_N$ and~$p\cdot\mathcal A_Np\geq L_N|p|^2$ give trace coercivity with constant~$\vartheta^2/(4d)$ at a finite cutoff, and~$\vartheta^2/d$ in the limit; here~$\mathcal A_N=s_N^{-1}\a(U;A_N)$. Condition~(a), the estimate~\eqref{e.cg.RHS} and the trace extension constructed in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}, with~$0<\sigma<\beta-\nf12$, give~(ii). To obtain~(iii), use the deterministic comparison argument in the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates}, with comparison coefficient~$s_N(W)I$. On the unit chart, divide both coefficient and source by~$s_N(W)$; the comparison coefficient is then~$I$. Use regularity parameter~$8s\leq\nf14$, so that the error index is~$s$. The proof after the anchor error estimate uses only that error, positive scalar comparison and the ellipticity bounds, and applies to this scalar as well. Condition~(b) and the choice of~$c_{\rm det}$ give the tolerance~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$; scaling back gives the source factor~$r^2s_N(W)^{-1}$. Condition~(c) replaces~$s_N(W)$ by~$s_N(q)$ up to a fixed factor when required. These bounds depend only on the coefficient and the deterministic geometry, so the same bounds apply to every weak solution.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Passage to the limit in sums over finitely many scales]
\label{mfd:lem-prefix-limit}
Along the subsequence that defines~$E$, the following statements hold for each fixed finite interval of scales.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item The quantities~$Z_{m,z}$ and~$\mathbf G_{m,z}$ and each of the finitely many coarse-grained quantities in (a)--(c) converge in~$L^p$, for each of the finitely many orders~$p$ fixed at the start of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness}. The moment bounds are uniform in the deterministic position of the interval of scales.
\item Let~$A,D>0$. If, at every cutoff, the sum of~$Z_{m,z}$ or of~$\mathbf G_{m,z}$ over the interval of scales exceeds a given threshold with probability at most~$e^{-AD}$, then the limit of this sum exceeds any larger threshold with probability at most~$e^{-AD}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We first prove~(i). For each requested~$p$, fix an auxiliary order~$P\geq2p$ with~$\nf dP$ below the margin of the geometric weights, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that~$C_P\gmcdelta^2$ is below the remaining margin, where~$C_P$ is the constant in the bound~$e^{C_P\gmcdelta^2j}$ on the~$L^P$ norm of~$\mathcal J$ obtained below. Split the terms that involve only the fields into finite sums at finitely many depths and geometrically weighted tails. By~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity} the tails are bounded in~$L^p$ by their geometric weights times the moments of the fields. The other parts are geometrically weighted maxima of~$J$. For the~$\mathcal J$-terms of~$\mathsf R$ and~$\mathbf G$, the cube scale is the stationary cutoff of its coefficient~$\ahom_m^{-1}\a_m$. Translation and rescaling to~$\cu_0$ give the coefficient~$A_m^0$ in its own reindexed environment, with no remaining cell scalar or normalization ratio. On~$\cu_0$ the exact decomposition is
\begin{equation*}
	J(\cu_0,e,e;B)
	=\nf12\Lambda_{\cu_0;B}(e\cdot x)
	+\nf12\,e\cdot\a_*^{-1}(\cu_0;B)e-|e|^2\,.
\end{equation*}
Both the affine Dirichlet and constant-flux Neumann responses occur. Include these own-cutoff, translated normalized responses in the countable extraction of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}. For the finite coordinates in~(a)--(c), include their actual normalized matrices, scalar ratios and geometrically weighted descendant arrays in that extraction as well.

\smallskip

We truncate the geometrically weighted sums at a large relative depth. The response extraction gives convergence in measure of the finitely many retained quantities; the uniform bounds at the larger fixed moment orders upgrade this to convergence in~$L^p$. At a relative depth~$j$ beyond the truncation there are at most~$C3^{dj}$ cells. By Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound} and the bound~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering.ratio} on the ratios~$\ahom_m\ahom_n^{-1}$, the quantity~$\mathcal J$ of each cell has~$L^P$ norm at most of order~$e^{C_P\gmcdelta^2j}$, and taking a spatial maximum at the auxiliary order~$P$ introduces a factor~$3^{\nf{dj}{P}}$. The tail is then exponentially small in the truncation depth, uniformly in the cutoff.

\smallskip

Below the smallest field scale~$3^{-N}$, the maximum of the coefficient on a cell whose side equals that scale controls every smaller descendant by the direct bounds~\eqref{e.direct.J.bound.subunit}, so the finer depths require no further spatial maximum. The continuous piecewise linear functions and minima are Lipschitz, and square roots satisfy~$|\sqrt x-\sqrt y|\leq\sqrt{|x-y|}$. Hence the quantities built from these are Cauchy in~$L^p$.

\smallskip

For~(ii), on the event that the limiting sum exceeds the larger threshold, a subsequence of the approximating sums that converges almost surely eventually exceeds some threshold strictly between the two thresholds, and each approximating sum exceeds that threshold with probability at most~$e^{-AD}$. Fatou's lemma applied to the indicators gives the bound for the limit.
\end{proof}

A cell~$q$ is \textbf{good} for~$E$ when the event~$G_E(q)$ specified above holds, and \textbf{bad} otherwise. Refine the subsequence defining~$E$ jointly for the deterministic reference ratios and the countable family of normalized arrays. In particular,~$s_N(q)\to s_E(q)>0$, with the scalar of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare}; for a negative-depth enlargement use the signed retained-sum formula above. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-prefix-limit} gives convergence in~$L^p$ for each of the fixed orders. A further diagonal refinement gives almost-sure convergence at all the countably many depths and observation centers. Each fixed finite set of conditions defining~$G_N^{\rm det}(q)$ then holds eventually on~$G_E(q)$, using~$\lambda_{\rm lim}<\lambda_{\rm det}$, the weaker finite-cutoff ellipticity and error bounds, and the open limiting ratio interval.

\smallskip

Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-deterministic} at each fixed depth to the finite-cutoff solutions converging to~$u=G_Ef$ with smooth~$f$. Pass their inequalities to the limit along recovery sequences, use Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-energy-measures}(a) for their local energy measures, and intersect over the depths. This gives, on~$G_E(q)$, the local H\"older estimate with source, the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values, and harmonic approximation with tolerance~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$. The error constant was chosen above to absorb the factor two in the finite-cutoff bound.
