The comparison argument of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare} applies regularity only to the solutions~$G_Ef$ with smooth~$f$. For the second limit form~$F$ introduced there, the argument uses only the maximum principle for harmonic extensions.

\subsubsection{Events depending on finitely many fields}

The conditions depend on all the fields. We approximate the quantities in the conditions by functions of the fields with indices in a finite interval around the scale of the cube, and then cover the failure of~$G_E(q)$ by events that depend on such intervals and have exponentially small probabilities.

\begin{lemma}[Approximation by functions of finitely many fields]
\label{mfd:lem-band}
Let~$X_{n,z}$ be the quantity~$\mathbf G_{n,z}$, one of the three summands of~$Z_{n,z}$, or a fixed finite nonnegative combination of the absolute values of the centered normalized coordinates specified in~(a)--(c), or their fixed continuous ramps. There exist~$a,c>0$, depending only on the fixed exponents and catalogue and not on the moment order, such that the following holds for every fixed~$1\leq p<\infty$. Include the needed auxiliary orders in the finite list fixed above. For sufficiently small~$\gmcdelta$, depending on that list and the fixed thresholds, there is~$C_p<\infty$. It depends on~$d$, the regularity and iteration exponents, the moment orders, the catalogue and the ramp thresholds, and is uniform in the valid scale, deterministic center and cutoff. For the quantities in~(a)--(c), the notation~$X_{n,z}$ refers to the cell of physical depth~$k=N-n$ and physical center~$3^{-N}z$ in the relabeled coordinates; all catalogue depths must be at most~$N$. The window about stationary index~$n$ is therefore the window about original bilateral index~$n-N=-k$. For every~$h\geq1$, every deterministic~$n$ and~$z$ for which these quantities are defined at the chosen cutoff, there is a random variable~$\widehat X_{n,z}^{(h)}$, measurable with respect to the fields with indices in an interval of length at most~$C(h+1)$ about~$n$, such that
\begin{equation}
	\bigl\|X_{n,z}-\widehat X_{n,z}^{(h)}\bigr\|_{L^p}\leq C_p\gmcdelta^c3^{-ah}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-22}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof proceeds in four steps, and the quantity we track is the error~$\|X_{n,z}-\widehat X_{n,z}^{(h)}\|_{L^p}$. In Steps~1--3 we truncate the geometrically weighted sums defining~$X_{n,z}$ at a depth proportional to~$h$, so that the retained terms are, or are approximated by, functions of the fields in the window: Step~1 treats the terms that involve only the fields, Step~2 the product term~$\mathsf P$, and Step~3 the energy terms of~$\mathsf R$ and~$\mathbf G$ and the normalized coordinates in~(a)--(c). Step~4 obtains the factor~$\gmcdelta^c$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-22}. The heart of the matter is Step~3, where Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-16} approximates each response by its conditional expectation given finitely many fields, and the geometric depth weights absorb the spatial maxima over the~$C3^{dr}$ cells at relative depth~$r$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We treat the terms that involve only the fields. Before the geometric weights are applied, the depth-$j$ sum in~$\mathsf F_{n,z}$ has~$L^p$ norm at most~$C_p\gmcdelta(j+1)^{\nf32}$: we cover~$z+\cu_{n+1+j}$ by cubes of the scale of each field, use the sub-Gaussian bound for the maximum from~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}, and sum the~$2j+1$ terms. We discard the depths~$j>ch$, whose total contribution is at most~$C_p\gmcdelta3^{-\nf{sch}{16}}$. The tail terms of~$\mathbf G$ (see~\eqref{e.def.bfG.mhq}) carry the explicit weight~$3^{k-i}$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We treat the product term. We retain the depths~$j\leq\lfloor h/2\rfloor$ and stop the second product at~$n+h$. Both~$\mathsf P$ and its approximant are at least~$2$, the approximant is not larger, and the removed depths contribute only their excess over~$2$. Fix~$j$. The first product factor at depth~$j$ is the exponential of~$\sup_{x\in z+\cu_{n+1+j}}\sum_{i=(n-j)\vee0}^{n+j}|\g_i(x)|$. We cover the cube by at most~$C_d3^{d(2j+1)}$ cubes of side~$3^{(n-j)\vee0}$. On each such cube, this sum is at most the sum over~$i$ of the value of~$|\g_i|$ at the center plus~$C_d3^{(n-j)\vee0}$ times the gradient norm of~$\g_i$ on the cube; these terms are independent in~$i$ and sub-Gaussian with parameter~$C_d\gmcdelta$. We take the exponential moment before the spatial maximum, which bounds the moment of order~$u$ on each cube by~$\exp(C_du\gmcdelta(j+1)+C_du^2\gmcdelta^2(j+1))$, and a maximum over the covering cubes, taken in~$L^q$ with~$q\geq p$, introduces a factor equal to their number to the power~$\nf1q$. With~$e^x-1\leq xe^x$, choosing~$q$ large depending on~$d,s,p$ and then~$\gmcdelta$ small shows that~$3^{-\nf{sj}{8}}$ times the~$L^p$ norm of the first product factor minus one is at most~$C_p\gmcdelta(j+1)3^{-\nf{sj}{16}}$.

\smallskip

The second product factor is the exponential of~$4\sup_x\sum_{i\geq n+j}|\g_i(x)-\g_i(z)|$, which is at most a sum, over~$i\geq n+j$, of sub-Gaussian variables multiplied by~$C_d3^{n+j-i}$. Hence its excess over one has~$L^p$ norm at most~$C_p\gmcdelta$, and the part with~$i>n+h$ contributes~$C_p\gmcdelta3^{j-h}$.

\smallskip

Summing over~$j\leq h/2$ gives~$C_p\gmcdelta3^{-\nf h2}$, and the discarded depths~$j>h/2$ contribute~$\sum_{j>h/2}3^{-\nf{sj}{8}}$ times the~$L^p$ norm of the sum of the two excesses at depth~$j$, which is at most~$C_p\gmcdelta3^{-ah}$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We treat the energy terms, fixing their moment orders first. Choose an auxiliary~$P\geq\max\{2,2p\}$ so large that~$d/P\leq\min\{s,\sigma/2\}/32$. Increase it, if necessary, to dominate the fixed spatial and comparison exponents. Fix moment orders~$3P$ and~$6P$ for the Morrey and smoothed Neumann bounds, the~$4P$ and~$8P$ centered-response orders, and the finitely many higher product and mesh orders required by those estimates, before reducing~$\gmcdelta$. Reduce~$\gmcdelta$ until twelve times each selected order is at most~$C^{-1}\gmcdelta^{-2}|\log\gmcdelta|^{-1}$ and the geometric moment conditions below hold. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15}, both clauses of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-16}, and their moment inputs hold for zero infrared and the positive coarse-scale partial infrared sums, uniformly in their truncation. Their proofs use only centered infrared increments on a fixed compact enlargement. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared} bounds these increments uniformly for the zero infrared potential, every positive partial sum and the full potential. Thus the moment inputs of Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} and the subsequent perturbation estimates are unchanged.

\smallskip

For~$\mathsf R$ and the energy part of~$\mathbf G$, every~$\mathcal J$ is an own-cutoff response, as in the preceding proof. Apply both clauses of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-16} to the affine Dirichlet and constant-flux Neumann terms, using the finite polarization family~$e_i,e_i+e_j$, normalizing these vectors and restoring their quadratic factors by homogeneity. The slower rate gives a nonnegative approximation~$\widehat{\mathcal J}$ with
\begin{equation*}
	\|\mathcal J-\widehat{\mathcal J}\|_{L^P}
	\leq C_P\gmcdelta^{\nf12}3^{-b\ell/256}\,.
\end{equation*}
The approximant can be obtained by conditioning~$\mathcal J$ itself; nonnegativity is preserved. When~$\ell$ exceeds its own cutoff, the zero-infrared response already lies in the retained window and the error is zero. There is no physical cell scalar in this estimate.

\smallskip

Retain relative depths~$r\leq R=\lfloor h/4\rfloor$ and use~$\ell=\lfloor h/2\rfloor$ for each retained response. At depth~$r$ there are at most~$C3^{dr}$ cells, so the maximum over them introduces a factor~$C3^{dr/P}$. The retained errors and the discarded tail of~$\mathsf R$ are bounded by
\begin{equation*}
	C_P\gmcdelta^{\nf12}3^{-bh/512}
	\sum_{r\geq2}3^{-(s/8-d/P)r}
	+C_P\gmcdelta^2
	\sum_{r>R}3^{-(s/8-d/P)r}\,.
\end{equation*}
For~$\mathbf G$ use~$|\sqrt{\min\{x,1\}}-\sqrt{\min\{y,1\}}| \leq\sqrt{|x-y|}$ and its stronger depth weight. The square root halves the error exponents. Hence, for these two energy families,
\begin{equation*}
	\|X-\widehat X\|_{L^p}
	\leq C_P\gmcdelta^{\nf14}3^{-a_{\rm RG}h}\,,
	\qquad a_{\rm RG}=\min\{b/1024,s/64\}>0\,.
\end{equation*}
Small~$h$ is absorbed into the constant. The retained original field indices lie in an interval of length~$C(h+1)$ about the parent scale. In bilateral physical coordinates, a parent of depth~$k$ has this interval about~$-k$. Fixed catalogue offsets only enlarge it by a fixed amount.

\smallskip

For the normalized coordinates in~(a)--(c), first write the coarse ellipticity sums, both response matrices and the error using the coefficient~$\widetilde A_{N,U}$. Split the sums at relative depth~$R$. On a retained descendant, separate the reference ratio between that descendant and~$U$. This ratio is part of the normalized coefficient decomposition; its positive and inverse moments satisfy~$\|c+c^{-1}\|_{L^u}\leq C_ue^{C_u\gmcdelta^2r}$. Retain the intervening coarse fields and truncate the remaining centered infrared increments. Apply the unit response bounds at~$2P$ and H\"older at~$2P$ to these products, using the already selected~$6P$ Morrey inputs. After the spatial maximum, the bound is multiplied by~$3^{-wr}3^{dr/P}e^{C_P\gmcdelta^2r}$, where~$w>0$ is the fixed depth weight of the quantity being treated. Choose~$P$ with~$d/P\leq w/4$ and then~$\gmcdelta$ with~$C_P\gmcdelta^2/\log3\leq w/4$. This leaves~$3^{-wr/2}$. The omitted infrared increments contribute at most~$C_P\gmcdelta3^{-ch}$ for a fixed~$c>0$. Below the smallest field scale, the direct bound~\eqref{e.direct.J.bound.subunit} on each terminal cell controls all its finer descendants, so there is one spatial maximum at that terminal depth.

\smallskip

The upper ellipticity sum is approximated first, then the inverse lower ellipticity sum. For the lower coefficient, truncate the reciprocal at~$t_h=\frac12\,3^{-ah/2}$ and use the moments at order~$4P$ to control the small-denominator part. This loses at most half the decay exponent~$a$; it does not change its independence from~$p$. Polarization supplies the two normalized matrices. Apply the same descendant argument to the error with comparison coefficient~$s_N(W)I$. The finite scalar ratios use their explicit signed retained-sum formula. These are exactly the three ellipticity coordinates, the two matrices, the error and the ratio specified above. The finite family of exponents and catalogue cubes permits one common field interval, the minimum of their positive decay rates and the minimum of their disorder thresholds. A fixed Lipschitz function of the approximated coordinates then gives the asserted band approximation for every finite test. In particular the~$(1,1)$ consequences require no extra approximation: their bounds follow deterministically from the~$(\sigma/2,2)$ tests on each enlargement.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We obtain the factor~$\gmcdelta^c$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-22}. The deviations of the quantities defining the conditions from their values at~$\gmcdelta=0$ satisfy~$\|\mathsf F\|_p+\|\mathsf P-2\|_p\leq C_p\gmcdelta$,~$\|\mathsf R\|_p\leq C_p\gmcdelta^2$ and~$\|\mathbf G\|_p\leq C_p\gmcdelta^{\nf12}$. The first bound follows from Steps~1 and~2. For~$\mathsf R$, group the cubes of~\eqref{e.good.sum.J} over which the supremum is taken by~$r=m-n$. There are at most~$C_d3^{dr}$ such cubes, the quantity~$\mathcal J$ of each has~$L^q$ norm~$C_q\gmcdelta^2$ by Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, and~$\sum_{r\geq2}3^{-(\frac s8-\frac dq)r}<\infty$ for a fixed~$q\geq p$ large enough. For~$\mathbf G$, we take~$m=n$ in Lemma~\ref{l.sum.the.errors} and obtain~$C_p\gmcdelta(1+|\log\gmcdelta|^{\nf12})\leq C_p\gmcdelta^{\nf12}$. If a truncation gives only~$C_p3^{-ah}$, without a positive power of~$\gmcdelta$, we combine it with a bound~$C_p\gmcdelta^b$, for some~$b>0$, on the distance of~$X$ from its deterministic value at~$\gmcdelta=0$. Conditional expectation onto the fields with indices in the interval of length~$C(h+1)$ about~$n$ is a contraction in~$L^p$. Thus the distance of~$X$ from its conditional expectation is at most twice its distance from the approximation. The same bound holds with the value at~$\gmcdelta=0$ in place of the approximation. The minimum of the two resulting bounds is at most their geometric mean~$2C_p\gmcdelta^{\nf b2}3^{-\nf{ah}{2}}$. For completeness, the centered moment bound for the normalized coordinates is~$\max_{\xi}\|\xi-\xi^{(0)}\|_{L^p}\leq C_p\gmcdelta^{c_1}$ with~$c_1>0$, where~$\xi$ runs over the fixed finite array,~$\xi^{(0)}$ is its unit-coefficient value and~$c_1$ is fixed before~$p$. To obtain it, apply~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} with comparison~$I$ after translation, scaling and removal of~$s_N(U)$. The small-error moments of~\eqref{e.homogenize.at.scale.m.E}, together with the centered infrared and coarse-field oscillation bounds, control the right-hand side uniformly in the own cutoff. The order~$\lambda\leq\Lambda$ turns the upper bounds for~$\Lambda$ and~$\lambda^{-1}$ into two-sided bounds, so their deviations from~$1$ are bounded by~$C(\mathcal E_{\sigma/2,\infty,2}+\mathcal E_{\sigma/2,\infty,2}^2)$. The spectral estimate in the proof of~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} gives the corresponding deviations of both matrices from~$I$. The error uses its own small-error bound; the signed retained-sum formula and the ordering of~$\ahom$ give the ratio deviation. All products use the larger orders already selected. Below the smallest field scale, use the direct terminal-cell bound as above. The fixed Lipschitz ramps inherit these bounds. Take the minimum of the finitely many positive decay and disorder-power exponents. These exponents are fixed by the geometric weights and the regularity parameters before~$p$ is chosen; only the auxiliary moment orders, disorder threshold and prefactor depend on~$p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Sums of $Z$ and $\mathbf G$ over scales at a fixed center]
\label{mfd:in-prefix}
Let~$\lambda>0$ and~$A<\infty$. If~$\gmcdelta$ is sufficiently small, then, for every deterministic center~$z$, every interval~$n,\ldots,n+k-1$ of scales, in the coordinates of~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization}, with all quantities evaluated at a single cutoff, and each~$X\in\{Z,\mathbf G\}$,
\begin{equation*}
	\P\Bigl[\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}X_{n+i,z}>\frac{\lambda k}{4}\Bigr]\leq Ce^{-Ak}\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For~$X=Z$ we apply Lemmas~\ref{l.bad.scales.for.nabla.gj},~\ref{l.exp.goodscales} and~\ref{l.discounted.J.local} at the lower thresholds of the continuous piecewise linear functions. The exponential rates in these lemmas tend to infinity as~$\gmcdelta\downarrow0$. For~$X=\mathbf G$ we apply Lemma~\ref{l.sum.the.errors}. Its estimate~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.uniform} concerns the average of the approximation errors over the interval, with an~$\mathcal O_{\Gamma_2}$ fluctuation of size~$(m-n+1)^{-\nf12}$. After multiplication by the length~$m-n+1$, the sum exceeds~$\lambda(m-n+1)$ with probability at most~$\exp(-c\lambda^2(m-n+1)\gmcdelta^{-2}|\log\gmcdelta|^{-1})$. The bounds are uniform in the position of the interval by the stationarity in Assumption~\ref{a.g1} and by Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity}.
\end{proof}

For use with the limiting good event, fix a physical depth~$j$, an observation center and a window~$\mathcal B_{j,h}$ about bilateral index~$-j$. Write~$X_N$ for any primitive summand or finite ramp above, pulled back from the stationary coordinates at cutoff~$N$, and~$X_E$ for its limit. The window~$\mathcal B_{j,h}$ is the same for all sufficiently large~$N$. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-prefix-limit}(i) and contraction give
\begin{equation*}
	\|\E[X_N\mid\mathcal B_{j,h}]-\E[X_E\mid\mathcal B_{j,h}]\|_{L^p}
	\leq\|X_N-X_E\|_{L^p}\longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation*}
By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-band}, the distance of~$X_N$ from its conditional expectation is at most~$2C_p\gmcdelta^c3^{-ah}$. Passing to the limit gives the same bound for~$X_E$; the centered moment bounds pass to the limit as well. Thus the limiting quantities have band approximants~$\E[X_E\mid\mathcal B_{j,h}]$, with the same decay exponents and at most twice the constant. For the primitive prefixes, apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-prefix} with parameter~$\lambda_{\rm cut}$ to the interval of depths~$\ell_U-k_1,\ldots,\ell_U+D$ of the prefix. Its length is at most~$3D$ since~$D\geq k_0\geq\max\{k_1,1\}$, so the resulting bound applies at threshold~$\lambda_{\rm cut}D<\lambda_{\rm lim}D/4$. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-prefix-limit}(ii) then gives the exponential bound at~$\lambda_{\rm lim}D/4$ for the limiting sum. The rate can be chosen arbitrarily large before the moment orders and disorder threshold are fixed.

\begin{lemma}[Covering the limiting bad event]
\label{mfd:lem-witness}
Use the limiting arrays defining~$G_E(q)$ and set~$\lambda=\lambda_{\rm lim}$. Let~$A<\infty$. If~$\gmcdelta$ is sufficiently small, then, for every cell~$q$ at triadic depth~$n$, there exist events~$W_{q,h}$ for~$h\geq1$ such that~$W_{q,h}$ is measurable with respect to the fields~$\g_{-j}$ whose indices~$j$ lie between~$n-h$ and~$n+2h$,~$\P(W_{q,h})\leq e^{-Ah}$, and, up to a null set,
\begin{equation*}
	\{G_E(q)\text{ fails}\}\subseteq\bigcup_{h\geq1}W_{q,h}\,.
\end{equation*}
The same conclusion holds for the failure of one additional scalar or matrix condition whose centered~$L^p$ norm is at most~$C_p\gmcdelta$ and whose approximation by a function of the fields with indices in an interval of length at most~$C(h+1)$ about~$n$ has error at most~$C_p\gmcdelta3^{-ah}$ in~$L^p$, for every~$h\geq1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Outside a common null set, all dyadic conditional-expectation approximants converge to their limiting quantities. This follows from the summable~$L^p$ errors and a countable intersection over the cells, centers and finite tests. If~$G_E(q)$ fails, then either one of the finite tests~(a)--(c) fails, or there are a prefix length~$k=D\geq k_0$, one of at most~$Ce^{Ck}$ choices of a cube and a center, and a limiting primitive prefix sum~$S$ with~$S\geq\lambda k$, where~$\lambda=\lambda_{\rm lim}$. The~$k_1$ coarser depths only change the constant in the bounds below. Approximate~$S$ by~$S^{(\ell)}$, the sum of the conditional expectations of its summands on windows of radius~$2^\ell k$. The limiting band bound above gives~$\|S-S^{(\ell)}\|_{L^p}\leq C_pk\gmcdelta^c3^{-a2^\ell k}$. If~$S\geq\lambda k$, then either~$S^{(0)}>\lambda k/2$ or, for some~$\ell\geq1$,~$|S^{(\ell)}-S^{(\ell-1)}|>\lambda k2^{-\ell-2}$. Indeed, the increment allowances sum to~$\lambda k/4$, and the approximants converge almost surely to~$S$.

\smallskip

The first event implies either~$S>\lambda k/4$ or~$|S-S^{(0)}|>\lambda k/4$. Use the limiting prefix bound proved before the lemma, with a rate larger than~$3A+C$, and Markov's inequality. Its probability is at most~$Ce^{-(3A+C)k}+(4C_p\gmcdelta^c/\lambda)^p3^{-apk}$. The~$\ell$th increment event has probability at most~$(C_p\gmcdelta^c)^p2^{p(\ell+C)}3^{-ap2^{\ell-1}k}$.

\smallskip

Take the union over the~$Ce^{Ck}$ spatial choices. For each~$h$, combine the events for pairs~$(k,\ell)$ with~$2^\ell k\asymp h$; there are polynomially many such pairs. Their field windows lie within an interval of radius~$Ch$ about~$n$. After relabeling~$h$, enlarge this interval to~$[n-h,n+2h]$. Choose~$p$ so that the decay rate~$ap\log3$ absorbs the prescribed rate, the spatial count and this fixed enlargement, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ to absorb the prefactors.

\smallskip

For each finite test~(a)--(c), let~$T_E\in[0,1]$ be its specified ramp. Failure of that test implies~$T_E=1$, including either endpoint of the open ratio interval. The limiting centered bound gives~$\|T_E\|_{L^p}\leq C_p\gmcdelta^{c_1}$, and its conditional-expectation error is at most~$C_p\gmcdelta^c3^{-ah}$. Start at a fixed window large enough to include the catalogue offsets. The probability that this first approximant exceeds~$1/2$ is small by its~$L^p$ norm; decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that it has the required exponential bound at that fixed radius. Telescope the approximants with increment allowances~$2^{-\ell-2}$ and apply Markov's inequality as for the prefixes. The finite union of these covers gives the required events for~(a)--(c), after starting with a slightly larger rate. The same argument applies to the additional centered condition in the statement, after dividing by its fixed positive failure threshold.
\end{proof}

Fix~$L=3^{H_1}$ and a bounded cube~$Q$. A descendant at~$L$-adic generation~$n$ has side length~$L^{-n}$ times the side length of~$Q$. When~$Q$ has triadic side length~$3^{-k_Q}$, its descendants at generation~$n$ have physical triadic depth~$k=k_Q+H_1n$. A chain of length~$J$ is a sequence of~$J$ cubes of the~$L$-adic subdivision of~$Q$, each a child of the preceding one and the first a child of~$Q$.

\begin{proposition}[Bad cells along every chain]
\label{mfd:prop-allchain}
Let~$L=3^{H_1}$, fix a bounded cube~$Q$, and let~$\theta\in(0,1)$ and~$b_*>0$. There exists~$\gmcdelta_0>0$, depending on~$L$,~$\theta$,~$b_*$ and the parameters of the good event, such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there is a random variable~$B_\omega<\infty$ such that, for every~$t>0$,
\begin{equation*}
	\P(B_\omega>t)\leq(1-e^{-b_*})^{-1}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{b_*t}{1-\theta}\Bigr)\,,
\end{equation*}
and such that, almost surely, for every~$J\geq1$ and every chain~$\pi$ of length~$J$,
\begin{equation}
	\#\{\text{bad cells on }\pi\}\leq\theta J+B_\omega\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-23}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness} with rate~$A$. On a fixed chain, successive generations have field-index centers spaced by~$H_1$; for a triadic root the center is~$-(k_Q+H_1n)$. The rare-interval estimate holds on every integer interval, so a fixed root offset does not change it. Enlarge the interval of field indices determining~$W_{q,h}$ to the symmetric interval of radius~$2h$ about its depth, set all events at other depths or odd radii to the empty event, and apply Lemma~\ref{l.concentration.rare.intervals}. In that lemma, the rate is~$A/3$ and the density is at least~$\theta/H_1$. Thus, once~$A\theta\geq CH_1$, the probability that this chain has at least~$\theta J$ bad cells is at most~$e^{-cA\theta J}$. There are~$L^{dJ}$ chains. Choose~$A$ with~$cA\theta\geq d\log L+b_*$. The probability that some chain of length~$J$ has at least~$\theta J$ bad cells is then at most~$e^{-b_*J}$. These choices precede the moment order and the threshold for~$\gmcdelta$ in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness}.

\smallskip

Set~$B_\omega=\sup_J\max_{|\pi|=J}(\#\{\text{bad cells on }\pi\}-\theta J)_+$. If~$B_\omega>t$, then for some~$J>t/(1-\theta)$ there is a chain of length~$J$ with more than~$\theta J$ bad cells. Summing~$e^{-b_*J}$ over these~$J$ proves the stated tail bound and~\eqref{eq:mfd-23}.
\end{proof}

Every point of~$Q$ lies in exactly one chain of half-open cells. Integrating~\eqref{eq:mfd-23} over these chains gives, on the same event, for every finite Borel measure~$\mu$ on~$Q$,
\begin{equation}
	\sum_{j=1}^J\mu(\text{union of the bad cells at $L$-adic generation }j)
	\leq(\theta J+B_\omega)\mu(Q)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-24}
\end{equation}
In particular this holds for the random energy measure~$\Gamma_E(u)$.

\subsection{Comparison of two subsequential limits}
\label{mfd:sec-compare}

In this subsection we show that two subsequential limit forms differ only by a deterministic scalar factor (Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-prop}). Let~$E$ and~$F$ be two such forms, obtained along two subsequences of cutoffs, realized in the same Skorokhod representation (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}), so that both are functions of the same fields. Their domains are not assumed to be equal. We compare them on the cubes of~$\mathcal C$.

\smallskip

The preliminary two-sided comparison (Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-C0}) adapts the argument of~\citet[Section~3.4]{DGComparison} on the ratios of the normalizations of the two forms. In that argument, comparisons built from paths around annuli, together with upper and lower bounds on crossing distances, exclude ratios of the two normalizations that are too large or too small. Here harmonic extensions play the role of the paths. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density} glues harmonic extensions on cubes at the scales where the ratio~$r_k$ of the two normalizations, defined below, is small, and shows that if this happens on a positive density of scales, then~$F$ is bounded by a small multiple of~$E$. By the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-18}, this contradicts the moment bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-response-reciprocal}. This excludes ratios that are too small. Exchanging~$E$ and~$F$ excludes ratios that are too large. The proof of Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-C0} then applies the same construction on the remaining scales, where the ratio is bounded above and below, to compare the domains and the energies in both directions.

\smallskip

As in the metric uniqueness proofs of~\citet[Section~6]{GM} and~\citet[Section~4.5]{DGUniqueness}, we consider the optimal upper and lower comparison constants. Those proofs shorten geodesic segments to obtain probability bounds incompatible with a strict difference between the constants. They must control which annuli a field-dependent geodesic visits. The probability comparison and counting argument in~\citet[Section~4.2]{DGUniqueness} provide this control after suitable smooth changes of the Gaussian free field.

\smallskip

Here Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} bounds the number of bad cells along every chain of nested cubes, on a single event that does not depend on the solution. Its integrated form~\eqref{eq:mfd-24} therefore applies to the energy measure of the solution being replaced. We use harmonic extensions on disjoint cubes in place of shorter path segments. Since the solution is close to affine on good cells, the comparison of the minimal energies of~$E$ and~$F$ with affine boundary values transfers to its boundary values. Summing, over these cubes, the amount by which the minimal~$F$-energy falls below the upper constant times the minimal~$E$-energy improves one of the comparison constants for the forms themselves.

\smallskip

In this subsection a cell~$q$ is called good only when both~$G_E(q)$ and~$G_F(q)$ hold. Applying Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} to each form with half the allowed density of bad cells and adding the two counts gives~\eqref{eq:mfd-23} for this notion of bad cell. On a good cell we thus have the regularity and coercivity estimates for~$E$ and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values for~$F$, and the same with the roles of~$E$ and~$F$ exchanged.

\smallskip

The comparison is expressed through the scalars~$s_E(q)$ and~$s_F(q)$ of the two forms. At a cell~$q$ of side~$3^{-k}$ and center~$z$, the scalar~$s_E(q)$ of~$E$ is
\begin{equation*}
	s_E(q)=\exp\Bigl(H(z)+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}(\g_{-j}(z)-\tau^2)\Bigr)
	\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\ahom_{N-k}}{\ahom_N}\,,
\end{equation*}
the limit being taken along the subsequence that defines~$E$. We define~$s_F(q)$ in the same way, along the subsequence that defines~$F$. After a further diagonal extraction, these limits exist for all~$k$ at once: since~$|\ahom_{N}\ahom_{N-k}^{-1}-1|\leq C\gmcdelta^2ke^{C\gmcdelta^2k}$ by~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering.ratio}, the ratio~$\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N$ lies in a compact subinterval of~$(0,\infty)$ that depends only on~$k$. The ratio~$r_k=\frac{s_F(q)}{s_E(q)}$ of these two scalars is deterministic and does not depend on~$z$.

\smallskip

Choose the parameters in the following order. First fix the geometric and regularity parameters below, then~$L$, the moment orders, and a first threshold for~$\gmcdelta$, which determines the comparison constant~$C_0$ of Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-C0}. With~$C_0$ fixed, we tighten the tolerance~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ of the harmonic comparison and add the comparison of the minimal energies of~$E$ and~$F$ with affine boundary values, of Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-32}, whose error is proportional to the difference between the optimal comparison constants defined below. This requires only a further decrease of~$\gmcdelta$. The thresholds for sums over intervals of scales and~$L$ stay fixed. None of these choices depends on the difference of the optimal constants.

\subsubsection{Comparison from a positive density of small ratios}

We first show that a positive density of scales at which~$r_k\leq a$ forces~$F\leq C_*aE$. Fix~$\eta_0$ with~$8(2(1-\alpha)+\eta)<\eta_0<\nf13$, which is possible by the choice of~$\alpha$ and~$\eta$ in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, and choose the parameters so that~$D\geq\nf{8d}{\eta_0}$ and~$\theta\leq\nf{\eta_0}{8}$. By the choice of~$\eta_0$ we have~$2(1-\alpha)+\eta<\eta_0/8$, which is equivalent to~$d-2+2\alpha-\eta>d-\nf{\eta_0}{8}$. Then choose~$L=3^{H_1}$ so large that~$L^{\nf{\eta_0}{3}-\nf{\eta_0}{8}}\geq2C_d^{\nf{\eta_0}{3}}$, where~$C_d$ is the constant in the bound below on the number of children whose enlargement is not contained in the parent. Here we use the~$L$-adic grid, translated as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density}, with cell sides~$L^{-n}$, so~$n$ is measured from side length one and the physical triadic depth is~$k=H_1n$. Thus~$r_{H_1n}$ is~$r_k$ at that physical depth. In an~$L$-adic subdivision starting from a root~$Q$ of side~$3^{-k_Q}$, generation~$n$ instead has physical depth~$k_Q+H_1n$. For a root of side~$L^{-n_0}$ this shifts every index by the fixed integer~$n_0$, which does not affect upper densities. For a child~$q$ of side~$r$ of a cell~$p$ of side~$Lr$, consider its concentric enlargement of side~$3r$. At most~$C_dL^{d-1}$ children of each cell have an enlargement whose closure is not contained in~$p$. A set~$S\subseteq\mathbb N$ has \textbf{upper density} at least~$\eta$ if~$\limsup_JJ^{-1}\#(S\cap\{1,\ldots,J\})\geq\eta$.

\smallskip

On a good cell whose enlargement has its closure in the parent, the regularity of~$E$ and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values of~$F$ combine as follows.

\begin{lemma}[Extension on good cells]
\label{mfd:lem-goodext}
Let~$Q$ be a cube of~$\mathcal C$ and~$f$ a smooth function, and set~$u=G_E^Qf$, where~$G_E^Q$ is the Dirichlet Green operator of~$E$ on~$Q$. The constant~$C$ below depends only on~$d$, the fixed regularity and iteration exponents,~$k_0$, the primitive and trace constants, the displayed good-event thresholds and ratio bounds, and the finite enlargement and grid geometry. It is independent of the cell, depth, source, extracted subsequence and environment. This constant uses only the primitive iteration thresholds, the extension exponent, the ellipticity bounds and the scalar comparisons on~$q$ and~$3q$; it is independent of~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ and~$c_{\rm det}$. It can therefore be fixed before the harmonic-comparison tolerance is tightened. The random constant~$K$ may also depend on~$Q$,~$f$ and the environment.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item If~$q=z+r\cu_0$ is a good child of~$p$ at depth~$k$ whose concentric enlargement of side~$3r$ has its closure in~$p$, then
\begin{equation*}
	\inf_{c\in\R}\|u(z+r\,\cdot)-c\|_{C^\alpha(\cu_0)}
	\leq Cr^{\nf{2-d}{2}}s_E(q)^{-\nf12}\sqrt{\Gamma_E(u)(p)}
	+Cr^2s_E(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty\,,
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
	\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})
	\leq Cr_k\bigl[\Gamma_E(u)(p)+s_E(q)^{-1}r^{d+2}\|f\|_\infty^2\bigr]\,.
\end{equation*}
\item There is a random constant~$K<\infty$ such that~$\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})\leq Kr^{d-2+2\alpha-\eta}$ for every cell~$q\subseteq Q$ of side~$r$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For~(i), the local H\"older estimate with source on the good cell bounds the oscillation on~$q$ by the normalized~$L^2$ oscillation on the concentric cube of side~$3r$ and the source (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}). The coarse-grained Poincar\'e inequality~\eqref{e.CG.Poincare.mean.zero} on that cube bounds its oscillation by its energy. Since its closure lies in~$p$, this energy is at most~$\Gamma_E(u)(p)$, giving the factor~$r^{\nf{2-d}{2}}$ in unnormalized units. The source is controlled by~\eqref{eq:mfd-5}: by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-primitive}, convolving~$f$ with the gradient of the Newtonian kernel gives a vector field with~$C^{\nf12}$ seminorm at most~$Cr^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty$ on a cube of side~$r$. Scaling gives the term~$r^2s_E(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty$. For the form~$F$ on~$q$, the coefficient factor in~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} is comparable to~$s_F(q)=r_ks_E(q)$. Inserting the first estimate into that bound gives the second estimate: we square the first bound and multiply by~$s_F(q)r^{d-2}$.

\smallskip

For~(ii), the global bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-5} for~$u$ gives~$\|u(z+r\,\cdot)-c\|_{C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)}\leq r^\alpha[u]_\alpha$, and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} on grid cubes bounds the factor in~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for~$F$ on~$q$ by~$Kr^{-\eta}$. Then~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} gives the bound~$Kr^{-\eta}r^{d-2}r^{2\alpha}[u]_\alpha^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Comparison from a positive density of small ratios]
\label{mfd:prop-density}
Assume that the parameters are chosen as above. There exists a deterministic~$C_*<\infty$, depending on~$d$,~$\eta_0$,~$L$ and the constants of the good event, such that the following holds for every~$a>0$. Let~$S$ be a deterministic set of~$L$-adic depths of upper density at least~$\eta_0$ with~$r_{H_1n}\leq a$ for every~$n\in S$. Then, on every cube of~$\mathcal C$,~$D(E)\subseteq D(F)$ and, for every~$v\in D(E)$,
\begin{equation}
	F(v)\leq C_*aE(v)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-25}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix a cube~$Q$ of~$\mathcal C$. Translate the~$L$-adic grid so that its origin is a corner of~$Q$. Since the side of~$Q$ is triadic, every sufficiently fine~$L$-adic mesh partitions~$Q$ exactly. Fix~$u=G_Ef$ with smooth~$f$, fix~$c>0$, and set~$\mu=\Gamma_E(u)+c\,dx$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} with~$\eta<2$, the source term in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-goodext} is at most~$Kr^{d+2-\eta}\|f\|_\infty^2\leq cr^d=c|q|$ for cells of side below a number~$h_0$ depending on~$u$,~$c$ and the environment.

\smallskip

Fix an~$L$-adic mesh of side at most~$h_0$ that partitions~$Q$; all cells below are subcubes of the finitely many cubes of this mesh, and we apply Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} to each of them.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We stop the subdivision at good cells that carry a fixed fraction of the mass of their parents, and bound the sum, over these cells~$q$, of the minimal~$F$-energy~$\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ with the boundary values of~$u$. Within each cube of the mesh of side at most~$h_0$ we follow the subdivision tree and stop at the first child~$q$ that lies at an~$L$-adic depth in~$S$, is good, has an enlargement with closure in its parent, and satisfies~$\mu(p)\leq L^D\mu(q)$. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-goodext}, each cell at which the subdivision stops satisfies~$\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})\leq Ca[\mu(p)+c|q|]\leq CaL^D\mu(q)$. The interiors of these cells are disjoint. Hence the sum of~$\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ over them is at most~$C_*a\mu(Q)$ with~$C_*=CL^D$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We show that the sum of~$\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ over the remaining cells, those on chains along which the subdivision never stops, tends to zero. There are arbitrarily large depths~$J$ at which at least~$\frac{3\eta_0}{4}J$ of the~$L$-adic depths below the mesh of side at most~$h_0$ belong to~$S$. Along a chain on which the subdivision has not stopped by such a depth~$J$, every step to a good child with an enlargement in its parent, at an~$L$-adic depth in~$S$, fails the condition~$\mu(p)\leq L^D\mu(q)$. Since~$\mu$ is monotone along the chain and drops by more than the factor~$L^D$ at each of these steps, while every leaf satisfies~$\mu(\text{leaf})\geq c|\text{leaf}|=cL^{-dJ}$ on the~$L$-adic mesh of depth~$J$, the number of these steps is at most
\begin{equation*}
	\frac{d}{D}J+\frac{\log(\mu(Q)/c)}{D\log L}
	\leq\frac{\eta_0}{8}J+O_{Q,c,u,\omega}(1)\,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{eq:mfd-23}, at most~$\theta J+B_\omega\leq\frac{\eta_0}{8}J+B_\omega$ of the steps at~$L$-adic depths in~$S$ are bad. For all sufficiently large such~$J$, the chain therefore has at least~$\frac{3\eta_0}{4}J-\frac{\eta_0}{4}J-O(1)\geq\frac{\eta_0}{3}J$ steps at~$L$-adic depths in~$S$ to a child whose enlargement is not contained in its parent. At a step to such a child there are at most~$C_dL^{d-1}$ choices of the child. At every other step there are at most~$L^d$. Summing over the at most~$2^J$ positions of the steps to such children, we find that the number of leaves on chains along which the subdivision does not stop is at most
\begin{equation*}
	C_Q2^JL^{dJ}(C_d/L)^{\nf{\eta_0J}{3}}\leq C(Q,c,u,\omega)L^{(d-\frac{\eta_0}{8})J}\,,
\end{equation*}
where we used~$L^{\nf{\eta_0}{3}-\nf{\eta_0}{8}}\geq2C_d^{\nf{\eta_0}{3}}$. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-goodext}(ii), the sum of~$\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ over them is at most~$KL^{(d-\frac{\eta_0}{8})J}$ times~$L^{-(d-2+2\alpha-\eta)J}$, which tends to zero because~$d-2+2\alpha-\eta>d-\nf{\eta_0}{8}$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We show that~$u\in D(F)$ and~$F(u)\leq C_*aE(u)$. At a finite depth~$J$, the cells at which the subdivision stops and the remaining leaves form a finite partition of~$Q$ into cells, on whose boundaries we take the values of~$u$. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-skeleton}, the~$F$-harmonic extensions of these boundary values glue to a function~$v_J\in D(F)$ with~$F(v_J)\leq C_*a\mu(Q)+o(1)$. By the maximum principle, each extension lies between the extreme boundary values of~$u$ on its cell, so~$\|v_J-u\|_\infty$ is at most the modulus of continuity of~$u$ at scale~$h_0$. For fixed~$h_0$ we take~$J$ large and then let~$h_0\to0$. Then~$v_J\to u$ uniformly, and the closedness of~$F$ gives~$F(u)\leq C_*a(E(u)+c|Q|)$. Letting~$c\downarrow0$ gives~$u\in D(F)$ and~$F(u)\leq C_*aE(u)$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We extend the inequality to~$D(E)$. Let~$v\in D(E)$. By Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}, there are smooth~$f_n$ with~$G_Ef_n\to v$ in the form norm of~$E$. By Step~3, the sequence~$(G_Ef_n)$ is Cauchy in the form norm of~$F$, and the closedness of~$F$ identifies its limit with~$v$. This gives~$v\in D(F)$ and~\eqref{eq:mfd-25}.
\end{proof}

% Provenance: Proposition mfd:prop-density and Theorem mfd:thm-C0 adapt Ding--Gwynne (bib key DGComparison),
% Section 3.4, where paths around annuli and upper and lower bounds on crossing distances exclude too large or too small
% ratios of the two normalizations. Changed here: harmonic extensions on cubes replace the paths, and the contradiction
% comes from the moment bounds of Lemma mfd:lem-response-reciprocal via the variational formula (eq:mfd-18).
\begin{theorem}[Two-sided comparison]
\label{mfd:thm-C0}
There exists a deterministic~$C_0\geq1$ such that, for every pair of subsequential limit forms and every~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_*$, where~$\gmcdelta_*$ is the initial threshold for~$\gmcdelta$ chosen above, on every cube of~$\mathcal C$,
\begin{equation}
	D(E)=D(F)\quad\text{and}\quad C_0^{-1}E\leq F\leq C_0E\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-26}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix a nonzero smooth source~$f_0$ on the unit cube. By the positive and negative moments of~$\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle$ and~$\langle f_0,G_Ff_0\rangle$ in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-response-reciprocal}, choose a deterministic~$A\geq1$, uniform over the subsequential limit forms and over~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_*$, so that~$\P[A^{-1}\leq\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle,\langle f_0,G_Ff_0\rangle\leq A]>0$. It suffices to choose~$A$ so that each of the four tail probabilities is less than~$\nf18$. Set~$a_0=(2C_*A^2)^{-1}$ and let~$S_-$ be the set of~$L$-adic depths~$n$ with~$r_{H_1n}\leq a_0$. Since the ratios~$r_k$ are deterministic, so is~$S_-$. If~$S_-$ has upper density at least~$\eta_0$, then Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density} gives~$D(E)\subseteq D(F)$ and~$F\leq C_*a_0E$ on~$D(E)$. The variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-18} then gives, almost surely,
\begin{align*}
	\langle f_0,G_Ff_0\rangle
	&=\sup_{v\in D(F)}\bigl\{2\langle f_0,v\rangle-F(v)\bigr\}
	\geq\sup_{v\in D(E)}\bigl\{2\langle f_0,v\rangle-C_*a_0E(v)\bigr\}
	\notag \\ &
	=(C_*a_0)^{-1}\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle=2A^2\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle\,,
\end{align*}
which contradicts~$\P[A^{-1}\leq\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle,\langle f_0,G_Ff_0\rangle\leq A]>0$. Hence~$S_-$ has upper density less than~$\eta_0$. Exchanging~$E$ and~$F$ gives the same bound for the set~$S_+$ of~$L$-adic depths with~$r_{H_1n}\geq a_0^{-1}$. The complement of~$S_-\cup S_+$ therefore has lower density greater than~$1-2\eta_0>\eta_0$, since~$\eta_0<\nf13$, and on this complement~$a_0<r_{H_1n}<a_0^{-1}$. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density}, applied in both directions with~$a=a_0^{-1}$, gives~$F\leq C_*a_0^{-1}E$ and~$E\leq C_*a_0^{-1}F$ with equal domains. Thus~\eqref{eq:mfd-26} holds with~$C_0=C_*/a_0=2C_*^2A^2$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The optimal constants are deterministic}

For~$a>0$ we write~$aE\leq F$ if~$aE^Q\leq F^Q$ on~$D(E^Q)=D(F^Q)$ for every cube~$Q$ of~$\mathcal C$, and we optimize over all these cubes:
\begin{equation*}
	m=\sup\{a:\ aE\leq F\},\qquad M=\inf\{a:\ F\leq aE\}\qquad\text{and}\qquad
	\Delta=M-m\,.
\end{equation*}
The numbers~$m$ and~$M$ are the optimal lower and upper comparison constants, and~$\Delta$ is their difference. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-26},~$C_0^{-1}\leq m\leq M\leq C_0$, and~$mE\leq F\leq ME$ by monotone limits. The comparison constants are defined for each realization; the next lemma shows that they are deterministic, which Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-prop} uses to conclude that the proportionality constant is deterministic.

\begin{lemma}[The optimal comparison constants are deterministic]
\label{mfd:lem-endpoints}
The optimal comparison constants~$m$ and~$M$ are almost surely constant.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The comparison constants are measurable: on each cube of~$\mathcal C$,~$aE\leq F$ is equivalent, by the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-18}, to~$\langle f,G_Ff\rangle\leq a^{-1}\langle f,G_Ef\rangle$ for all sources~$f$ in the countable dense family.

\smallskip

Resampling one field multiplies both energy measures by the same positive continuous weight, by~\eqref{eq:mfd-21}. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-sincos}, an inequality between the two forms is equivalent to the same inequality between their energy measures. Multiplying both measures by a common weight preserves these inequalities in both directions. Thus resampling one field leaves~$m$ and~$M$ unchanged. Since these constants are bounded, Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-resampling-sum}, with no fields retained, gives~$\|m-\E m\|_1=\|M-\E M\|_1=0$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Concentration of the difference between the energies}

Assume~$\Delta>0$. We prove a concentration bound for the difference of the two energies, with an error proportional to~$\Delta$.

\smallskip

We first check that the minimal energies of~$E$ and~$F$ with given boundary values on a cell are taken over the same class of admissible functions. Let~$q$ be a cell and let~$Q\supset q$ be a larger cube of~$\mathcal C$. A boundary value~$\ell$ on~$\partial q$, affine or of class~$C^\beta$ with~$\beta>\nf12$, is realized by an~$E$-harmonic extension through a neighbourhood of~$\partial q$, which belongs to~$D(E^Q)$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-26} this extension lies in~$D(F^Q)$. Its restriction to~$q$, together with the identification of the minimal energies in Propositions~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary} and~\ref{mfd:prop-gluing}, places it in the admissible class of both~$\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)$ and~$\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)$. Thus both minimal energies below are taken over the same class of functions with the prescribed boundary values, on which~$m\Lambda_{E,q}\leq\Lambda_{F,q}\leq M\Lambda_{E,q}$ as quadratic forms.

\begin{lemma}[Difference of two minimizers]
\label{mfd:lem-diff}
Let~$u_E$ and~$u_F$ be the minimizers of~$E$ and~$F$ in~$q$ with the same affine boundary values, let~$c\in[m,M]$, and set~$D=F-cE$ and~$w=u_F-u_E$. Then~$F(w,v)=-D(u_E,v)$ for every~$v\in D(E^q)$, and
\begin{equation}
	E(w)\leq\Bigl(\frac{\Delta}{m}\Bigr)^2E(u_E)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-27}
\end{equation}
Moreover, for all~$u,v$ in the common domain and every Borel set~$B$,
\begin{equation*}
	|\Gamma_D(u,v)|(B)\leq\Delta\sqrt{\Gamma_E(u)(B)\Gamma_E(v)(B)}\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Subtracting the Euler equations~$F(u_F,v)=0=E(u_E,v)$ gives~$F(w,v)=F(u_F,v)-F(u_E,v)=-F(u_E,v)+cE(u_E,v)=-D(u_E,v)$. Since~$(m-c)E\leq D\leq(M-c)E$ and~$|m-c|,|M-c|\leq\Delta$, the symmetric form~$D$ satisfies~$|D(u,v)|\leq\Delta\sqrt{E(u)E(v)}$. Taking~$v=w$ gives~$mE(w)\leq F(w)=-D(u_E,w)\leq\Delta\sqrt{E(u_E)E(w)}$, which is~\eqref{eq:mfd-27}.

\smallskip

For a Borel set~$A$, Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-sincos} gives~$|\Gamma_D(h)(A)|\leq\Delta\Gamma_E(h)(A)$. Polarization therefore gives, for every~$t>0$,
\begin{equation*}
	4t|\Gamma_D(u,v)(A)|
	\leq2\Delta\bigl[\Gamma_E(u)(A)+t^2\Gamma_E(v)(A)\bigr]\,.
\end{equation*}
Optimizing in~$t$ gives~$|\Gamma_D(u,v)(A)|\leq\Delta\sqrt{\Gamma_E(u)(A)\Gamma_E(v)(A)}$. Sum this bound over a finite Borel partition of~$B$, apply Cauchy--Schwarz, and take the supremum over the partitions to obtain the total-variation bound.
\end{proof}

We normalize the minimal energies with affine boundary values on a cell. Fix~$c\in[m,M]$. Let~$\ell$ range over the affine boundary values in a fixed finite set that contains the coordinate functions and determines quadratic forms by polarization. Set~$D_q=\sum_{i=1}^d\Lambda_{E,q}(x_i)$ and, for one of these boundary values,~$\mathcal R=(\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)-c\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell))/D_q$. By polarization, these quantities give the entries of the matrix~$B_k$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-31}. Define the normalized measures
\begin{equation*}
	\nu=\frac{1}{D_q}\sum_{\ell}\bigl[\Gamma_E(u_E^{\ell})+\Gamma_E(u_F^{\ell})\bigr]\,,
	\qquad
	\zeta=\frac{1}{D_q}\sum_{\ell}\Gamma_E(w^{\ell})\,,
\end{equation*}
where the sums run over the fixed affine boundary values and~$w^\ell=u_F^\ell-u_E^\ell$. Then~$\nu(q)\leq C(C_0)$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-26}, and~$\zeta(q)\leq C(C_0)\Delta^2$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-27}. The measure~$\nu$ inherits the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} of the energy measures of the minimizers. For a bounded~$g$ supported in~$B$, let~$\mathcal R^g$ denote the quantity~$\mathcal R$ computed for the forms~$E^g$ and~$F^g$.

\begin{lemma}[Perturbation of the normalized difference~$\mathcal R$]
\label{mfd:lem-relvar}
There exists~$C(C_0)<\infty$ such that, for every bounded~$g$ supported in~$B$,
\begin{equation}
	|\mathcal R^g-\mathcal R|\leq C\|g\|_\infty e^{C\|g\|_\infty}
	\bigl[\Delta\nu(B)+\sqrt{\nu(B)\zeta(B)}\bigr]\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-30}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let~$u_E^g$ and~$u_F^g$ minimize the weighted forms~$E^g$ and~$F^g$ with the same boundary values as~$u_E$ and~$u_F$, respectively. Put~$H_q=\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)-c\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)$,~$D=F-cE$,~$D^g=F^g-cE^g$,~$v_E=u_E^g-u_E$ and~$v_F=u_F^g-u_F$. Subtract the weighted Euler equations to obtain, for every test function~$\phi$ with zero boundary values on~$\partial q$,
\begin{equation}
	F^g(v_F-v_E,\phi)=-\int_B(e^g-1)
	[d\Gamma_F(w,\phi)+d\Gamma_D(u_E,\phi)]-D^g(v_E,\phi)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-28}
\end{equation}
Test~\eqref{eq:mfd-28} with~$v_F-v_E$ and use Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-diff} and the perturbation bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-14} for~$v_E$. Cauchy--Schwarz gives
\begin{equation}
	E^g(v_F-v_E)\leq C\|g\|_\infty^2e^{C\|g\|_\infty}
	[\Gamma_E(w)(B)+\Delta^2\Gamma_E(u_E)(B)]\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-29}
\end{equation}
By~\eqref{eq:mfd-14},
\begin{equation*}
	H_q^g-H_q
	=\int_B(e^g-1)\,d\bigl[\Gamma_F(u_F)-c\Gamma_E(u_E)\bigr]
	-\bigl[F^g(v_F)-cE^g(v_E)\bigr]\,.
\end{equation*}
Use the bilinear identities
\begin{align*}
	\Gamma_F(u_F)-c\Gamma_E(u_E)
	&=\Gamma_D(u_E)+\Gamma_F(w,u_F+u_E)\,,\\
	F^g(v_F)-cE^g(v_E)
	&=D^g(v_E)+F^g(v_F-v_E,v_F+v_E)\,.
\end{align*}
Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-diff} bounds the first line, in total variation on~$B$, by~$CD_q[\Delta\nu(B)+\sqrt{\nu(B)\zeta(B)}]$. For the second,~\eqref{eq:mfd-14} for each form and~\eqref{eq:mfd-29} give
\begin{equation*}
	E^g(v_E)+E^g(v_F+v_E)\leq C\|g\|_\infty^2e^{C\|g\|_\infty}D_q\nu(B)\,,
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
	E^g(v_F-v_E)\leq C\|g\|_\infty^2e^{C\|g\|_\infty}D_q[\zeta(B)+\Delta^2\nu(B)]\,.
\end{equation*}
Cauchy--Schwarz and~$|D^g(v_E)|\leq\Delta E^g(v_E)$ thus bound the correction by~$C\|g\|_\infty^2e^{C\|g\|_\infty}D_q[\Delta\nu(B)+\sqrt{\nu(B)\zeta(B)}]$. Absorbing~$\|g\|_\infty^2$ into~$\|g\|_\infty e^{C\|g\|_\infty}$ proves the required bound for~$|H_q^g-H_q|/D_q$.

\smallskip

Finally,~$|H_q|\leq C\Delta D_q$,~$e^{-\|g\|_\infty}D_q\leq D_q^g\leq e^{\|g\|_\infty}D_q$, and~\eqref{eq:mfd-14} summed over the coordinate data gives~$|D_q^g-D_q|\leq C\|g\|_\infty e^{C\|g\|_\infty}D_q\nu(B)$. Apply these bounds to~$\mathcal R^g-\mathcal R=(H_q^g-H_q)/D_q^g +H_q(D_q-D_q^g)/(D_qD_q^g)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Concentration of the normalized difference~$B_k$]
\label{mfd:prop-conc}
Let~$A_E$ and~$A_F$ be the coarse-grained matrices of~$E$ and~$F$ on a cell~$q$ at depth~$k$, defined by~$\Lambda_{E,q}(p\cdot x)=|q|\,p\cdot A_Ep$, and similarly for~$F$. Set~$c_k=\E[\operatorname{tr}A_F/\operatorname{tr}A_E]$ and~$B_k=(A_F-c_kA_E)/\operatorname{tr}A_E$. Then~$c_k\in[m,M]$ and~$\E B_k=0$. Moreover, there exists~$a>0$, depending only on the dimension and on the regularity exponents fixed earlier, and not on~$C_0$ or~$p$, such that the following holds for every~$1\leq p<\infty$. If~$\gmcdelta$ is sufficiently small, depending on~$p$ and~$C_0$, then, for every~$k$ and every integer~$h\geq0$,
\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
		&\|B_k\|_{L^p}\leq C_p(C_0)\gmcdelta\Delta\,,\\
		&\bigl\|B_k-\E[B_k\mid\text{fields with indices within distance }h\text{ of }k]\bigr\|_{L^p}\leq C_p(C_0)\gmcdelta\Delta3^{-ah}\,,
	\end{aligned}
	\label{eq:mfd-31}
\end{equation}
where~$C_p(C_0)$ does not depend on~$k$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}

\emph{Step 1.} The form order gives~$c_k\in[m,M]$. By Assumptions~\ref{a.g1} and~\ref{a.g3}, the joint law of~$A_E$ and~$A_F$, after their common scalar factor is removed, is invariant under signed permutations of the coordinates. Thus~$\E B_k$ is a scalar matrix. Its trace is zero by the definition of~$c_k$, so~$\E B_k=0$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We bound the moments of the constant in the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} for~$\nu$, uniformly in~$k$. Rescale a cell to the unit cube and divide~$E$ by~$s_E(q)$ and~$F$ by~$s_F(q)$. The fields of scale smaller than the cell then have their original law, and the sum of the fields of larger scale, with its value at the cell center subtracted, has the moments of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}, uniformly in~$k$. At finite cutoff, let~$u$ minimize the normalized energy with affine boundary values of slope~$e_1$. Test the Neumann variational formula for boundary flux~$e_1\cdot n$ with scalar multiples of~$u$. The product of the normalized Dirichlet and Neumann energies is then at least~$(\int_{\cu_0}e_1\cdot\nabla u)^2=1$. This inequality passes to the extracted limits. The moment bounds of Lemmas~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} and~\ref{mfd:lem-response-reciprocal} therefore control each normalized minimal energy and its reciprocal. For some deterministic~$A<\infty$, both normalized minimal energies with slope~$e_1$ lie in~$[A^{-1},A]$ with positive probability. On that event, the order of the original minimal energies and their scaling give that~$r_k$ times the ratio of the two normalized energies lies in~$[m,M]$, so that~$mA^{-2}\leq r_k\leq MA^2$. The last bounds hold deterministically because~$r_k$ is deterministic. Also the reciprocal of the sum of the normalized minimal energies over the coordinate directions is at most the normalized energy of the Neumann problem for~$E$ with boundary flux~$e_1\cdot n$. H\"older's inequality, the Morrey bounds for the two minimizers and these reciprocal moments now give the moments, of the finitely many orders fixed at the start of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness}, of the constant in the Morrey bound for~$\nu$, uniformly in~$k$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} Let~$p\geq2$, and consider one of the fields of scale smaller than the cell, of scale~$r$. Use the cutoff functions and strips of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15}, with boxes of side~$r^{(t-d+1)/(t+1)}$ and strips of width~$4\sqrt d\,r$. The restrictions of the field to the separated interior subboxes are independent by~\ref{a.g1}, and by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-borel-weights} the whole field can be removed from the limit forms. With~$b$ from Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-strips}, the original measure satisfies~$\nu(\mathrm{strips})\leq Kr^b$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-30}, the change of~$\mathcal R$ caused by deleting the field on the strips is at most~$C\Delta Se^{CS}(Kr^b+K^{\nf{1}{2}}r^{\nf{b}{2}})$, where~$S$ is the sup norm of the field. As in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15},~\eqref{eq:mfd-14} controls the changed minimizers, so the energy measures after the cutoff function is applied to the field satisfy~$\widehat\nu(q)\leq C$,~$\widehat\zeta(q)\leq C\Delta^2$ and~$\max_B\widehat\nu(B)\leq Ce^{CS}Kr^b$. The bounds on~$\widehat\nu(q)$ and~$\widehat\zeta(q)$ follow from the order between the two forms, which is preserved when the same cutoff function is applied to the field in both forms. The new ingredient in the Efron--Stein estimate is the bound on the sum of squares
\begin{equation*}
	\sum_B[\Delta\widehat\nu(B)
	+\sqrt{\widehat\nu(B)\widehat\zeta(B)}]^2
	\leq2\max_B\widehat\nu(B)
	[\Delta^2\widehat\nu(q)+\widehat\zeta(q)]
	\leq C\Delta^2\max_B\widehat\nu(B)\,.
\end{equation*}
Thus the first and last terms of~\eqref{eq:mfd-fourterm} in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15}, and the conditional Efron--Stein estimate, all keep the factor~$\Delta$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-layernorm}, the norms of the fields are absorbed after reducing the exponent~$b$ by a fixed fraction. For an entry~$X$ of~$B_k$, resampling the field at relative index~$j$ consequently gives
\begin{equation*}
	\|X-X^{(j)}\|_p\leq C_p(C_0)\gmcdelta\Delta
	\begin{cases}3^{-aj},&j\geq0,\\3^j,&j<0.\end{cases}
\end{equation*}
For~$j<0$ this follows directly from~\eqref{eq:mfd-30} and the bound of Step~2 on the sum of the fields of larger scale, with their value at the cell center subtracted. Choose~$a$ below a fixed fraction of~$b$. Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-resampling-sum} with no fields retained and then with the fields within distance~$h$ of~$k$ retained. Centering, polarization and the two geometric sums give~\eqref{eq:mfd-31}.
\end{proof}

By the concentration bound, we can add to the good event the requirement that the minimal energies of~$F$ and~$c_kE$ with affine boundary values differ by at most a small multiple of~$\Delta$ times the minimal energy of~$E$ with the same boundary values.

\begin{corollary}[Minimal energies with affine boundary values on good cells]
\label{mfd:cor-32}
Let~$\epsilon>0$. The good event of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches} can be strengthened by one additional condition, with a threshold for~$\gmcdelta$ and a bad-cell density~$\theta$ that do not depend on~$\Delta$, such that, on every good cell~$q$ at depth~$k$ and for every affine boundary value~$\ell$,
\begin{equation}
	|\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)-c_k\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)|\leq\epsilon\Delta\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-32}
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply the last assertion of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness} to~$X=B_k/\Delta$, with the additional condition~$|X|\leq\epsilon c_0$, where~$c_0>0$ is the deterministic constant of the next paragraph. Its hypotheses are precisely~\eqref{eq:mfd-31}. Reserve half the allowed density of bad cells for this condition. Choose the rate~$A$ of the events~$W_{q,h}$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness} larger than the exponential growth rate~$d\log L$ of the number of chains. Then choose the required moment order and decrease~$\gmcdelta$. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} gives the strengthened good event, with choices independent of~$\Delta$.

\smallskip

We pass from~$B_k$ to~\eqref{eq:mfd-32}. Condition~(a) of the good event bounds the condition number of the normalized matrix~$A_E$: the coarse-grained ellipticity constants~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.ellipticity} bound~$|A_E|$ and~$|A_E^{-1}|$. Hence~$\lambda_{\min}(A_E)\geq c_0\operatorname{tr}A_E$ with a deterministic~$c_0>0$. For an affine function with slope~$\xi$, we obtain~$|\xi\cdot(A_F-c_kA_E)\xi|\leq|B_k|\operatorname{tr}A_E|\xi|^2\leq\epsilon c_0\Delta\operatorname{tr}A_E|\xi|^2\leq\epsilon\Delta\,\xi\cdot A_E\xi$, which is~\eqref{eq:mfd-32}.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Affine approximation of the boundary values}

\begin{lemma}[Affine approximation of the boundary values]
\label{mfd:lem-affine}
Let~$\nf12<\beta<\alpha<1$,~$0<\gamma<1$,~$\zeta>0$ and~$\varrho>0$, and assume that
\begin{equation}
	\frac{d+\zeta}{2}
	-\gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}+\alpha
	+\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+d/2}(2-\alpha)\right)<0\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-33}
\end{equation}
Let~$E$ be a subsequential limit form along the subsequence of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, realized in the Skorokhod representation, let~$f\in C_c^\infty(Q)$,~$u=G_E^Qf$,~$c>0$ and~$\mu=\Gamma_E(u)+c\,dx$. Suppose that~$L=3^{H_1}$ is chosen large, with~$\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+6<H_1$, that~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ is then chosen small, and that~$\gmcdelta$ is then chosen small, all independently of~$f$ and~$c$. Then the following holds almost surely for cells of side below a number~$h_0>0$, which may depend on~$f$,~$c$ and the environment. Let~$q$ be a good child, of side~$r$, of a cell~$p\subset Q$ of side~$Lr$. If the cube concentric with~$q$ of side~$3^{\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+4}r$, used in conditions~(b)--(c), has its closure in~$p$, and~$\mu(p)\leq L^{d+\zeta}\mu(q)$, then there is an affine function~$\ell_q$ such that
\begin{equation}
	\Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q}-\ell_q)\leq\varrho\mu(q)\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-34}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Take~$L=3^{H_1}$ with~$\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+6<H_1$ and put~$S=3^{\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+4}r/729$. Condition~(c) of the good event compares~$s_E$ at the sides~$729S$ and~$r$ within a factor two. The mass bound on the parent and the estimate of the oscillation by the energy bound the oscillation of~$u$ on the cube of side~$S$ concentric with~$q$ by~$CS^{\nf{2-d}{2}}s_E(q)^{-\nf12}L^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}}\sqrt{\mu(q)}$, together with the source term~$CS^2s_E(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty$. Condition~(b) and Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates}, with the harmonic comparison on the concentric cube of side~$9S$ and its error measured on the concentric cube of side~$S$, show that the~$\underline L^2$ distance on the cube of side~$S$ between~$u$ and the Laplace-harmonic function on the cube of side~$9S$ with the boundary values of~$u$ is at most
\begin{equation*}
	C\epsilon_{\rm hom}S^{(2-d)/2}s_E(q)^{-\nf12}
	L^{(d+\zeta)/2}\sqrt{\mu(q)}
	+CS^2s_E(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
The estimates at finite cutoff pass to the extracted solutions by uniform convergence and Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-energy-measures}, since the energy measure~$\Gamma_E(u)$ does not charge the faces of~$p$. Let~$\ell_q$ be the affine function that best approximates~$u$ in~$L^2(q)$; we use it for both norms below. Approximating the Laplace-harmonic function by its first-order Taylor polynomial gains a factor~$(r/S)^2$ in~$L^2(q)$. Restricting the error of the harmonic approximation from the concentric cube of side~$S$ to~$q$ introduces a factor~$(S/r)^{\nf d2}$ in volume-normalized~$L^2$. The local H\"older bound on~$q$ gains a factor~$(r/S)^\alpha$, since the affine projection is bounded on~$C^\alpha$. Since~$L^\gamma r/27<S\leq L^\gamma r/9$, after division by~$r^{\nf{2-d}{2}}\sqrt{\mu(q)/s_E(q)}$ and reducing~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ after fixing~$L$, the rescaled~$L^2$ norm of~$u-\ell_q$ on~$q$ is therefore at most
\begin{equation*}
	CL^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}-\gamma\frac{d+2}{2}}
	+C\epsilon_{\rm hom}L^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}+\gamma}\,,
\end{equation*}
and its rescaled~$C^\alpha$ norm on~$q$ is at most~$CL^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}-\gamma(\frac{d-2}{2}+\alpha)}$. Interpolate between these two bounds with the exponent~$(\alpha-\beta)/(\alpha+d/2)$ and apply the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values on the good cell, where the factor in it is comparable to~$s_E(q)$. The spatial and coefficient factors cancel, giving
\begin{equation*}
	\Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q}-\ell_q)
	\leq C\bigl(L^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}-\gamma\frac{d+2}{2}}
	+\epsilon_{\rm hom}L^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}+\gamma}\bigr)^{\nf{2(\alpha-\beta)}{(\alpha+d/2)}}
	L^{2(1-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+d/2})(\frac{d+\zeta}{2}-\gamma(\frac{d-2}{2}+\alpha))}\mu(q)\,.
\end{equation*}
At~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}=0$, half the exponent of~$L$ is the left side of~\eqref{eq:mfd-33}, which is negative. First choose~$L$ to make this contribution smaller than~$\frac{\varrho}{4}$. Next choose~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ so small that the contribution of the error of the harmonic approximation is comparably small. Then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that the enlarged good event has the required density of bad cells along every chain. After the same normalization, the source terms are at most~$C_Lc^{-\nf12}s_E(q)^{-\nf12}r\|f\|_{C^1}\leq K_{L,\omega}c^{-\nf12}r^{1-\nf\eta2}\|f\|_{C^1}$, by~$\mu(q)\geq cr^d$ and~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}. They tend to zero because~$\eta<2$. A small~$h_0$ makes them smaller than~$\frac{\varrho}{4}$. The left side of~\eqref{eq:mfd-33} tends to~$-(1-\beta)/(1+d/2)<0$ as~$\alpha,\gamma\uparrow1$ and~$\zeta\downarrow0$, so the requirement on~$\alpha$ in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form} and the choices of~$\gamma$ and~$\zeta$ can be met in every dimension.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Strict improvement of a comparison constant}

We now combine concentration for affine boundary values with the affine approximation of the solution's boundary values to rule out~$\Delta>0$. Let~$\mathcal U=\{n:c_{H_1n}\leq(m+M)/2\}$. Either this set or its complement has upper density at least~$\nf12$. Fix~$\epsilon=\nf18$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-32}. On a good cell~$q$ at an~$L$-adic depth in~$\mathcal U$, for every affine~$\ell$,
\begin{equation*}
	B_q(\ell)\coloneqq M\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)-\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)\geq
	(M-c_k-\epsilon\Delta)\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)\geq\frac38\Delta\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)\,.
\end{equation*}
Since both minimal energies are taken over the same class of admissible functions,~$0\leq B_q\leq\Delta\Lambda_{E,q}$ on all admissible boundary values. If~$u$ is replaced on~$q$ by the~$F$-harmonic function with the same boundary values~$b$, then the new function has~$F$-energy~$\Lambda_{F,q}(b)=M\Lambda_{E,q}(b)-B_q(b)$ on~$q$, which is at most~$M\Gamma_E(u)(q)-B_q(b)$.

\smallskip

Harmonic replacements will give~$F(u)\leq(M-3\Delta/256)E(u)$ for~$u=G_Ef$, once the errors they introduce are controlled. Density extends this inequality to the common domain, contradicting the optimality of~$M$. If the complementary set of~$L$-adic depths has upper density at least~$\nf12$, then we interchange the forms and improve the optimal upper constant~$1/m$ instead. Both the energy saved by the replacements and the approximation error are proportional to~$\Delta$, so the tolerances can be chosen independently of its size.

\smallskip

With~$\beta$ and~$\alpha$ as fixed in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, choose~$\gamma$ and~$\zeta$ satisfying~\eqref{eq:mfd-33}. For~$L=3^{H_1}$ as in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-affine}, set~$b=H_1-\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor-4$. Then~$b\geq1$,~$H_1\geq b+2$ and~$3^{-b}Lr$ is the side of the cube concentric with~$q$ used for the harmonic comparison in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-affine}. With the dimensional constant~$C_d\geq1$ of the next lemma, the closure of this cube lies in~$p$ for every child at distance at least~$C_d3^{-b}$ times the side of~$p$ from~$\partial p$, while~$C_d3^{-b}\leq81C_dL^{\gamma-1}$ tends to zero as~$L$ grows. Averaging over finitely many translated grids makes the mass of the children close to the boundary of their parent small, for every measure.

\begin{lemma}[Shifted rational grids]
\label{mfd:lem-shifts}
Let~$L=3^{H_1}$ with~$H_1\geq b+2$, and set~$\mathsf M=3^b+1$. Translate the nested~$L$-adic grid by the shifts~$\mathcal S=\{0,1/\mathsf M,\ldots,(\mathsf M-1)/\mathsf M\}^d$. Here~$\mathcal S$ denotes this finite set of grid shifts; in Definition~\ref{d.mathcalS.def} it denotes the induction assertion. Consider the children at depth~$n$ whose closure is within distance~$C_d3^{-b}L^{1-n}$ of the boundary of their parent. The dimensional constant in the counting bounds below may be larger than the constant~$C_d$ in this distance. Then, for every~$x$ and every~$n\geq1$,
\begin{multline*}
	\mathsf M^{-d}\#\{\sigma\in\mathcal S:\ \text{the depth-$n$ child of }x\text{ in grid }\sigma\text{ is within distance }C_d3^{-b}L^{1-n}
	\\
	\text{of the boundary of its parent}\}\leq2d\Bigl(C_d3^{-b}+\frac1L+\frac1{\mathsf M}\Bigr)
	\leq C_d3^{-b}\,.
\end{multline*}
Consequently, for every finite Borel measure~$\mu$ on~$Q$, the average over~$\sigma\in\mathcal S$ of the mass of the cells at depth~$n$ within distance~$C_d3^{-b}L^{1-n}$ of the boundary of their parent is at most~$C_d3^{-b}\mu(Q)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix a coordinate~$i$. Relative to its parent at depth~$n-1$, the point~$x$ has fractional coordinate~$\{L^{n-1}(x_i-\sigma_i)\}$. Since~$\gcd(L,\mathsf M)=1$, multiplication by~$L^{n-1}$ permutes the residues modulo~$\mathsf M$. As~$\sigma_i$ ranges over the~$\mathsf M$ shifts, these fractional coordinates form an equally spaced set of~$\mathsf M$ points with a common rotation. If the child is within this distance of the boundary of its parent in coordinate~$i$, then~$x_i$ is within~$(C_d3^{-b}L+1)L^{-n}$ of a face of the parent. Its fractional coordinate then lies in two intervals of total length at most~$2C_d3^{-b}+2/L$. A rotated equally spaced set has at most~$2\mathsf M(C_d3^{-b}+1/L)+2$ points in these two intervals. We divide by~$\mathsf M$, sum over the~$d$ coordinates, and integrate the pointwise bound against~$\mu$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Good cells carry a fixed fraction of the mass]
\label{mfd:lem-mass}
Suppose that~$\mathcal U$ has upper density at least~$\nf12$. Fix~$f\in C_c^\infty(Q)$ and~$c>0$, put~$u=G_Ef$ and~$\mu=\Gamma_E(u)+c\,dx$, let~$\epsilon_{\rm coll}>0$, and assume that~$\theta+L^{-\zeta}+C_d3^{-b}+\epsilon_{\rm coll}\leq\nf18$. Then, at arbitrarily large~$L$-adic depths~$n\in\mathcal U$, there are shifts~$\sigma\in\mathcal S$ and finite families~$\mathcal Q$ of cells at depth~$n$ in the grid~$\sigma$ such that~$\mu(\bigcup\mathcal Q)\geq\mu(Q)/8$. Every cell~$q\in\mathcal Q$, of side~$r$ and with parent~$p$, is good, satisfies~$\mu(p)\leq L^{d+\zeta}\mu(q)$, and has its concentric enlargement of side~$3^{\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+4}r$ with closure in~$p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
At one~$L$-adic depth, a child that violates~$\mu(p)\leq L^{d+\zeta}\mu(q)$ has mass less than~$L^{-(d+\zeta)}\mu(p)$, and each parent has at most~$L^d$ children. Thus the total mass of these children is at most~$L^{-\zeta}\mu(Q)$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-24} for each of the~$\mathsf M^d$ grids, the mass of the bad cells over~$J$~$L$-adic depths is at most~$(\theta J+B_\omega^\sigma)\mu(Q)$. The maximum over the finitely many grids of the random constants~$B^\sigma_\omega$ is finite on the intersection of the events of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} for these grids. After division by~$J$, this maximum is absorbed in the~$o(1)$ below. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-shifts}, the mass of the cells within distance~$C_d3^{-b}L^{1-n}$ of the boundary of their parent, averaged over~$\sigma$, is at most~$C_d3^{-b}\mu(Q)$ at every depth.

\smallskip

We omit a neighbourhood of~$\partial Q$ of mass at most~$\epsilon_{\rm coll}\mu(Q)$. At sufficiently large~$L$-adic depths, the parents of the remaining points lie inside~$Q$. Along depths~$J$ with~$\#(\mathcal U\cap\{1,\ldots,J\})\geq J/2-o(J)$, we sum the mass of the good cells with enlargement in the parent that satisfy the bound on their parents over~$n\in\mathcal U\cap\{1,\ldots,J\}$ and~$\sigma\in\mathcal S$, and divide by~$J|\mathcal S|$. This normalized sum is at least
\begin{equation*}
	\Bigl[\frac12-\epsilon_{\rm coll}-\theta-L^{-\zeta}-C_d3^{-b}
	-o(1)\Bigr]\mu(Q)>\frac{\mu(Q)}{4}
\end{equation*}
for large~$J$. A finite average that exceeds~$\mu(Q)/4$ has a summand that exceeds it, and since every fixed finite set of depths contributes~$o(J)$, such depths can be taken arbitrarily large.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Harmonic replacement]
\label{mfd:lem-replace}
Let~$u\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ and let~$\mathcal Q$ be a finite disjoint family of cells of side at most~$h$, whose threefold enlargements lie in~$Q$. Assume that~$u|_{\partial q}\in C^\alpha$ for every~$q\in\mathcal Q$, with~$\alpha>1/2$, and let~$u^{\mathcal Q}$ be the function equal to the~$E$-harmonic extension of~$u|_{\partial q}$ on each~$q\in\mathcal Q$ and to~$u$ elsewhere. Then~$u-u^{\mathcal Q}$ is the~$E$-orthogonal projection of~$u$ onto~$\bigoplus_qD(E^q)$, and~$E(u)=E(u^{\mathcal Q})+E(u-u^{\mathcal Q})$. If moreover~$u=G_Ef$, then~$E(u-u^{\mathcal Q})\leq Kh^{\nf32}\|f\|_2^2$. In particular,~$\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}[\Gamma_E(u)(q)-\Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q})] =E(u-u^{\mathcal Q})\to0$ as~$h\to0$, uniformly over the choice of~$\mathcal Q$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The Euler equations and strong locality identify~$w=u-u^{\mathcal Q}$ as the stated orthogonal projection and give both energy identities. Recovery sequences and~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} give~$\|v\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^2\leq KE(v)$; they also show that~$D(E^q)\subset H^{\nf34}(q)$ on each cell and that its functions have zero trace on~$\partial q$. The fractional Poincar\'e inequality for zero-trace functions, rescaled to~$q$, gives
\begin{equation*}
	\|w\|_2^2
	\leq Ch^{\nf32}\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}[w]_{H^{\nf34}(q)}^2
	\leq Ch^{\nf32}[w]_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^2
	\leq Kh^{\nf32}E(w)\,.
\end{equation*}
Here the seminorm is the Gagliardo double integral. Finally, for~$u=G_Ef$,~$E(w)=E(u,w)=\langle f,w\rangle\leq\|f\|_2\|w\|_2$, which proves the estimate.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proportionality]
\label{mfd:thm-prop}
The optimal comparison constants coincide:~$\Delta=0$. Hence there exists a deterministic~$c\in[C_0^{-1},C_0]$ such that
\begin{equation}
	F=cE\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-36}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Suppose for a contradiction that~$\Delta>0$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We show that~$\mathcal U$ having upper density at least~$\nf12$ contradicts the definition of~$M$. Fix a cube~$Q$ in~$\mathcal C$,~$u=G_Ef$ with smooth~$f$,~$c>0$,~$\mu=\Gamma_E(u)+c\,dx$ and choose~$\varrho\leq3/1024$ in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-affine}. Choose the parameters in the order above, enlarging~$L$ so that~$\theta+L^{-\zeta}+C_d3^{-b}+\epsilon_{\rm coll}\leq\nf18$ with~$b=H_1-\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor-4$, before fixing~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ and~$\gmcdelta$. Let~$\mathcal Q$ be the family of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-mass} at a sufficiently large~$L$-adic depth, with maximal side~$h$. For each cell~$q$ of this family, the triangle inequalities for~$\sqrt{B_q}$ and~$\sqrt{\Lambda_{E,q}}$, applied to~$u|_{\partial q}=\ell_q+(u|_{\partial q}-\ell_q)$, give~$B_q(u|_{\partial q})\geq\Delta[\frac{3}{16}\Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q})-4\varrho\mu(q)]$, by~\eqref{eq:mfd-34}. Summing over~$q\in\mathcal Q$ and using Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-replace}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
	\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}B_q(u|_{\partial q})
	&\geq\Delta\Bigl[\frac{3}{16}\sum_q\Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q})-4\varrho\mu(Q)\Bigr]\\
	&\geq\Delta\Bigl[\frac{3}{16}\Gamma_E(u)\bigl(\textstyle\bigcup\mathcal Q\bigr)
	-\frac{3}{16}Kh^{\nf32}\|f\|_2^2-4\varrho(E(u)+c|Q|)\Bigr]\,.
\end{align*}
Since~$\mu(\bigcup\mathcal Q)\geq\mu(Q)/8$, we have~$\Gamma_E(u)(\bigcup\mathcal Q)\geq E(u)/8-7c|Q|/8$, and with~$\varrho\leq3/1024$ this gives
\begin{equation*}
	\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}B_q(u|_{\partial q})\geq\Delta\bigl[\frac{3}{256}E(u)-Cc|Q|-o_h(1)\bigr]\,.
\end{equation*}
Now~$u\in D(F)$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-26}. Let~$v$ be the function equal to the~$F$-harmonic extension of~$u|_{\partial q}$ on each~$q\in\mathcal Q$ and to~$u$ elsewhere. By strong locality and the bound~$\Gamma_F\leq M\Gamma_E$ off the cells,
\begin{align*}
	F(v)
	&\leq M\Gamma_E(u)\bigl(Q\setminus\textstyle\bigcup\mathcal Q\bigr)+\sum_q\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})
	\notag \\ &
	=ME(u)-\sum_q\bigl[M\Gamma_E(u)(q)-\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})\bigr]\leq ME(u)-\sum_qB_q(u|_{\partial q})\,,
\end{align*}
where the last inequality uses~$\Gamma_E(u)(q)\geq \Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q})$. As~$h\to0$ along the~$L$-adic depths of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-mass}, the maximum principle bounds~$\|v-u\|_\infty$ by the modulus of continuity of~$u$ at scale~$h$, which tends to zero. Thus~$v\to u$ in~$L^2$. Lower semicontinuity of~$F$ gives~$F(u)\leq(M-3\Delta/256)E(u)+C\Delta c|Q|$.

\smallskip

The tolerances and~$\epsilon_{\rm coll}$ do not depend on~$c$. Letting~$c\downarrow0$ gives~$F(u)\leq(M-3\Delta/256)E(u)$ for every~$u=G_E^Qf$ with smooth~$f$, on every cube~$Q$ in~$\mathcal C$. Step~4 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density} extends the bound to~$D(E^Q)$ for every cube~$Q$ of~$\mathcal C$. This contradicts the definition of~$M$ as an infimum over all these cubes.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We show that the complement of~$\mathcal U$ having upper density at least~$\nf12$ contradicts the definition of~$m$. On good cells at~$L$-adic depths outside~$\mathcal U$, we have~$\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)\geq(c_k-\epsilon\Delta)\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)\geq(m+3\Delta/8)\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)$. We regard~$F$ as the first form and~$E$ as the second. The comparison constants of~$E$ relative to~$F$ are~$1/M\leq1/m$, with difference~$1/m-1/M$, and the gain at affine boundary values is at least~$(3/8)(1/m-1/M)$, because~$m+3\Delta/8\leq M$. The argument of Step~1, with the solutions~$G_Ff$ for smooth~$f$, improves the optimal upper comparison constant~$1/m$ of this pair, which contradicts the optimality of~$m$.

\smallskip

Hence~$\Delta=0$, so~$F=mE$, and~$c=m$ is deterministic by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-endpoints}.
\end{proof}

We now show that the proportionality constant~$c$ in Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-prop} is one, using the fact that the homogenized matrix of the stationary coefficients~$A_N^0$ is the identity.

\subsection{Determination of the proportionality constant}
\label{mfd:sec-scalar}

The coefficients~$A_N^0$ of~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization} are stationary, have range of dependence at most~$\sqrt d$, and have homogenized matrix~$I$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization}.

\begin{theorem}[Uniform qualitative homogenization]
\label{mfd:thm-eta}
For every unit vector~$e$,
\begin{equation*}
	\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_N\E J(\cu_k,e,e;A_N^0)=0\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The proof proceeds in three steps, and the quantity we track is the sum~$f_N(k)$ of the expectations~$\E J(\cu_k,e_j,e_j;A_N^0)$ over the coordinate directions, defined in Step~1. Step~1 proves uniform moment bounds and chooses cutoffs~$N_k$ for which~$f_{N_k}$ changes little between the scales~$k$ and~$4k$. Step~2 extracts a subsequence of these cutoffs along which the expectations of the coarse-grained matrices~$A_{N_k}^0(\cu_n)$ converge to a deterministic matrix~$P$, and uses the finite range of dependence to show that, along it, the matrices~$(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_n)$ concentrate around a deterministic matrix~$R$. Step~3 uses the estimates of Section~\ref{s.self.similar} to show that~$P=R^{-1}$, so that~$P=R=I$. The heart of the matter is Step~3, where the small gaps in the subadditivity inequality obtained in Step~1 force the two limits to agree.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We obtain uniform moment bounds and choose cutoffs for which the expected energy changes little across a long interval of scales. Fix a sufficiently large even integer~$\xi$. Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, subadditivity~\eqref{e.subadditivity.J} and stationarity give~$\sup_{N,k\geq0}\|\sup_{|e|=1}J(\cu_k,e,e;A_N^0)\|_{L^\xi}<\infty$. Moreover, the family
\begin{equation*}
	\left\{\Lambda_{1/4}(\cu_k;A_N^0)+\lambda_{1/4}(\cu_k;A_N^0)^{-1}:N,k\geq0\right\}
\end{equation*}
is tight. This follows from Lemma~\ref{l.multiscale.response.large.cubes}, with~$s=\nf18$,~$r=2$ and the initial error~$C\xi\log(2+\xi)\gmcdelta^2$, followed by~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} and weighted Cauchy--Schwarz. Choose~$\xi$ large enough for that lemma and then reduce~$\gmcdelta$.

\smallskip

Write
\begin{equation*}
	f_N(k)=\sum_{j=1}^d\E J(\cu_k,e_j,e_j;A_N^0)
	=\frac12\E\operatorname{tr}\bigl(A_N^0(\cu_k)+(A_N^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_k)-2I\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{e.subadditivity.a} and~\eqref{e.annealed.matrices.def},~$\E A_N^0(\cu_k)$ and~$\E(A_N^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_k)$ decrease to~$I$. Thus~$f_N(k)\geq0$ decreases in~$k$, and the limit of~$\sup_N f_N(k)$ as~$k\to\infty$ exists. Choose~$N_k$ with~$f_{N_k}(4k)\geq\sup_N f_N(4k)-k^{-1}$. Then
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_{k\leq n\leq4k}\bigl(f_{N_k}(n)-f_{N_k}(4k)\bigr)
	\leq\sup_N f_N(k)-\sup_N f_N(4k)+k^{-1}\longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation*}

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We show that the coarse-grained matrices~$(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_n)$ concentrate and that the expectations of the coarse-grained matrices~$A_{N_k}^0(\cu_n)$ converge along these scales. By subadditivity, the average of~$(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(z+\cu_k)$ over the scale-$k$ subcubes of~$\cu_n$, minus~$(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_n)$, is positive semidefinite and has expected trace at most~$2(f_{N_k}(k)-f_{N_k}(n))$. This difference tends to zero in~$L^1$, uniformly for~$k\leq n\leq4k$. The moment bound of Step~1 and~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.matrix.deviation.by.J} upgrade this to~$L^2$. For~$n\geq2k$, the variance of the average is at most~$C3^{-d(n-k)}$, because the finite range of dependence splits the subcubes into a dimensional number of classes of independent cubes. Consequently, after extracting a subsequence, there are deterministic matrices~$P,R\geq I$ such that
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_{k\leq n\leq4k}\bigl\|\E A_{N_k}^0(\cu_n)-P\bigr\|\longrightarrow0\,,
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_{2k\leq n\leq4k}\bigl\|(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_n)-R\bigr\|_{L^2}\longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation*}
For the first convergence, extract a limit of~$\E A_{N_k}^0(\cu_{4k})$ and note that~$\E A_{N_k}^0(\cu_n)-\E A_{N_k}^0(\cu_{4k})$ is positive semidefinite with trace at most~$2(f_{N_k}(n)-f_{N_k}(4k))$, which tends to zero by Step~1.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We use the estimates of Section~\ref{s.self.similar} to show that both limiting matrices equal the identity. Fix~$p\in\Rd$ and let~$v=v(\cdot,\cu_{4k},p,R^{-1}p;A_{N_k}^0)$. Apply~\eqref{e.Besov.norms.gradient} with~$s=\nf14$ and~$L_0=2k$. The expected gaps in the subadditivity inequality~\eqref{e.subadditivity.a} tend to zero uniformly over~$2k\leq n\leq4k$ by Step~1. The mean-square differences of the coarse-grained matrices~$(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_n)$ tend to zero by Step~2 and stationarity. The weights of the two sums in~\eqref{e.Besov.norms.gradient} are summable, and~$(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_{4k})R^{-1}p-p\to0$ in~$L^2$. Since the coarse-grained ellipticity constants are tight, we obtain~$3^{-4k}\|\nabla v\|_{\Hminusul(\cu_{4k})}\to0$ in probability. Now~\eqref{e.J.L.bound}, with the same tight constants and the vanishing gaps in the subadditivity inequality, gives~$J(\cu_{4k-1},p,R^{-1}p;A_{N_k}^0)\to0$ in probability. The moment bounds make these energies uniformly integrable, so their expectations tend to zero. By~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.matrices}, the limit of these expectations is also~$\frac12p\cdot(P-R^{-1})p$. Thus~$P=R^{-1}$. Since~$P,R\geq I$, both equal~$I$, and~$f_{N_k}(4k)\to0$. The choice of~$N_k$ then shows that the limit of~$\sup_Nf_N(k)$ is zero.
\end{proof}
