The energy identity follows by changing variables~$x=z+ry$. Stationarity, scaling and independence show that~$\Theta_{k,z}$ preserves the joint field law. The same transformed environment serves every cutoff. In particular, dividing the two energies at cutoffs~$N$ and~$M$ by their respective scalars~$s_N(q)$ and~$s_M(q)$ cancels those scalars exactly and gives the two unit-cube energies at cutoffs~$N-k$ and~$M-k$ in this one environment.

\smallskip

To prove that the normalizations converge, fix a nonconstant affine datum. Both minimal energies in the identity converge in probability, and their limits are strictly positive. By~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering}, for fixed~$k$ the deterministic ratios~$\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N$ lie in~$[1,e^{2\tau^2k}]$, a compact subset of~$(0,\infty)$. Two subsequential limits of these ratios must coincide, since otherwise the same positive minimal energy of the limit form would equal one random variable multiplied by two different constants. Finally, we pass to the limit in the identity for the countable family of smooth traces of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension} gives the identity for every trace on which the minimal energy of the limit form is defined.
\end{proof}

We compare the two cutoffs on a cell~$q$ through the trace on~$\partial q$ of a single function, which is H\"older continuous but otherwise arbitrary. The next lemma reduces this comparison to finitely many smooth traces.

\begin{lemma}[Reduction to finitely many smooth boundary values]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests}
Let~$1/2<\beta<\alpha<1$,~$A<\infty$ and~$\eta>0$. There exists a finite family of smooth functions on~$\partial \cu_0$ with the following property. Let~$\Lambda_1$ and~$\Lambda_2$ be minimal energies on~$\cu_0$ with given boundary values such that~$\Lambda_i(g)\leq A\|g\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}^2$ for~$i\in\{1,2\}$ and every~$g\in C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)$, and such that~$|\Lambda_1(h)-\Lambda_2(h)|\leq\eta/2$ for every function~$h$ of the family. Then, for every~$g\in C^\alpha(\partial \cu_0)$,
\begin{equation*}
	|\Lambda_1(g)-\Lambda_2(g)|
	\leq\eta\|g\|_{C^\alpha/\mathbb R}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Since~$\Lambda_i^{\nf12}$ is a seminorm, the triangle inequality gives, for all~$g,h\in C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)$,
\begin{equation*}
	|\Lambda_i(g)-\Lambda_i(h)|
	\leq A\|g-h\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}
	\bigl(\|g\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}+\|h\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
The unit ball of~$C^\alpha/\mathbb R$ is precompact in~$C^\beta/\mathbb R$, so for every tolerance it has a finite net consisting of smooth functions. Take the family to be a sufficiently fine such net, and apply the last display to~$\Lambda_1$ and to~$\Lambda_2$. This gives the assertion for~$g$ in the unit ball, and homogeneity gives it for every~$g$.
\end{proof}

The number of functions in the family and their smooth norms may depend on~$\eta$; the exponents and the moment orders do not. The constant~$A$ is the one in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}(iii) below, and it is fixed before the family is chosen.

\smallskip

On a good cell we also need, at a finite cutoff, the local H\"older estimate and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values that Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-goodext} provides for the limit form. The scalar~$s_N(q)$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations} is the finite-cutoff analogue of the scalar~$s_E(q)$ of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare}.

\begin{lemma}[Good cells at finite cutoff]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}
Fix~$1/2<\beta<\alpha<1$, a subdivision factor~$L$, a distance factor~$b_0\geq3$ and a rate~$B>0$ for the exponential bounds in~(i). The thresholds for the sums over intervals of scales, the finite moment orders and~$\gmcdelta_0>0$ can be chosen in this order so that the following holds for~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$. There are deterministic constants~$C,A<\infty$, independent of the cutoff, the cell, the source and the tolerance~$\eta$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests}, and good events~$G_N(q)$ with the following properties. Here~$N\geq k\geq0$,~$q=z+r\cu_0$ with~$r=3^{-k}$, and, after~$q$ is rescaled to~$\cu_0$, the coefficient has cutoff~$m=N-k$. The conclusions hold on the same good events both for~$A_N$ and for~$A_N^0=e^{-H}A_N$, with their respective scalars~$s_N(q)$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations} and~$s_N^0(q)=e^{-H(z)}s_N(q)$. Below,~$s(q)$ denotes the scalar of the coefficient under consideration, and~$\Gamma(u)$ denotes the energy measure of~$u$ for that coefficient.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item Up to a null set, the complement of~$G_N(q)$ is covered by events indexed by integers~$h\geq1$, the event of index~$h$ being measurable with respect to~$\sigma(\g_{-j}:k-h\leq j\leq k+2h)$ and having probability at most~$e^{-Bh}$.
\item Suppose~$G_N(q)$ holds and~$q$ lies in its parent~$p$ of side~$Lr$ with~$\operatorname{dist}(q,\partial p)\geq b_0r$. For every~$f\in L^\infty(p)$ and every weak solution~$u\in H^1(p)$ of~$-\nabla\cdot(A\nabla u)=f$ on~$p$, there is a constant~$c_q$ such that
\begin{equation}
	\|u(z+r\,\cdot)-c_q\|_{C^\alpha(\cu_0)}
	\leq C r^{(2-d)/2}s(q)^{-\nf12}\Gamma(u)(p)^{\nf12}
	+Cr^2s(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-finite-good-holder}
\end{equation}
\item On~$G_N(q)$, for every~$g\in C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)$, the normalized minimal energy satisfies
\begin{equation*}
	r^{2-d}s(q)^{-1}\Lambda_{N,q}\bigl(g((\,\cdot-z)/r)\bigr)
	\leq A\|g\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The proof proceeds in four steps. Step~1 defines~$G_N(q)$ and proves~(i) for it. Step~2 bounds the normalized coefficient and its gradient on~$q$ and its fixed enlargement. Step~3 proves~(ii), by the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} above the smallest field scale and an interior Sobolev estimate below it. Step~4 adds condition~(a), proves~(iii) and intersects the good events of the two coefficients. The main point is Step~3: the growth of the coefficient bounds of Step~2 below the smallest field scale must be absorbed by the gain~$\alpha_1-\alpha$ of the H\"older exponent, which fixes how small~$\sigma_*$ and~$\tau^2$ must be.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We define the event~$G_N(q)$. It imposes the conditions of~$G(q)$ on sums over intervals of scales, down to the smallest field scale, with an exponent~$\alpha_1\in(\alpha,1)$ in place of~$\alpha$ in the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC}. We choose the integrability exponent used in Step~3 so that the Sobolev embedding gives a H\"older exponent larger than~$\alpha_1$, then a small test exponent~$\sigma_*\in(0,\nf1{32}]$, and add the root and enlargement tests
\begin{equation*}
	\mathsf F_{m+\iota,\,3^Nz}\leq\epsilon,\qquad
	\mathsf P_{m+\iota,\,3^Nz}\leq12
	\qquad(\iota=0,1),\qquad m=N-k\,.
\end{equation*}
These are the quantities of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}, evaluated in the relabelled fields~$\g_i^{(N)}(y)=\g_{i-N}(3^{-N}y)$ with~$s=\sigma_*$. The index~$m$ belongs to~$q$, and~$m+1$ to its fixed concentric enlargement~$3q$; their stationary centers are both~$3^Nz$. In the original bilateral indexing their field windows are centered at~$-k$ and~$1-k$, respectively.

\smallskip

For these extra tests use the continuous ramps of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}, with threshold pairs~$(\epsilon/2,\epsilon)$ and~$(6,12)$; they are two of the three summands of~$Z_{m+\iota,\,3^Nz}$. Their zero-disorder values are zero. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-band} approximates them by quantities depending on windows about the same root indices, with exponentially decreasing errors, uniformly in~$N$ and~$k$. Its proof applies verbatim with~$s=\sigma_*$, because the exponent~$s\in(0,\nf1{32}]$ of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches} may be any number fixed before~$\gmcdelta$. The proof also gives an~$L^p$ bound of order~$C_p\gmcdelta^c$ for these ramps. The telescoping argument of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness}, applied to each ramp at this one cutoff, therefore covers its failure by events with any prescribed exponential rate after the moment orders are fixed and~$\gmcdelta$ is reduced. Enlarge and reindex the windows to absorb their fixed widths and the offset~$1$, and increase the rate before taking the finite union. All these events then have the windows and the probability bound in~(i), centered at the physical root depth~$k$, uniformly in~$m$. For the conditions on sums over intervals of~$D\leq m$ scales, we apply, at the single fixed cutoff~$N$, the telescoping argument from the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness}, which uses dyadically increasing intervals of field indices. Lemmas~\ref{mfd:in-prefix} and~\ref{mfd:lem-band} are uniform at that cutoff, so the union over the at most~$Ce^{CD}$ spatial choices has the same bound. Choose the moment order used in the approximation so large that the resulting exponential rate exceeds both~$B$ and the exponential growth rate of the number of spatial choices, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$. This gives~(i) directly at finite cutoff.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We bound the coefficient on the cell and its fixed enlargement. Write~$y_0=3^Nz$ for the stationary center. The term~$j=m$ in the root product test gives
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_{y\in y_0+\cu_{m+1}}\sum_{i=0}^{m}|\g_i^{(N)}(y)|
	\leq\log12+\frac{\sigma_* m}{8}\log3\,.
\end{equation*}
Indeed, the first product in that term is bounded by~$12\cdot3^{\sigma_* m/8}$ on the larger cube~$y_0+\cu_{2m+1}$, and includes all indices~$0,\ldots,m$. The term~$j=0$ in the same test bounds
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_{y\in y_0+\cu_{m+1}}
	\sum_{i=m+1}^{\infty}
	|\g_i^{(N)}(y)-\g_i^{(N)}(y_0)|
	\leq\frac14\log12\,.
\end{equation*}
For~$A_N^0$ this sum stops at~$i=N$, so the same bound applies. The identity of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations}, with the respective scalar~$s(q)$, expresses the logarithm of the normalized coefficient as the sum of the fine fields~$i=0,\ldots,m$ and these coarse-field increments, minus~$\log\kappa_m$. Here~$\kappa_m=e^{(m+1)\tau^2}\ahom_m$. Since~$e^{-2\tau^2}\leq\ahom_0\leq1$ by~\eqref{e.ord.bounds.truncated} and Jensen's inequality,~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering} gives~$e^{-2\tau^2(m+1)}\leq\ahom_m\leq1$, hence~$|\log\kappa_m|\leq\tau^2(m+1)$. Thus its logarithm is bounded by~$C+C(\sigma_*+\tau^2)m$, for both coefficients.

\smallskip

The root field test controls the gradients separately. Its term~$j=m$ bounds the sum of~$3^i|\nabla\g_i^{(N)}|$ for~$0\leq i\leq m$ by~$3^{\sigma_* m/8}$. For~$i=m+j>m$, its term of depth~$j$ gives~$3^i\|\nabla\g_i^{(N)}\|_\infty\leq3^{\sigma_* j/8}$ on~$y_0+\cu_{m+1}$. Hence
\begin{equation*}
	\sum_{i=m+1}^{\infty}
	\|\nabla\g_i^{(N)}\|_{L^\infty(y_0+\cu_{m+1})}
	\leq3^{-m}\sum_{j\geq1}3^{-(1-\sigma_*/8)j}\,.
\end{equation*}
This series converges, also bounding every partial infrared sum. In unit coordinates~$x$, the stationary coordinate is~$y_0+3^mx$, and the smallest field scale is~$3^{-m}$. Denoting the coefficient in these unit coordinates divided by~$s(q)$ by~$\widetilde A$, the value and gradient bounds give
\begin{equation*}
	\|\widetilde A^{-1}\|_\infty+\|\widetilde A\|_\infty
	+3^{-m}\|\nabla\log\widetilde A\|_\infty
	\leq C3^{C(\sigma_*+\tau^2)m}\,.
\end{equation*}
The norms here are on~$3\cu_0$. The same estimate holds with~$H$ omitted or replaced by any of its partial sums, since all omitted terms were bounded in absolute value.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We prove~(ii). Above the scale~$3^{-m}$, the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} on the event controlling sums over intervals of scales gives~\eqref{eq:mfd-finite-good-holder} with exponent~$\alpha_1$. Below~$3^{-m}$, we use the interior Sobolev estimate, with this exponent, of Step~4 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} on balls of radius~$a3^{-m}/[C(1+3^{-m}\|\nabla\log\widetilde A\|_\infty)]$, on which the oscillation of~$\log\widetilde A$ is small. After matching with the estimate at scale~$3^{-m}$, the energy term carries the factor~$3^{-m(\alpha_1-\alpha)}$ and the source term the factor~$3^{-m(2-\alpha)}$, each multiplied only by fixed powers of the quantities in the last display. We choose~$\sigma_*$, and then~$\tau^2$, so that~$C(\sigma_*+\tau^2)<\alpha_1-\alpha$, where~$C$ depends only on~$d$ and the integrability exponent. The net exponents of~$3^{-m}$ then remain positive, and~\eqref{eq:mfd-finite-good-holder} holds at all radii with a deterministic constant.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We prove~(iii) and obtain one good event for both coefficients. First intersect the event of Step~1 with condition~(a) on~$q$ and its fixed enlargements, using the finite-cutoff ellipticity bounds of~$G(q)$ at the trace exponent of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}. These normalized ellipticity conditions have the centered moment and band bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-band}, also with zero infrared potential. Their failure is covered at the fixed cutoff by the finite-test telescoping argument of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness}. Add these covers to those of Step~1, using a larger rate before the finite union. Thus~(i) covers condition~(a) as well. On that condition, Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension} gives~(iii), with deterministic~$A$ independent of the trace-net tolerance. Finally, we apply the construction separately to the coefficient with and without its factor~$e^H$, and take the intersection of the two good events. When~$H$ is replaced by one of its partial sums, the same bounds hold uniformly in the number of terms, by the summable bounds on the increments of the fields of scale larger than one.
\end{proof}

The comparison of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests} on a cell holds when the normalized minimal energies at the two cutoffs are close on the finite family of smooth boundary values of that lemma. By the convergence in probability, this comparison fails only with small probability, but the counting argument of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches} requires more: the failure must be covered by events of exponentially small probability, each measurable with respect to the fields with indices in a short interval.

\begin{lemma}[Rare events and field indices]
\label{mfd:lem-rare-tests}
For each~$m\in\N$, let~$I_m$ be a countable index set and~$X_{m,i}$,~$i\in I_m$, random variables with values in~$[0,1]$. Assume that they tend to zero in probability uniformly in~$i$, that is,~$\sup_{i\in I_m}\P(X_{m,i}>\varepsilon)\to0$ as~$m\to\infty$, for every~$\varepsilon>0$. For each~$(m,i)$ let~$n_{m,i}$ be its deterministic field index, and let~$\mathcal B_{m,i,H}$ be the~$\sigma$-field of a nested interval of length~$C(H+1)$ about~$n_{m,i}$. Suppose that, uniformly in~$m,i,H$,
\begin{equation*}
	\|X_{m,i}-\E[X_{m,i}\mid\mathcal B_{m,i,H}]\|_{L^p}
	\leq C_*3^{-aH}\,.
\end{equation*}
There is an absolute constant~$C$ such that, for every~$B$ with~$ap\log3>CB$, there are common integers~$H_0,m_0$ with the following property. For every~$m\geq m_0$ and~$i\in I_m$, up to a null set,~$\{X_{m,i}>1/2\}$ is contained in a union of events indexed by~$h\geq H_0$. The event with index~$h$ is measurable with respect to fields in an interval of radius at most~$Ch$ about~$n_{m,i}$ and has probability at most~$e^{-Bh}$. The initial radius~$H_0$ depends on~$C_*$ and~$B$, but not on~$m,i$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix~$(m,i)$ and suppress~$i$ from the notation in this proof. Let~$H_0$ be large, to be chosen uniformly in~$m,i$, and put~$X_m^{(\ell)}=\E[X_{m,i}\mid\mathcal B_{m,i,2^\ell H_0}]$ for~$\ell\geq0$. Since the~$L^p$ errors of these approximations are summable in~$\ell$, we have~$X_m^{(\ell)}\to X_m$ almost surely. The allowances~$2^{-\ell-3}$,~$\ell\geq1$, sum to~$1/8$. Hence, outside the null set where this convergence fails, if~$X_m>1/2$, then either~$X_m^{(0)}>1/4$ or the increment~$X_m^{(\ell)}-X_m^{(\ell-1)}$ exceeds~$2^{-\ell-3}$ for some~$\ell\geq1$. By Markov's inequality, the probability of the first event is at most~$\P(X_m>1/8)+(8C_*)^p3^{-apH_0}$. We first choose~$H_0$ so large that the second term is smaller than~$e^{-CBH_0}$ with the required margin, and then use the uniform convergence in probability to make the first term smaller than~$e^{-CBH_0}$ for all~$m\geq m_0$. By Markov's inequality, the probability that the~$\ell$th increment exceeds its allowance is at most~$(C C_*)^p2^{p\ell}3^{-ap2^{\ell-1}H_0}$, and increasing~$H_0$ absorbs the factor~$2^{p\ell}$ uniformly in~$\ell$. Each of these events is measurable with respect to the fields indexed by the larger of the two intervals that determine the two conditional expectations in its definition. We index them by~$h=2^\ell H_0$ and enlarge their intervals to the form used in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness}; the assumed margin in~$ap\log3>CB$ absorbs this fixed enlargement.
\end{proof}

We verify the conditioning and convergence inputs for the finite trace tests. Let~$h_1,\ldots,h_J$ be the smooth family of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests}. Let~$\psi_\eta$ vanish on~$[0,\eta/4]$, equal one on~$[\eta/2,\infty)$ and be linear in between. For~$q=z+r\cu_0$,~$r=3^{-k}$, and~$M\geq N\geq k$, put
\begin{equation*}
	X_{N,M}(q)=\psi_\eta\left(\max_{i\leq J}
	\left|r^{2-d}s_N(q)^{-1}\Lambda_{N,q}\bigl(h_i((\cdot-z)/r)\bigr)
	-r^{2-d}s_M(q)^{-1}\Lambda_{M,q}\bigl(h_i((\cdot-z)/r)\bigr)\right|\right)\,.
\end{equation*}
By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations}, these are the responses at the two unit-cube cutoffs~$N-k$ and~$M-k$, in the same environment~$\Theta_{k,z}\omega$. No moment of either cell scalar enters this test.

\smallskip

Write~$\mathcal F_\ell=\sigma(\g_j:|j|\leq\ell)$ and~$\mathcal R_{m,i}=\Lambda_{m,\cu_0}(h_i)$. Extend~$h_i$ smoothly to~$\overline{\cu_0}$. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} supplies its Morrey bound, with a factor~$\|h_i\|_{C^2}^2$, and~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} supply its uniform response moments. Thus Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15} bounds the change caused by resampling~$\g_{-j}$ by~$C_{p,i}\gmcdelta3^{-bj/8}$ in~$L^p$ for~$0\leq j\leq m$, where~$b$ is the exponent of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-strips}; for~$j>m$ that field is absent. For~$j\geq1$, resampling~$\g_j$ changes the infrared potential by its increment about the origin, whose supremum has sub-Gaussian size~$C\gmcdelta3^{-j}$. The coefficient comparison~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult} and H\"older's inequality at the~$3p$ response order bound this change by~$C_{p,i}\gmcdelta3^{-j}$. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-resampling-sum} therefore gives, for every~$m,\ell\geq0$,
\begin{equation*}
	\|\mathcal R_{m,i}-\E[\mathcal R_{m,i}\mid\mathcal F_\ell]\|_{L^p}
	\leq C_{p,i}\gmcdelta\left(\sum_{j=\ell+1}^{m}3^{-bj/8}+\sum_{j>\ell}3^{-j}\right)
	\leq C_{p,i}\gmcdelta3^{-b\ell/8}\,.
\end{equation*}
The first sum is empty when~$\ell\geq m$. This is the proof of the Dirichlet branch of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-16}, extended to windows beyond the cutoff; its stated restriction~$m\geq\ell$ is unnecessary here.

\smallskip

The preimage of~$\mathcal F_\ell$ under~$\Theta_{k,z}$ is~$\mathcal B_{k,\ell}=\sigma(\g_{-j}:|j-k|\leq\ell)$. Conditional expectation commutes with this law-preserving change of environment. Apply~$\psi_\eta$ to the maximum of the differences of the two vectors of conditional expectations on this common window, obtaining a~$\mathcal B_{k,\ell}$-measurable variable~$Y$. The Lipschitz constant~$4/\eta$ of~$\psi_\eta$, the finite-maximum inequality in~$L^p$, and contraction of conditional expectation give
\begin{equation*}
	\|X_{N,M}(q)-\E[X_{N,M}(q)\mid\mathcal B_{k,\ell}]\|_{L^p}
	\leq \frac{16\gmcdelta}{\eta}
	\left(\sum_{i=1}^J C_{p,i}^p\right)^{1/p}3^{-b\ell/8}\,.
\end{equation*}
Enlarging the window multiplies this bound by at most two, by another use of contraction. The constants are independent of~$k,z,N,M$. Choose~$p\geq2$ so that~$(b/8)p\log3>CB$ for the counting rate~$B$, choose the finite higher orders needed for the Morrey, response and product bounds, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$. These orders and this disorder threshold are independent of~$\eta$ and of the smooth net. Only the norm factors~$C_{p,i}$, its size~$J$, and hence~$C_*$ and the initial window depend on~$\eta$.

\smallskip

Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-response-compact} and Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-c1} give full-sequence convergence of the unit-cube energies on the original field space. Their uniform higher moments give~$L^p$ convergence. The seminorm inequality for~$\Lambda_{m,\cu_0}^{\nf12}$ and~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} extend it from the dense trace family~$\mathcal D$ to each~$h_i$. Consequently, for every~$\varepsilon>0$,
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_{m'\geq m}\P\left[
	\psi_\eta\left(\max_{i\leq J}|\mathcal R_{m,i}-\mathcal R_{m',i}|\right)>\varepsilon
	\right]\longrightarrow0\qquad(m\to\infty)\,,
\end{equation*}
since~$\psi_\eta(x)>\varepsilon$ forces~$x>\eta/4$. The joint rescaling makes this uniform over cells with~$N-k\to\infty$. Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-rare-tests} with~$m=N-k$ and with the family indexed by the cells and~$M\geq N$. Its uniform conditioning estimate and uniform convergence in probability supply common initial windows and eventual remaining cutoffs for all these tests. It is applied here to the finite-cutoff~$A_N$ energies. The comparison for~$A_N^0$ is transferred in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-source-comparison}, on the cells at which the subdivision stops.

\smallskip

For an initial cube~$Q$ of the triadic subdivision, write its side length as~$3^{-k_Q}$ and define the eligible generations by~$I_Q^{a,b}(N)=\{n\in\mathbb N_0:aN\leq k_Q+H_1n\leq bN\}$. Thus the interval~$[aN,bN]$ always refers to physical triadic depth. Good and bad cells are counted by their generation indices in this set.

\begin{lemma}[Simultaneous good generations for two cutoffs]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-good-levels}
Fix~$0<a<b<1$,~$\theta\in(0,1)$,~$L=3^{H_1}$ and a bounded initial cube~$Q$ of the subdivision. Choose the finite moment orders and the threshold~$\gmcdelta_0$ as in Lemmas~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell} and~\ref{mfd:lem-rare-tests}, with the rate~$B$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}(i) large enough for the counting estimate in the proof. For every~$\eta>0$ there are~$N_\eta,C_\eta<\infty$ and~$c_\eta>0$ such that, for each pair~$M\geq N\geq N_\eta$, outside an event of probability at most~$C_\eta3^{-c_\eta N}$, every chain in the~$L$-adic subdivision of~$Q$ has at most~$\theta N/H_1$ bad generations with index in~$I_Q^{a,b}(N)$.

\smallskip

Here a generation~$n\in I_Q^{a,b}(N)$ of a chain is good if, on its cell of physical depth~$k_Q+H_1n$, Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell} holds at both cutoffs and the two separately normalized minimal energies for~$A_N$ satisfy the conclusion of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests} for every~$C^\alpha$ trace; otherwise it is bad. The comparison for~$A_N^0$ is obtained by the common-weight transfer in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-source-comparison}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The preceding application of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-rare-tests} and the covers of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}(i) at the two cutoffs cover each bad generation by events of the form in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}(i), with a prescribed rate~$B$, after increasing that rate by a fixed factor. For a cell of physical depth~$k$, the extra tests use stationary indices~$N-k$ and~$N-k+1$ at cutoff~$N$, and~$M-k$ and~$M-k+1$ at cutoff~$M$. Both pairs correspond to the same original bilateral indices~$-k$ and~$1-k$, so their covers have the same field windows at that cell. Their field windows are centered at~$-(k_Q+H_1n)$, for~$n\in I_Q^{a,b}(N)$, and these centers are spaced by~$H_1$. As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain}, apply Lemma~\ref{l.concentration.rare.intervals} on this interval of field indices, with the events at unselected indices set to the empty event. For sufficiently large~$N$, the eligible set has~$(b-a)N/H_1+O(1)$ elements and its last generation is~$(bN-k_Q)/H_1+O(1)$. Thus the number of chains ending there is at most~$C_Q L^{dbN/H_1}$, where the fixed root offset is absorbed in~$C_Q$. Choose~$B$ so that the exponential rate exceeds this growth and take a union bound. If~$\theta\geq b-a$, use the estimate with a smaller positive value of~$\theta$; it implies the stated bound.
\end{proof}

At physical triadic depth~$k=k_Q+H_1n$ the minimal energies at the cutoffs~$N$ and~$M$ carry the normalizations~$\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N$ and~$\ahom_{M-k}/\ahom_M$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations}, while the random factor in the scalars~$s_N(q)$ and~$s_M(q)$ is common to both. The ratio~$r_{N,M}(k)=(\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N)/(\ahom_{M-k}/\ahom_M)$ of these two normalizations is therefore, up to the tolerance~$\eta$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests}, the factor relating the two minimal energies on a good cell of depth~$k$. The next lemma adapts the stopping argument of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density} to two finite cutoffs.

\smallskip

For comparison on an arbitrary cube~$Q$ of side~$3^{-k_Q}$, first partition~$Q$ into triadic cubes~$R$ of common depth~$k_R=H_1\lceil k_Q/H_1\rceil$. This uses fewer than~$H_1$ preliminary triadic subdivisions and gives finitely many roots, independently of~$N$ and~$M$. Subdivide each~$R$~$L$-adically. All these roots have the same eligible set~$I_R^{1/4,3/4}(N)$, and generation~$n$ in any of them has physical depth~$k_R+H_1n$. For large~$N$, writing~$j=k_R/H_1+n$ identifies this set exactly with~$\{j\in\mathbb N_0:N\leq4H_1j\leq3N\}$. The same preliminary partition and the same eligible set are used in both comparisons below. At these depths~$r_{N,M}(k_R+H_1n)=r_{N,M}(H_1j)$. For each fixed cube and all sufficiently large~$N$, its eligible generations therefore correspond to the same set of absolute indices~$j$ with~$N\leq4H_1j\leq3N$.

\begin{lemma}[Comparison of two cutoffs by stopping]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-stopping}
Let~$\vartheta>0$. The geometry, the exponents and the finitely many moment orders can be chosen so that there exists~$\gmcdelta_0>0$ such that, if~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then the following holds with constants~$D$,~$L$ and~$C$ independent of~$\eta$,~$c$,~$N$ and~$M$. Let~$Q$ be a fixed cube of triadic side length and~$g$ a fixed smooth function on~$\partial Q$. For every~$\eta>0$ there exist~$C_\eta<\infty$ and~$\gamma_\eta>0$ with the following property. Let~$N\leq M$ be cutoffs and~$c\leq2$. Use the preliminary roots~$R$ just described, and assume that a deterministic set~$S\subseteq I_R^{1/4,3/4}(N)$ satisfies~$\#S\geq\vartheta\#I_R^{1/4,3/4}(N)$ and~$r_{N,M}(k_R+H_1n)\leq c$ for every~$n\in S$. Then, outside an event of probability at most~$C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}$,
\begin{equation*}
	\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)
	\leq c(1+C\eta L^D)\Lambda_{M,Q}(g)
	+C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}\|g\|_{C^2}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
Under the same assumptions with~$r_{N,M}^{-1}$ in place of~$r_{N,M}$, the reverse inequality holds, with~$\Lambda_{N,Q}$ and~$\Lambda_{M,Q}$ interchanged.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We define where the subdivision stops and estimate the energy on the cells at which it stops. Let~$u$ be the~$E_M$-harmonic extension of~$g$ to~$Q$, fix a small~$\zeta>0$, and let~$\nu=\Gamma_M(u)+3^{-\zeta N}\,dx$, so that~$\nu(q)\geq3^{-\zeta N}|q|$ for every cell~$q$. Use the preliminary partition into roots~$R$ and their~$L$-adic subdivisions. Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-levels} with~$a=1/4$,~$b=3/4$ to each of these finitely many roots, and take the union of their exceptional events. Choose the fraction of bad generations below. Put~$J=\lfloor N/H_1\rfloor$ and~$I=I_R^{1/4,3/4}(N)$. For large~$N$ its first and last generations are
\begin{equation*}
	n_-=\Bigl\lceil\frac{N/4-k_R}{H_1}\Bigr\rceil\,,
	\qquad n_+=\Bigl\lfloor\frac{3N/4-k_R}{H_1}\Bigr\rfloor\,.
\end{equation*}
We follow each root subdivision from generation~$n_-$ through generation~$n_+$. Stop at a child~$q$ with parent~$p$ if its generation~$n(q)$ belongs to~$S$, the cell~$q$ is good and at distance at least~$b_0$ times its side from~$\partial p$, and~$\nu(p)\leq L^D\nu(q)$. At such a cell~$q$, put~$k=k_R+H_1n(q)$. The local H\"older estimate~\eqref{eq:mfd-finite-good-holder} and the comparison of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests} give
\begin{equation*}
	\Lambda_{N,q}(u|_{\partial q})
	\leq r_{N,M}(k)\Lambda_{M,q}(u|_{\partial q})
	+C\eta r_{N,M}(k)\Gamma_M(u)(p)\,.
\end{equation*}
Since~$\Lambda_{M,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ is bounded by~$\Gamma_M(u)(q)$, the stopping rule and the disjointness of the cells at which the subdivision stops show that the sum of~$\Lambda_{N,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ over these cells is at most
\begin{equation*}
	c E_M(u)+C\eta c L^D
	\bigl(E_M(u)+3^{-\zeta N}|Q|\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
This bounds the energy of the competitor of Step~4 on these cells.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We count the cells at generation~$n_+$ on chains along which the subdivision never stops. The moment estimates of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local} are uniform in both cutoffs, so, outside an event of probability at most~$C3^{-p\epsilon N}$, each of the finitely many global constants used below is at most~$3^{\epsilon N}$. Consider a chain of nested cells along which the subdivision does not stop. At each of its steps at which the generation belongs to~$S$ and the child is good and at distance at least~$b_0$ times its side from the boundary of its parent,~$\nu$ drops by a factor larger than~$L^D$. Since~$\nu(Q)$ is controlled by the global energy and~$\nu(q)\geq3^{-\zeta N}|q|$ for every cell~$q$ at generation~$n_+$, the number of such steps is at most
\begin{equation*}
	\frac1{D\log L}\Bigl(\log\bigl(3^{\zeta N}\nu(Q)\bigr)
	+\tfrac34 dN\log3+O_Q(1)\Bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
We choose~$D$ large in terms of~$d$ and~$\vartheta$, and~$\theta$,~$\epsilon$ and~$\zeta$ small. Since~$\#I=J/2+O(1)$, each chain has at least~$\vartheta J/2-O(1)$ steps with generation in~$S$. Discarding the steps counted above and the bad steps with generation in~$S$ leaves at least~$\vartheta J/3-O(1)$ steps with generation in~$S$ at which the child is within distance~$b_0$ times its side of the boundary of its parent. Each cell has at most~$C_dL^{d-1}$ children within that distance of its boundary, so, for~$L$ large, the number of cells at generation~$n_+$ on chains where the subdivision has not stopped is at most~$C_Q L^{(\frac{3d}{4}-\frac{\vartheta}{4})J}$. Thus these remaining cells form an exponentially small fraction of the cells at generation~$n_+$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We bound the energies on the remaining cells at generation~$n_+$. Their physical depth is~$k_R+H_1n_+=3N/4+O(H_1)$, so their side length is comparable to~$3^{-3N/4}$. On such a cell of side~$r$, the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values, together with the bounds~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} on grid cubes, bounds the energy by~$K_N[u]_\alpha^2r^{d-2+2\alpha-\eta_0}$. We choose the fixed exponents so that~$2-2\alpha+\eta_0<\vartheta/8$. The sum of the energies on these cells is then at most~$C3^{3\epsilon N}L^{-\vartheta \frac{J}{8}}$, which decays exponentially in~$N$ if~$\epsilon$ is small.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We compare the minimal energies with boundary values~$g$. The cells at which the subdivision stops and the remaining cells at generation~$n_+$ form a finite partition of~$Q$. Joining the~$E_N$-harmonic extensions of the traces of~$u$ on the cells of this partition gives a competitor with boundary values~$g$. The Dirichlet principle then gives the asserted upper bound for~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)$, and all excluded events have exponentially small probability. The reverse inequality follows from the same proof with the cutoffs interchanged. The finitely many smaller cutoffs are covered by taking their exceptional event to be the whole probability space and enlarging~$C_\eta$.
\end{proof}

The same construction compares the energies of solutions with a volume source.

\begin{lemma}[Comparison for volume sources]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-source-comparison}
Assume the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-stopping}. There is an event of probability at least~$1-C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}$ on which the following holds. Let~$u_M$ solve~$-\nabla\cdot A_M\nabla u_M=f$ on~$Q$, either with a smooth Dirichlet datum~$g$, in which case we put~$B=\|f\|_\infty+\|g\|_{C^2}$, or with zero conormal derivative and mean zero, in which case we assume that~$\int_Qf=0$ and put~$B=\|f\|_\infty$. Then there is a trial function~$v_N$, with the same Dirichlet datum in the first case and with mean zero in the second, such that
\begin{equation*}
	\|v_N-u_M\|_\infty\leq C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}B
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
	E_N^Q(v_N)\leq c(1+C\eta L^D)E_M^Q(u_M)
	+C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}B^2\,.
\end{equation*}
The same holds with~$A_N$ and~$A_M$ replaced by~$A_N^0=e^{-H}A_N$ and~$A_M^0=e^{-H}A_M$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
We use the partition of the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-stopping}, now with~$\nu=\Gamma_M(u_M)+3^{-\zeta N}B^2\,dx$ in place of the measure of that proof. On a good cube~$q$ of side~$r$ whose parent~$p$ satisfies~$\operatorname{dist}(q,\partial p)\geq b_0r$, the source term in~\eqref{eq:mfd-finite-good-holder} of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}(ii) adds to the local comparison the term~$C\eta r_{N,M}(k)s_M(q)^{-1}r^{d+2}\|f\|_\infty^2$. On the event of the preceding proof, the bounds~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} on grid cubes give~$s_M(q)^{-1}\leq3^{\epsilon N}r^{-\eta_0}$. Since the side of every cube used in the comparison is at most~$C3^{-\nf{N}{4}}$, this source term is bounded by~$C\eta c3^{-\zeta N}B^2|q|$ if~$\epsilon+\zeta<(2-\eta_0)/4$, so it is absorbed in the lower bound~$3^{-\zeta N}B^2|q|$ for~$\nu(q)$. The global estimates of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} (for cubes of side larger than one, Remark~\ref{mfd:rem-large-cube-growth}) and Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} bound the energy by~$3^{\epsilon N}B^2$ and the~$C^\alpha$ norm by~$3^{\epsilon N}B$, after these constants are added to the finite list of constants required to be at most~$3^{\epsilon N}$.

\smallskip

On each element of the partition we take the~$A_N$-harmonic extension of the trace of~$u_M$. By the maximum principle, its distance from~$u_M$ is at most twice the oscillation of~$u_M$ on that element. All elements have side at most~$C3^{-\nf{N}{4}}$, so this distance is at most~$C3^{-(\frac{\alpha}{4}-\epsilon)N}B$. In the Neumann case we first subtract the mean. This proves both inequalities.

\smallskip

For~$A_N^0$ and~$A_M^0$, the Dirichlet estimates with source on larger cells are supplied by the whole-cell argument leading to~\eqref{eq:mfd-zero-large-cell-growth}. For the mean-zero Neumann solutions, Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} applies to~$A_N^0$ and~$A_M^0$ on every fixed cube. Its chains use roots of radius at most~$R_*$, so their outer scales do not exceed the cutoff, and the fixed-cube coercivity of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error} bounds the global energy at the same finitely many moment orders. The local trace comparison transfers separately. On each cube~$q=z+r\cu_0$ used in the partition,~$r\leq C3^{-N/4}$. Put~$\varepsilon_q=\operatorname{osc}_q H$ and~$s_j^0(q)=e^{-H(z)}s_j(q)$ for~$j=N,M$. The variational formula gives, for every trace~$g$,
\begin{equation*}
	e^{-\varepsilon_q}s_j(q)^{-1}\Lambda_{j,q}(g)
	\leq s_j^0(q)^{-1}\Lambda_{j,q}^0(g)
	\leq e^{\varepsilon_q}s_j(q)^{-1}\Lambda_{j,q}(g)\,.
\end{equation*}
Both cutoffs use this same weight, and~$s_N^0(q)/s_M^0(q)=r_{N,M}(k)$. Add~$e^{\|H\|_{C^1(Q)}}$, whose fixed moments are finite, to the constants required to be at most~$3^{\epsilon N}$. For large~$N$,~$\varepsilon_q\leq C3^{-N/5}$ on this event. The uniform good-cell trace bound then transfers the finite trace test with an additional tolerance~$C3^{-N/5}$; choose the original tolerance smaller by a fixed factor. Equivalently, a local energy comparison is multiplied by at most~$e^{2\varepsilon_q}=1+O(3^{-N/5})$. After summing over the cells at which the subdivision stops, its additional error is at most~$C3^{-N/5}E_M^0(u_M)$. It is absorbed by the multiplicative tolerance after increasing the deterministic initial cutoff. The additive source and terminal-cell errors retain a positive exponential rate. This proves the two-cutoff comparison for~$A_N^0$ without a convergence assertion uniform over partial infrared sums.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Almost-sure convergence of minimal energies]
\label{mfd:prop-as-dirichlet}
Let~$Q$ be a cube of triadic side length and~$g$ a smooth function on~$\partial Q$. Then~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)\to\Lambda_{E,Q}(g)$ almost surely as~$N\to\infty$ along the full sequence of integers.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We show that the normalizations are close to their limits on most of the eligible generations of the preliminary roots~$R$. Put~$I=I_R^{1/4,3/4}(N)$ and~$r_N(k)=(\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N)/e(k)$, and fix~$0<\varepsilon<1$ and a density~$0<\vartheta<1/3$. Suppose that, for infinitely many~$N$, the set~$S=\{n\in I:r_N(k_R+H_1n)\leq1-\varepsilon\}$ has~$\#S\geq\vartheta\#I$. For each such fixed~$N$, the set~$S$ is finite. The convergence~$\ahom_{M-k}/\ahom_M\to e(k)>0$ at each of its physical depths~$k=k_R+H_1n$ therefore gives one cutoff beyond which~$r_{N,M}(k_R+H_1n)\leq1-\varepsilon/2$ for every~$n\in S$. Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-stopping} on~$\cu_0$, using the corresponding set of indices~$\{k_R/H_1+n:n\in S\}$ and a fixed nonconstant affine datum, with~$\eta$ so small that~$C\eta L^D<\varepsilon/4$, and let~$M\to\infty$ first. Along these~$N$, the minimal energy at cutoff~$N$ is bounded by a fixed factor strictly smaller than one times its limit, up to an error that tends to zero in probability. This contradicts its convergence in probability to a strictly positive limit. The reverse comparison, using~$r_{N,M}^{-1}$ and the finite set~$\{n\in I:r_N(k_R+H_1n)\geq1+\varepsilon\}$, excludes in the same way a fraction~$\vartheta$ of large ratios. Hence, for all sufficiently large~$N$, the set~$S=\{n\in I:1-\varepsilon\leq r_N(k_R+H_1n)\leq1+\varepsilon\}$ has~$\#S>(1-2\vartheta)\#I$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We prove an exponential bound and conclude. For each large~$N$, we apply the two comparisons of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-stopping}, with~$S\subseteq I_R^{1/4,3/4}(N)$ the set of generation indices found in Step~1, and let~$M\to\infty$. Taking~$\eta$ small relative to~$\varepsilon$ gives, with constants depending on~$\varepsilon$ and on the datum,
\begin{equation*}
	\P\left[
	|\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)-\Lambda_{E,Q}(g)|
	>C\varepsilon\Lambda_{E,Q}(g)+C_\varepsilon3^{-\gamma_\varepsilon N}
	\right]\leq C_\varepsilon3^{-\gamma_\varepsilon N}\,.
\end{equation*}
The right side is summable in~$N$, so the Borel--Cantelli lemma for~$\varepsilon=1/j$,~$j\in\N$, gives the almost-sure convergence.
\end{proof}

The same argument applies to the quantities~(ii)--(iv) of the next proposition.

\begin{proposition}[Summable error probabilities]
\label{mfd:prop-as-response-bank}
Let~$Q$ be a cube of triadic side length, and let~$Z_N$ be one of the following quantities on~$Q$, computed for the coefficient~$A_N$ or for~$A_N^0$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item the minimal energy~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)$ with a fixed smooth boundary value~$g$;
\item the quadratic form~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle$ of~\eqref{eq:mfd-quadratic-response} for a fixed bounded source~$f$;
\item the quadratic form~$\sup_{v\in H^1(Q)}\{2\int_Qfv-E_N^Q(v)\}$ for a fixed bounded source~$f$ with~$\int_Qf=0$;
\item the Neumann energy~$|Q|\,p\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)p$ of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-environment}, for the constant flux given by a fixed vector~$p$.
\end{enumerate}
There exists~$\gmcdelta_0>0$ such that, if~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then~$Z_N$ converges in probability to a limit~$Z$, and, for every~$\varepsilon>0$, there exist~$C_\varepsilon<\infty$,~$c_\varepsilon>0$ and a deterministic~$N_\varepsilon$ such that, for every~$N\geq N_\varepsilon$,
\begin{equation}
	\P\bigl[|Z_N-Z|>
	C\varepsilon Z+C_\varepsilon3^{-c_\varepsilon N}\bigr]
	\leq C_\varepsilon3^{-c_\varepsilon N}\,.
	\label{eq:mfd-summable-response}
\end{equation}
The finitely many moment orders and the threshold~$\gmcdelta_0$ do not depend on~$\varepsilon$. For the coefficients~$A_N^0$, the constants are uniform over deterministic translations of~$Q$. In particular, for every countable family of such quantities, all of them converge almost surely along the full sequence of cutoffs.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For the minimal energies of~$A_N$, the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response} was proved in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-dirichlet}. For~$A_N^0$, Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-21} and~\eqref{eq:mfd-weighted-boundary-minimum}, with the weight~$e^{-H}$ and the same padded limit form, give convergence in probability. The common-weight transfer in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-source-comparison} supplies the two-cutoff comparison. Fix before disorder all moment orders needed for the rare tests, the global estimates and the products below; their number and values are independent of the tolerance.

\smallskip

For a volume source~$f$, let~$u_M$ be the solution and write~$Z_M=E_M(u_M)=\int_Qfu_M$. If~$a$ is the factor multiplying~$E_M(u_M)$ in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-source-comparison}, insert~$a^{-1}v_N$ in the variational formula for~$Z_N$. For~$1/2\leq a\leq3$, the trial-energy and sup-distance bounds give
\begin{equation*}
	Z_M\leq aZ_N+C_\varepsilon3^{-\gamma N}\|f\|_\infty^2\,.
\end{equation*}
Interchanging the cutoffs gives the reverse comparison. Step~1 of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-dirichlet} supplies a set of eligible generations, of density larger than~$1/3$, on which the normalization ratio is close to one. For every fixed tolerance and all large~$N$, the resulting comparisons therefore hold with~$a\leq1+C\varepsilon$, uniformly in all sufficiently large~$M$. The exceptional events are independent of the bounded source, and~$\gamma>0$ depends on the tolerance.

\smallskip

We first record the moment bound used in case~(iii). The mean-zero coercivity constant~$K_{Q,N}$ in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error} gives
\begin{equation*}
	0\leq Z_N\leq K_{Q,N}\|f\|_{L^2(Q)}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
Only upper moments of this constant are needed. For clarity, its factor on a fixed larger triadic cube~$Q=z+R\cu_0$,~$R=3^s>1$, can be computed exactly. Set~$\widetilde\g_i(y)=\g_{i+s}(z+Ry)$; these fields have the original joint law. If~$\widetilde A_{N+s}$ is their anchored coefficient, expansion of the logarithms gives
\begin{equation*}
	A_N(z+Ry)=c_N(Q)\widetilde A_{N+s}(y),\qquad
	c_N(Q)=\frac{\ahom_{N+s}}{\ahom_N}
	\exp\left(H(z)-\sum_{j=1}^s(\g_j(z)-\tau^2)\right)\,.
\end{equation*}
The energy scales by~$R^{d-2}c_N(Q)$ and the squared~$L^2$ norm by~$R^d$. Thus the physical~$L^2$ coercivity constant is~$R^2c_N(Q)^{-1}\widetilde K_{N+s}$. For the inhomogeneous~$H^{\nf34}$ norm it is at most
\begin{equation*}
	\max\{R^2,R^{\nf12}\}\,c_N(Q)^{-1}\widetilde K_{N+s}\,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering.ratio} and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}, both~$c_N(Q)$ and its inverse have every fixed finite moment uniformly in~$N$, with constants depending on~$Q$ and its anchor. Take~$\widetilde K_{N+s}$ at twice the requested order before choosing disorder, and apply H\"older's inequality; no independence is needed. For~$A_N^0$, the bound~$E_N^{0,Q}\geq e^{-\sup_QH}E_N^Q$ introduces one more finite-moment factor. For sides at most one use Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-coercivity}; any finitely many cutoffs below the cell depth are also bounded by~$\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$ and the ordinary cube Poincar\'e inequality.

\smallskip

Now compare two sufficiently large cutoffs with the same fixed~$N$. These upper moments make the multiplicative error small in probability as~$\varepsilon\downarrow0$, and the two exceptional probabilities vanish as~$N\to\infty$. This proves the Cauchy property in probability in case~(iii), without a Neumann limit form. In case~(ii), the limit is the quadratic form of the unique Dirichlet limit form. Letting~$M\to\infty$ with~$N$ fixed gives~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response} in both cases.

\smallskip

For case~(iv), take the mean-zero source~$f_{Q,h}$ constructed in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error}. On~$Q=w+R(0,1)^d$ it contains the physical factor~$R^{-1}$ and satisfies~$\|f_{Q,h}\|_\infty\leq C_{Q,p}h^{-1}$. The fixed-cube load comparison there gives an~$L^q$ approximation error at most~$C_{q,Q,p}h^{\nf14}$ and uniformly bounded~$L^q$ moments of the smoothed responses, at the selected orders. In the preceding triadic chart, the physical flux response is~$R^dc_N(Q)^{-1}$ times the unit-cube response; the factor~$R^{-1}$ in the source gives the same scaling for its smoothed response. That proof also supplies the coercivity constant~$K_{Q,N}$ for both infrared conventions on the same fixed cube, with a uniform polynomial tail
\begin{equation*}
	\P[K_{Q,N}>T]\leq B_QT^{-p_0},\qquad p_0>0\,.
\end{equation*}
For the required larger triadic cubes, its construction uses the law-preserving shift to cutoff~$N+k$, the finitely many retained coarse layers and the two compact infrared restrictions. The unit-cube first-moment bound already suffices for~$p_0=1/2$. This transfer fixes its disorder threshold before the tolerance.

\smallskip

Let~$Y_{N,h}$ be the response to the smoothed volume source~$f_{Q,h}$. The load comparison gives
\begin{equation*}
	|Z_N-Y_{N,h}|
	\leq C_{Q,p}h^{\nf14}K_{Q,N}^{\nf12}Z_N^{\nf12}
	+C_{Q,p}h^{\nf12}K_{Q,N}\,.
\end{equation*}
For fixed~$h$, case~(iii) and the uniform moment approximation prove that~$(Z_N)_N$ is Cauchy in probability. For the rate, use the two-cutoff comparison directly, with~$h_N=3^{-\gamma N/4}$ and~$T_N=3^{\gamma N/32}$. Outside the source-independent comparison events and the two events~$\{K_{Q,N}>T_N\}$,~$\{K_{Q,M}>T_N\}$, absorb the mixed terms by~$ab\leq\varepsilon a^2+C_\varepsilon b^2$. Applying this absorption in both directions, and taking~$\varepsilon\leq1/2$, yields
\begin{equation*}
	|Z_N-Z_M|
	\leq C\varepsilon Z_N+
	C_\varepsilon
	\left(3^{-\gamma N}h_N^{-2}+T_Nh_N^{\nf12}\right)\,.
\end{equation*}
The two deterministic remainders decay as~$3^{-\gamma N/2}$ and~$3^{-3\gamma N/32}$. The probability of either coercivity event is at most~$B_Q3^{-p_0\gamma N/32}$, uniformly for every sufficiently large~$M$. Hence a common positive rate is~$c_\varepsilon=\min\{3\gamma/32,p_0\gamma/32\}$. Let~$M\to\infty$ with~$N$ fixed, using convergence in probability and then a further subsequence converging almost surely. The resulting bound first has~$C\varepsilon Z_N$; choosing the tolerance smaller absorbs this term against the limit~$Z$ and gives~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response}. No centered estimate on the physical cube is used in this comparison.

\smallskip

The finite list of moment orders is chosen before disorder. Only the comparison geometry, initial field windows, constants and the positive rate~$\gamma$ depend on the tolerance; the coercivity order does not. For~$A_N^0$, translate the fixed cube and its boundary datum or volume source together, and leave the constant flux vector unchanged. Every finite-cutoff response on the translated cube equals the response on the original cube in the translated bilateral field. Choose its limit by the same translation. Stationarity therefore preserves the joint law of the response and its limit, so the same constants and deterministic initial cutoff work for every deterministic translate of this fixed cube. They may depend on its side and the fixed data. The final assertion follows by Borel--Cantelli at~$\varepsilon=1/j$,~$j\in\N$, and a countable intersection.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Almost-sure bounds uniform in the cutoff}
\label{mfd:sec-pathwise-bounds}

We now make the local estimates uniform over all cutoffs on one event of probability one. On cells of depth at most~$\theta N$, Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-response-bank} compares the finite-cutoff energies with their limits, with error probabilities summable over the cells and over~$N$. For the deeper cells, the moment bounds imply that, almost surely, the random constants in the estimates are smaller than any fixed exponential in~$N$ once~$N$ is large. The margins in the spatial exponents absorb this growth.

\begin{lemma}[Coarse-grained ellipticity bounds uniform in the cutoff]
\label{mfd:lem-as-coarse}
Let~$Q$ be a cube of triadic side length and let~$s$ be one of the positive fractional orders used above. Then, almost surely, for~$q\in\{1,2\}$,
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_N\bigl(\Lambda_{s,q}(Q;A_N)
	+\lambda_{s,q}(Q;A_N)^{-1}\bigr)<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
In particular, the coercivity bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values on~$Q$ hold with one finite random constant for all~$N$. The same conclusions hold with~$A_N^0$ in place of~$A_N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
It suffices to consider a unit cube.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We bound the finite-cutoff energies of the cells of depth at most~$\theta N$ by their limits. Denote by~$Z_m(z)$ the finite family consisting of the minimal energies with the affine boundary values~$x\mapsto e\cdot x$ and the Neumann energies for the fluxes~$e$, for~$e\in\{e_i,e_i+e_j\}$, computed for~$A_m^0$ in the environment shifted to~$z$, at cutoff~$m$. We apply~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response} with one fixed small tolerance to this family on every grid cube of depth~$k\leq\theta N$, whose cutoff after rescaling to the unit cube is~$m=N-k$. There are at most~$C3^{dk}$ such cubes. We choose~$\theta>0$ so small that~$d\theta<c(1-\theta)/2$, where~$c$ is the exponent in~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response} at this tolerance; then the sum over these cubes of the exceptional probabilities is summable in~$N$. Hence, almost surely, for all sufficiently large~$N$, each of these finite-cutoff energies is bounded by~$C(1+Z(\Theta_{k,z}\omega))$, where~$Z$ is its limit. All the entries are nonnegative, and polarization recovers the coarse-grained matrices from this finite family.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We bound the coarse-grained matrices of the cells of depth at most~$\theta N$. For a cube~$q=z+3^{-k}\cu_0$, separating the scalar~$s_N(q)$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations} gives
\begin{equation*}
	A_N(z+3^{-k}y)=
	s_N(q)\exp\Bigl(\Bigl[H+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\g_{-j}\Bigr](z+3^{-k}y)
	-\Bigl[H+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\g_{-j}\Bigr](z)\Bigr)
	A_{N-k}^{0,\Theta_{k,z}\omega}(y)\,.
\end{equation*}
The deterministic ratio in~$s_N(q)$ and its inverse are at most~$e^{C\tau^2k}$. Hence the factors in front of~$A_{N-k}^{0,\Theta_{k,z}\omega}$ and their inverses are bounded by
\begin{equation*}
	e^{C\tau^2k}\exp\Bigl(\Bigl|H(z)+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\g_{-j}(z)\Bigr|
	+\operatorname{osc}_{q}\Bigl[H+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\g_{-j}\Bigr]\Bigr)\,,
\end{equation*}
whose~$L^p$ norm is at most~$C_pe^{C_p\gmcdelta^2 k}$, by the independence of the fields and the bound on their gradients weighted by their scales. The limits~$Z$ have the same moment bounds as their approximants. We choose~$\rho<s/4$, a moment order~$p$ with~$p\rho>2d$, and then~$\gmcdelta$ small enough. A union bound over all~$k$ and~$z$ gives a random~$K_\omega<\infty$ such that, for every grid cell of depth~$k$, the product of the expression in the last display and~$1+Z(\Theta_{k,z}\omega)$ is at most~$K_\omega3^{\rho k}$. Since both coarse-grained matrices are monotone in the coefficient,~$a(q;A_N)+a_*^{-1}(q;A_N)\leq CK_\omega3^{\rho k}$ on the cells of depth at most~$\theta N$, with the same~$K_\omega$ for all sufficiently large~$N$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We bound the matrices of the cells of depth greater than~$\theta N$ and sum over all depths. For~$\theta N<k\leq N$, the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}, applied only to the matrices of the cells, gives the bound~$K_N3^{\rho k}$, where the moments of~$K_N$ of the finitely many orders fixed at the start of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness} are bounded uniformly in~$N$. By the Borel--Cantelli lemma,~$K_N\leq3^{\xi N}$ for all large~$N$, for every fixed~$\xi>0$. The contribution of these cells to either sum defining~$\Lambda_{s,q}$ or~$\lambda_{s,q}$ is at most~$CK_N3^{-(s-\rho)\theta N}$, which is bounded if~$\xi<(s-\rho)\theta$. The cells below the smallest field scale contribute at most~$C3^{-sN}(\sup_QA_N+\sup_QA_N^{-1})$, which is bounded almost surely after the fixed decrease of~$\gmcdelta$, by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes} and Markov's inequality. The cells of depth at most~$\theta N$ contribute at most~$CK_\omega\sum_{k\geq0}3^{-(s-\rho)k}$. For~$q=1$, weighted Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the square of the sum of the square roots of the matrices by this sum of matrices; for~$q=2$, the definition is already a sum of matrices, with the smaller weight~$3^{-2sk}$. Finally, the coercivity bound and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values follow from these bounds as in the proofs of Lemmas~\ref{mfd:lem-coercivity} and~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regularity uniform in the cutoff]
\label{mfd:lem-as-regularity}
Let~$0<\alpha<1$ and~$d-1<t<d$ be strictly smaller than the exponents fixed in the moment estimates of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} and Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source}. Then, for every fixed cube~$Q$, almost surely the estimates of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth}, extended to larger cubes by Remark~\ref{mfd:rem-large-cube-growth}, and Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} with the exponents~$\alpha$ and~$t$ hold on~$Q$ with one finite random constant for all~$N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
We run the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} using only the primitive conditions on~$\mathsf F$,~$\mathsf P$ and~$\mathsf R$, and the accumulated error~$\mathbf G$, defined in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}. The normalized tests~(a)--(c) of~$G(q)$ are not inputs to this iteration. Its reference ratios follow from~\eqref{e.ratio.of.bs}; the actual coefficient's initial energy and ellipticity bounds are supplied separately by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-as-coarse}. We transfer the primitive prefixes above the scale~$3^{-\theta N}$ to their limits (Steps~1--5), and use the uniform moment bounds below that scale (Step~6).

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We pass the primitive scores to the limit. In~$\mathsf R$ and~$\mathbf G$, the response at stationary scale~$n$ is computed for~$\ahom_n^{-1}\a_n$. In its own translated unit chart it is a response of~$A_n^0$, with no cell scalar or infrared factor. Define the limits of these affine Dirichlet and constant-flux Neumann responses by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-response-bank}, and use them to define the limiting primitive scores. The pure-field parts are taken in the same reindexed environment. The responses converge along the full sequence of cutoffs by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-response-bank}, so the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-prefix-limit}(i) gives their finite-interval~$L^p$ limits along that full sequence. Geometric tail bounds pass by Fatou, and the conditional-expectation argument preceding Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness} gives their limiting band bounds. The matrices computed for~$A_N/s_N(U)$ include the centered infrared field and are not identified with these own-cutoff responses. Their almost-sure uniform ellipticity bounds are those of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-as-coarse}, and no convergence of their normalized tests is used here.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We compare the finite-cutoff primitive scores with their limits. Fix~$\eta>0$ below the margins between the lower and upper ramp thresholds. Truncate only the primitive quantities at relative depth~$\lfloor\theta'N\rfloor$, and consider intervals of length at most~$\lfloor\theta N\rfloor$ at all the centers needed in the regularity proof, including the face meshes and the centers of the initial balls. For a primitive response at physical depth~$j$, the remaining own cutoff is~$N-j$; its limit is taken in that same translated and reindexed environment. Thus all the retained responses are unit-cube responses of~$A_{N-j}^0$, not responses of the normalized anchored coefficient. There are at most~$CN^C3^{C_d(\theta+\theta')N}$ of them, with remaining cutoffs at least~$[1-C(\theta+\theta')]N-C$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response} and a union bound, the probability that any retained response differs from its limit by more than~$C\eta$ times the limit plus the exponentially small additive error is at most
\begin{equation*}
	C_\eta N^C3^{C_d(\theta+\theta')N-c_\eta[1-C(\theta+\theta')]N}\,.
\end{equation*}
The discarded primitive tails, at finite cutoff and in the limit, have~$L^p$ norm at most~$C_p3^{-a\lfloor\theta'N\rfloor}$. The probability that one of them exceeds~$\eta$ is at most~$C_\eta N^C3^{C_d\theta N-ap\theta'N}$. Choose~$\theta'$ small enough for the first exponent to be negative, and then~$\theta$ small enough for both exponents to be negative. Both bounds are summable in~$N$. Borel--Cantelli gives these comparisons simultaneously for all sufficiently large cutoffs.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We compare the finite and limiting primitive prefixes. The relative error of the unit-cube energies controls the continuous piecewise linear functions: polarization gives~$|\mathcal J_N-\mathcal J_\infty|\leq C\eta(1+\mathcal J_\infty)$ on each retained cell at its own cutoff, and weighting gives the same inequality for the condition on~$\mathsf R$. A bounded continuous piecewise linear function of this quantity changes by at most~$C\eta$, and the square-root terms of the sums over intervals of scales change by at most~$C\sqrt\eta$. The terms of~$\mathbf G$ involving the fields~$\g_j$ at a retained depth coincide once their finite interval of field indices lies below~$N$, and their omitted tails satisfy the geometric bound of Step~2. Consequently, almost surely for all large~$N$, every retained sum over an interval of scales at finite cutoff is bounded by the corresponding limiting sum plus~$C\sqrt\eta$ per scale.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We bound, uniformly over the choices of center and initial radius, the number of scales after which the limiting sums over intervals of scales satisfy the thresholds. For a fixed choice~$\pi$ of center and initial radius, let~$B_\pi$ be the least integer after which the limiting primitive prefixes satisfy the stricter thresholds of the iteration. Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-prefix} to~$Z$ and~$\mathbf G$ at a smaller finite-cutoff threshold and pass the bounds to the limit by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-prefix-limit}(ii). Summing over the interval lengths gives~$\P(B_\pi>b)\leq Ce^{-Ab}$, uniformly in~$\pi$. At target depth~$n$, the meshes of centers and initial radii used in the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} involve at most~$C(n+1)^C3^{C_dn}$ choices of centers, initial radii and faces, edges and corners. We fix a small~$\xi_0>0$ before decreasing~$\gmcdelta$, and choose the rate~$A$ so that~$A\xi_0>C_d\log3+2$. A union bound over the choices~$\pi$ at depth~$n$ gives
\begin{equation*}
	\P\Bigl[\max_\pi B_\pi>\xi_0 n+b\Bigr]
	\leq C(n+1)^C3^{C_dn}e^{-A(\xi_0 n+b)}\,.
\end{equation*}
Summing over~$n$ shows that, almost surely, there is one finite~$B_\omega$ such that~$B_\pi\leq\xi_0n+B_\omega$ for every choice~$\pi$ at every depth~$n$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 5.} We transfer these bounds to finite cutoffs and prove the estimates at radii~$r\geq 3^{-\theta N}$. We take~$\eta$ so small that the loss per scale of Step~3 is below the strict margin of the iteration. Then, for finite cutoffs and depths~$n\leq\theta N$, the same numbers~$B_\pi$ apply with slightly weaker thresholds. The~$Z$ prefix bounds the number of failures of the primitive good-scale conditions, since a failed upper threshold contributes one to~$Z$; the~$\mathbf G$ prefix bounds the accumulated comparison error. These are the two score inputs of~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.applied}. The reference-ratio input is~\eqref{e.ratio.of.bs}, derived from the same primitive conditions and annealed ordering. Thus the iteration applies without imposing~(a)--(c) at these cells. Its initial averages and energy factors are bounded separately as follows. The estimate of Lemma~\ref{mfd:thm-fold} transfers the original field bounds to the reflected coefficients near faces, edges and corners. The reflection across faces is applied at most~$d+1$ times, and the numbers~$B_\pi$ of these applications add up, so their total contribution is bounded by a common random constant times~$3^{C\xi_0 n}$.

\smallskip

We next show that the averages~$b_i$ at the initial radius of each application are bounded by a random constant times a small negative power of the initial radius. Let~$\theta_0$ be the value of~$\theta$ in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-as-coarse}, so that the cells of depth at most~$\theta_0N$ were compared with their limits there, and decrease~$\theta$ to at most~$\theta_0/2$. For an initial radius at depth~$k\leq\theta N$, we split its coarse-grained sum at descendant depth~$\theta_0N$. For~$0<\rho<s$, the part with descendant depth at most~$\theta_0N$ is at most~$CK_\omega3^{\rho k}$, and the part with descendant depth greater than~$\theta_0N$ is at most
\begin{equation*}
	CK_N3^{\rho k}3^{-(s-\rho)(\theta_0N-k)}
	\leq C3^{\rho k}3^{(\xi-(s-\rho)\theta_0/2)N}\,,
\end{equation*}
where~$K_N\leq3^{\xi N}$ for large~$N$, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-as-coarse}. We choose~$\xi<(s-\rho)\theta_0/2$; the contribution below the smallest field scale satisfies the same bound by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes}. The average at its own scale involves only the value of~$H+\sum_{j<k}\g_{-j}$ at the center and the deterministic ratio~$\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N$. Hence the energy in the initial ball, the coercivity constant and the constant in~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} are bounded by a random constant times a small negative power of the initial radius.

\smallskip

We apply the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} with exponents strictly larger than~$\alpha$ and~$t$, and choose~$\xi_0$ and~$\rho$ so small that the margins in the exponents absorb both losses. The iteration gives one random constant for the oscillation and energy bounds at all radii~$r\geq 3^{-\theta N}$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 6.} We treat the radii~$r\leq 3^{-\theta N}$. We choose exponents~$\alpha_0>\alpha$ and~$t_0>t$ for which the Morrey bounds at all radii of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} (on cubes of side larger than one, Remark~\ref{mfd:rem-large-cube-growth}) and Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} hold. Their constants satisfy~$K_N\leq3^{\xi N}$ almost surely for all large~$N$, for every fixed~$\xi>0$. For~$r\leq 3^{-\theta N}$, we have~$K_Nr^{\alpha_0-\alpha}\leq3^{(\xi-\theta(\alpha_0-\alpha))N}$ and~$K_Nr^{t_0-t}\leq3^{(\xi-\theta(t_0-t))N}$. We choose~$\xi$ smaller than~$\theta(\alpha_0-\alpha)$ and~$\theta(t_0-t)$; then these quantities are bounded. In the oscillation estimates at radii larger than~$3^{-\theta N}$, point values are compared with averages over balls of radius~$3^{-\theta N}$, using the estimate at small radii just proved.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Almost-sure convergence of energies and resolvents]
\label{mfd:prop-as-forms}
Almost surely, for every cube~$Q\in\mathcal C$,~$G_N^Q\to G^Q$ in operator norm on~$L^2(Q,dx)$,~$E_N^Q$ Mosco-converges to~$E^Q$, and, for every~$f\in C(\overline Q)$ and~$\lambda>0$,
\begin{equation*}
	\|R^Q_{N,\lambda}f-R^Q_\lambda f\|_{C(\overline Q)}\longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation*}
On each fixed cube, the~$C^{\nf14}(\overline Q)$ norms of~$R^Q_{N,\lambda}f$ are bounded by one finite random constant, uniformly in~$N$, in~$\lambda$ in a compact subset of~$(0,\infty)$, and in~$f$ with~$\|f\|_\infty\leq1$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Intersect the events of Lemmas~\ref{mfd:lem-as-coarse} and~\ref{mfd:lem-as-regularity}, Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-chaos-growth}, and Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-response-bank} for a countable dense family of bounded sources and their pairwise sums. Coercivity makes the images of the~$L^2$ unit ball under~$G_N^Q$ bounded in~$H^{\nf34}$ uniformly in~$N$, and polarization gives convergence of the matrix elements of~$G_N^Q$ on the dense family. The finite-rank argument and the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-quadratic-response} of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse} give norm and Mosco convergence to the limits already identified in probability.

\smallskip

All the constants in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-uniform-resolvent} are now bounded along the full sequence. Its variational and Arzel\`a--Ascoli argument therefore proves uniform convergence and the H\"older bound.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Almost-sure convergence of the path laws}
\label{mfd:sec-as-paths}

\begin{proposition}[Almost-sure quenched convergence]
\label{mfd:prop-as-quenched}
Almost surely, for every compact~$B\subset\Rd$,
\begin{equation*}
	\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in B}
	d_{\mathrm{weak}}(K^\omega_{N,x},K^\omega_x)=0\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Intersect the full-probability events of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-forms}, Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-limit-properties} and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-killing} over the countable family of cubes, and fix an environment in this event. The killed occupation resolvents of~$K^\omega_{N,x}$ converge to those of~$K^\omega_x$, uniformly on each closed cube.

\smallskip

We first obtain path tightness in this fixed environment. The image of a compact starting set under the continuous kernel~$x\mapsto K^\omega_x$ is a compact family of laws on continuous path space. Its paths have infinite lifetime, so their probability of leaving a large cube before a fixed time, or of moving a fixed distance in a sufficiently short time, tends to zero uniformly in the starting point. Uniform convergence of the killed resolvents transfers these bounds to all sufficiently large cutoffs. For compact containment, compare~$R^Q_{N,1}1$ with its limiting counterpart on a sufficiently large cube: the identity~$1-R^Q_{N,1}1(x)=E^\omega_{N,x}e^{-\tau_Q}$ bounds exits before a fixed time. For short exits, fix cubes~$U\Subset V$, with~$V\in\mathcal C$, and a compact starting set~$A\subset U$. Choose~$0\leq f\leq1$ continuous, supported in~$V$, zero near~$A$ and one near~$\partial U$. Continuity of the limiting path kernel, whose image of~$A\cup\partial U$ is compact, gives~$\lambda R^V_\lambda f\to f$ uniformly there. Given~$\varepsilon>0$, first choose~$\lambda$ so that~$\lambda R^V_\lambda f\leq\varepsilon$ on~$A$ and~$\lambda R^V_\lambda f\geq3/4$ on~$\partial U$. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-forms} and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-killing} then give, for all sufficiently large~$N$,~$u_N:=\lambda R^V_{N,\lambda}f\leq2\varepsilon$ on~$A$ and~$u_N\geq1/2$ on~$\partial U$. The strong Markov property at~$\tau_U$, applied to the nonnegative occupation integral killed at~$\tau_V$, gives
\begin{equation*}
	u_N(x)\geq E^\omega_{N,x}
	\left[e^{-\lambda\tau_U}u_N(Z_{\tau_U});\,\tau_U\leq h\right]
	\geq\frac12e^{-\lambda h}P^\omega_{N,x}(\tau_U\leq h)\,,
	\qquad x\in A\,.
\end{equation*}
Consequently,~$\sup_{x\in A}P^\omega_{N,x}(\tau_U\leq h)\leq8\varepsilon$ for~$h\leq(\log2)/\lambda$. The choices are in the order~$\varepsilon$, then~$\lambda$, then~$N$, then~$h$. Using small cubes~$U$ and a finite cover gives the bound for a prescribed displacement, uniformly over a compact starting set.

\smallskip

Apply this short-exit bound at successive exits from small balls, as in Step~3 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{tight:prop-tightness}. The strong Markov property bounds the number of exits on a finite time interval; it also makes the probability of a very short gap tend to zero. Thus the moduli of continuity are tight. Together with compact containment, Arzel\`a--Ascoli gives tightness on continuous path space.

\smallskip

Now let~$N_j\to\infty$ and~$x_j\to x$. Localization as in~\eqref{eq:mfd-path-localization} and killed-resolvent convergence give convergence of the whole-space discounted occupation integrals. Tightness and uniqueness of the Laplace transform give convergence of the one-time laws at every time. The Markov identity then gives convergence of the finite-dimensional laws, and approximation by cylinder functions on compact sets of paths gives~$K^\omega_{N_j,x_j}\to K^\omega_x$. If convergence were not uniform on a compact~$B$, a sequence of offending starting points would have a convergent subsequence, contradicting this conclusion and continuity of~$x\mapsto K^\omega_x$. This proves the result in~$d_{\mathrm P}$; tightness transfers it to every metric inducing the weak topology.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{t.scaling.limit}(i)]
The relabeling~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization} and the changes of space and time transform the weak elliptic resolvent equations of the physical process into those defining~$K^\omega_{N,x}$. Uniqueness of the weak solutions identifies the resolvents and hence the semigroups; the resulting finite-dimensional distributions identify the continuous path laws.

\smallskip

Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-quenched} gives the almost-sure convergence~\eqref{eq:mfd-main-as} of the quenched laws, and Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-quenched-convergence} shows that the limiting kernel is measurable with respect to the fields. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-limit-properties} gives a continuous kernel, which is the family of laws of a continuous, conservative, strong Markov process with a~$\mu$-symmetric semigroup. Since the kernel is continuous, the semigroup maps bounded continuous functions to continuous functions. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-chaos-growth} shows that~$\mu=\lim_N\mu_N$ is locally finite, has no atoms and has full support, and Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-finite-exit} gives the finite mean exit times. Finally, integrating the quenched convergence against~$\P$ and using that the path functionals are bounded gives the convergence of the annealed laws.
\end{proof}

\section{The limiting process and measure}
\label{lim:sec-process-measure}

In this section we prove the remaining assertions of Theorem~\ref{t.scaling.limit}. The mean-exit-time bound of Lemma~\ref{lim:lem-mean-exit} and power-law decay of the effective diffusivities between arbitrary scales bound the mean time to leave a cube of side~$3^{-k}$ by~$C3^{-(2+\eta)k}$, for every cutoff~$L\geq k$, with~$C$ independent of~$L$ and~$k$. These bounds pass to the limit and show that the limiting paths leave small cubes faster than Brownian paths. Consequently, their pointwise H\"older exponent is at most~$(2+\eta)^{-1}$, and the annealed path laws are mutually singular with every Brownian law; for each deterministic starting point, so is the quenched path law in almost every environment.

\smallskip

We then prove that the reversible measure is invariant and singular with respect to Lebesgue measure, and that the transition laws are absolutely continuous with respect to it. Together with the bounds on its mass in small balls, this shows that no transition law is Gaussian.

\smallskip

Throughout, we assume~\ref{a.g1}--\ref{a.g4} at weak disorder and rescale cubes to~$\cu_0$ as in Section~\ref{s.tightness}, with unit of time~\eqref{tight:eq-local-time}. We write~$P_x^\omega$ and~$E_x^\omega$ for the law and expectation, in the environment~$\omega$, of the diffusion under consideration ($X^{(L)}$,~$X$, or their rescalings to~$\cu_0$) started at~$x$.

\subsection{Power-law decay and mean exit times}

We first extend the power-law decay of the effective diffusivities in Proposition~\ref{p.homogenized.coefficient.strict.decay} to the ratio between two arbitrary scales.

\begin{lemma}[Power-law decay between arbitrary scales]
\label{lim:lem-strict-decay}
There exist constants~$C<\infty$ and~$\eta>0$, depending only on~$d$ and the law of~$\g_0$, such that, for every~$\ell,m\in\N_0$ with~$\ell\leq m$,
\begin{equation}
	\frac{\ahom_m}{\ahom_\ell}\leq C3^{-\eta(m-\ell)}\,,
	\label{lim:eq-strict-decay}
\end{equation}
where in dimension two one can take~$\eta=\tau^2/\log3$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In dimension two, use~\eqref{e.renormalized.diffusivities.two.dimensions}. For~$d\geq3$, we start the gluing argument of Proposition~\ref{p.homogenized.coefficient.strict.decay} at scale~$\ell$.

\smallskip

We first prove for~$\spx_\ell^\pi$ the energy bound that Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound} gives for cubes. The Whitney cubes of~$\spx_\ell^\pi$ of side~$3^{\ell-i}$ occupy at most a proportion~$C3^{-i}$ of its volume. For~$i\leq\ell$, use on each cube the minimizer of the energy with coefficient~$\a_{\ell-i}$ among functions equal to~$p\cdot x$ on the boundary of the cube. The factors contributed by the fields in~$\a_\ell$ that are absent from~$\a_{\ell-i}$ are independent of this minimizer and have mean one. By~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering}, the expected energy density is bounded by~$C\ahom_\ell e^{2\tau^2i}|p|^2$. For~$i>\ell$, the affine competitor gives the same bound since~$\ahom_\ell\geq e^{-2\tau^2(\ell+1)}$. Join these competitors, which all equal~$p\cdot x$ on the boundaries of the Whitney cubes. Uniformly in~$\ell$,~$\pi$ and~$p$, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
	\E[p\cdot\a_\ell(\spx_\ell^\pi)p]
	\leq C\ahom_\ell|p|^2\sum_{i\geq0}3^{-i}e^{2\tau^2i}
	\leq C\ahom_\ell|p|^2\,.
\end{equation*}

\smallskip

Take~$R$ and~$q_R<1$ from~\eqref{e.strict.decay.qR.gap}. Keep the fields with indices at most~$\ell$ and with indices~$\ell+R,\ell+2R,\ldots$, and omit the others. The induction~\eqref{e.strict.decay.sparse.induction}, started at scale~$\ell$ from the bound for~$\spx_\ell^\pi$ above, and reinsertion of the omitted fields by~\eqref{e.strict.decay.full.sparse.comparison} give~$\ahom_m\leq C\ahom_\ell q_R^{\lfloor(m-\ell)/R\rfloor}$. This proves~\eqref{lim:eq-strict-decay} with~$\eta=-\log q_R/(R\log3)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Mean exit times]
\label{lim:lem-mean-exit}
There exists~$C<\infty$ such that, for every~$L,m\in\N_0$ and every~$z\in\Rd$, the cube~$U=z+\cu_m$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
	\E\Bigl[\sup_{x\in U}E_x^\omega\tau_U^{(L)}\Bigr]
	\leq C3^{2m}/\ahom_{m\wedge L}\,.
	\label{lim:eq-mean-exit}
\end{equation}
More generally, there is~$c>0$ such that the following holds. For every~$q<\infty$, there exists~$\gmcdelta_0>0$ such that, if~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there are random constants~$K\geq1$, one for each~$L,m\in\N_0$ and~$z\in\Rd$, with~$\E[K^q]$ bounded uniformly in~$L$,~$m$ and~$z$, such that the process obtained by rescaling~$z+\cu_m$ to~$\cu_0$ satisfies
\begin{equation*}
	\sup_{x\in\cu_0}E_x^\omega\tau_{\cu_0}\leq K
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
	\inf_{x\in\cu_{-2}}E_x^\omega\tau_{\cu_0}\geq cK^{-2}\,.
\end{equation*}
The same assertions hold for the diffusion~$X$ without cutoff, with~$\ahom_m$ in~\eqref{lim:eq-mean-exit}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We rescale~$z+\cu_m$ to~$\cu_0$ as in Section~\ref{s.tightness}, for either the diffusion~$X^{(L)}$ or the diffusion~$X$; here~$\mu$ has a positive, locally~$C^{1,1}$ density. The mass bound~\eqref{tight:eq-mass}, the trace inequality~\eqref{tight:eq-trace} and the coercivity bound~\eqref{tight:eq-coercivity} for functions with zero boundary values give, on every cube~$U$ that is fixed in the rescaled coordinates,
\begin{equation}
	\|v\|_{L^{\nf{2d}{(d-1)}}(U,\mu)}^2\leq K\mathcal E(v;U)\,,
	\qquad v\in H_0^1(U)\,.
	\label{lim:eq-killed-sobolev}
\end{equation}
Here~$K$ may depend on~$U$ and has the asserted uniform moment bounds.

\smallskip

Let~$u$ solve~$-\nabla\cdot(A\nabla u)=d\mu/dx$ in~$\cu_0$ with zero boundary values. Testing with~$(u-h)_+$ gives, for~$k>h\geq0$,
\begin{equation*}
	\mu(\{u>k\})\leq\left(\frac K{k-h}\right)^{\nf{2d}{(d-1)}}\mu(\{u>h\})^{\nf{(d+1)}{(d-1)}}\,.
\end{equation*}
Iterate this inequality along the thresholds equal to~$1-2^{-i}$ times a suitable multiple of~$K\mu(\cu_0)^{\nf1d}$ to obtain~$\|u\|_\infty\leq CK\mu(\cu_0)^{\nf1d}$, which by~\eqref{tight:eq-mass} is bounded by another random constant~$K$ with the asserted moments.

\smallskip

% Bass, Chapter VII, Section 6, Theorem 6.3 and Proposition 6.4 (symmetric operators);
% adapted: eigenfunction expansion of the killed semigroup on L^2(cu_0, mu).
We identify~$u$ with the mean exit time. The semigroup of the diffusion killed on leaving~$\cu_0$ is associated with the part of the Dirichlet form on~$\cu_0$~\citep[Theorems~4.4.2--4.4.3]{FOT}. Interpolate~\eqref{lim:eq-killed-sobolev} with~$L^1(\mu)$ to obtain a Nash inequality with respect to~$\mu$. On each fixed cube, it bounds the killed semigroup from~$L^1(\mu)$ to~$L^\infty(\mu)$ by a constant times~$t^{-d}$. We adapt the eigenfunction construction for symmetric operators in~\citet[Chapter~VII, Section~6, Theorem~6.3 and Proposition~6.4]{Bass} to~$L^2(\cu_0,\mu)$. Interior elliptic regularity of the eigenfunctions, and uniform convergence of the eigenfunction expansion on compact sets of positive times and interior points, give a kernel~$p_{\cu_0}(t,x,y)$ that is jointly continuous at positive times and interior points.

\smallskip

Since the drift of the rescaled stochastic differential equation is locally bounded, It\^o's formula applied to the squared distance from the starting point, stopped on exit from a small ball, bounds the probability of leaving that ball before time~$t$ by a quantity tending to zero as~$t\downarrow0$, uniformly over starting points in a compact subset of a fixed cube.

\smallskip

The local Nash bounds, this exit estimate and continuity of the semigroup on compactly supported continuous functions show, by approximation in~$L^1(\mu)$ on an enclosing cube, that the semigroup of the diffusion without killing maps bounded Borel functions to continuous functions at each positive time. The exit estimate also gives continuity in the starting point of the transition probabilities of the diffusion killed on leaving~$\cu_0$. For each fixed positive time and Borel subset of~$\cu_0$, the killed transition probability and the integral of~$p_{\cu_0}$ over that subset are continuous and agree~$\mu$-almost everywhere. They therefore agree at every interior starting point, since~$\mu$ has full support.

\smallskip

Coercivity gives a positive spectral gap. Together with the bound on the killed semigroup, this gives~$P_x^\omega(\tau_{\cu_0}>t)\leq Ce^{-ct}$ for~$t\geq1$, uniformly in~$x\in\cu_0$. Integrate the survival probability in time to obtain a function of the starting point that is continuous at interior points; as an element of~$L^2(\mu)$, it equals the inverse of the positive killed generator applied to~$1$. This function is the continuous representative of~$u$ at interior points. With this representative,
\begin{equation*}
	u(x)=\int_0^\infty\int_{\cu_0}p_{\cu_0}(t,x,y)\,\mu(dy)\,dt
	=\int_0^\infty P_x^\omega(\tau_{\cu_0}>t)\,dt
	=E_x^\omega\tau_{\cu_0},\qquad x\in\cu_0\,.
\end{equation*}
The bound on~$\|u\|_\infty$ obtained above therefore holds at every interior point for this representative. This proves the upper bound after rescaling, and restoring the time unit~\eqref{tight:eq-local-time} gives~\eqref{lim:eq-mean-exit}. The lower bound follows from~\eqref{tight:eq-fast-exit}: taking~$t=(2K)^{-2}$ gives~$P_x^\omega(\tau_{\cu_0}>t)\geq\nf12$ for every~$x\in\cu_{-2}$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Annealed and quenched path laws}

For a deterministic starting point~$x$, the annealed law~$\E[K_x^\omega]$ of the limiting process is the average over~$\omega$ of its quenched law~$K_x^\omega$.

\smallskip

Let~$\mathsf P$ be a subsequential limit of the annealed laws of the rescaled diffusions~$X^{(L)}$ or~$X$ in Proposition~\ref{tight:prop-tightness}, started at deterministic points that converge in rescaled coordinates; in particular,~$\mathsf P$ may be~$\E[K_x^\omega]$. Here~$\mathsf P$ is a probability measure on paths. In Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches},~$\mathsf P_{m,z}$ and its abbreviation~$\mathsf P$ denote the scalar discounted coefficient-ratio statistic.
